## st-0128 — The Fox and the Grapes
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One day, a man walks. He looks for food. His body feels very bad. He wants something good to eat. Then he sees a tall plant. A number of things stay high. The food looks very good. The man runs toward the plant. He wants to get those things. He moves up with all force. He cannot reach the food. His hand lets go of the plant. He falls back down to land. He sits on the floor. He looks up at the food again.

The man does not give up. He steps back and tries again. He moves as high as possible. The things remain too high up. He still cannot get any thing. The man stops moving and stands. He feels bad now. He uses so much force. He looks up one last time. He sees the result is not possible. His body really feels bad now. He stays right there for a minute.

The man turns away from the plant. He walks down a long way. He tells himself a little story. He says the things are bad. He thinks he never wants them. He knows the food is not good. He walks away into the day. He looks for other food now.

### A2
One fine day, a certain animal walked through a field. He looked for food because his body felt quite low. Suddenly, he saw a beautiful group of fine food. The food stayed high upon a green plant. The good food made him really want to eat. The animal ran toward the plant to get the food.

He tried to get up with all his power. However, he could not reach the food. His hand missed the plant completely. Then he fell back down to the ground. The animal did not give up yet. He stepped back and tried again and again.

He tried as high as possible. The food remained too high above him. After many hard tries, he still could not get them. Down and low, the animal stopped playing. He stood upon the ground. He felt completely done from his hard effort.

Looking up one last time, he realized success was out of reach. His foot and body felt bad. Quickly, the person turned away from the plant. He began walking down the road. He told himself a story to feel better. He said that the food was probably very bad.

He thought he never wanted them anyway. The day was long and hard. He will go home now. He will find other food to eat. Every animal has bad days like this. He will try again another time.

### B1
One fine day, a certain animal walked through a warm field looking for food. His inner body felt quite empty, and he really wanted something good to eat. Suddenly, he saw a beautiful collection of fine food hanging high on a green plant. The fruit looked very sweet and full of water, which made him even more ready. The animal ran toward the plant because he wanted to get those wonderful things. He jumped up with all his power, but he could not reach the fruit. If he had been tall, he might have touched them on the first try. Instead, his feet missed the branch completely, and he fell back down to the ground. Not giving up yet, the smart animal stepped back and tried again and again. He jumped as high as possible, reaching out his front feet with every single effort. However, the food remained too high above him, hanging safely out of his reach. After many hard tries, he still could not get even one single piece. Tired and angry, the animal stopped jumping and stood quietly on the ground. He was completely tired out because he had spent so much energy. He had spent that energy trying to win the award. Looking up one last time at the high plant, he realized that success was impossible.

His tired legs hurt, and his breath came fast from the difficult exercise. Slowly, the sad animal turned away from the plant and began walking down the road. To make himself feel better about failing, he told himself a little story. He spoke low that the food was probably very bad and not worth eating anyway. The animal acted like he never wanted them. He showed how people often hate what they cannot have. He had lived in the country for many years and knew how things worked. Every single morning, he went out to search for something nice. Today was no different from any other time in his long life. The sun shone brightly down upon the green fields and tall trees. Birds flew high across the blue sky while the wind blew softly. He walked past several houses and small shops on his way. Nobody was outside on the street during those quiet hours. The air felt warm and nice against his face as he moved forward. He always hoped that today would bring him a wonderful meal. Instead, he only found trouble and a high plant that troubled him.

He had already tried everything within his power to reach the top. His heart beat fast against his body from all the hard jumping. If he had longer legs, the story would have ended differently. He might have walked away with a full body and a happy mind. Now he only had empty feelings and sore feet to show for his work. The afternoon sun began to drop low behind the far hills. Dark shapes grew long across the land path where he stood alone. He took one last look at the hanging food high above him. The green leaves shook slightly in the calm evening wind. He turned his back on the plant for good and started his journey home. The long road stretched out before him in the light. He promised himself that he would find food somewhere else tomorrow. Life must go on even when things do not go the right way. He walked slowly into the night until he was completely gone from sight.

### B2
One fine day, a certain animal walked through a warm field looking for food. His stomach felt quite empty, and he really wanted something good to eat. Suddenly, he saw a beautiful bunch of sweet fruit hanging high on a green plant. The food looked very nice and full of water, which made him even more interested. The animal ran toward the plant because he wanted to get those fine things. He jumped up with all his power, but he could not reach the fruit. If he had been taller, he might have touched them on the first try. Instead, his hands missed the branch completely, and he fell back down to the ground. Not giving up yet, the smart creature stepped back and tried again and again. He jumped as high as possible, reaching out his front hands with every single effort. However, the sweet fruit remained too high above him, hanging safely out of his reach. After many hard tries, he still could not get even one single piece. Tired and sad, the animal stopped jumping and stood quietly on the ground. He was completely exhausted because he had spent so much energy trying to win the prize. Looking up one last time at the high plant, he realized that success was impossible. His tired legs hurt, and his breath came fast from the difficult exercise. Slowly, the sad animal turned away from the plant and began walking down the road. To make himself feel better about failing, he told himself a little story. He said that the sweet fruit was probably very bad and not worth eating anyway. The animal pretended he never wanted them, showing how people often look down on what they cannot have. Many hours passed while he walked across the large field in the warm sun. He thought about the wonderful plant and the sweet food hanging above his head.

It seemed very strange that such a fine prize could remain completely out of reach. Perhaps another day might bring better luck if he returned to the same place. However, the animal knew deep inside that success would require much more height. His body felt weak from all the hard jumping and the long walk. Therefore, he decided to continue his journey toward home without looking back. The afternoon sun slowly moved across the blue sky as evening began to approach. Small birds flew quietly between the trees while the wind blew gently through the grass. Everything around the tired traveler became quiet and still as night started to fall. He found a safe place to rest his tired body until the morning light returned. Even though he had failed, the experience taught him something about human nature and desire. People often pretend that things they cannot achieve are simply not worth having. This smart trick of the mind helps individuals deal with trouble and failure. Throughout history, stories like this one have been shared across many different cultures. Children and adults together learn valuable lessons from the actions of animals in tales. The animal in the story chose to protect his pride rather than accept his limitation. By convincing himself that the sweet fruit was bad, he felt better about his loss. This psychological defense mechanism allows everyone to maintain a positive self-image despite problems. The warm field remained empty as the stars slowly appeared in the night sky above. No other creatures came to disturb the quiet place where the high plant grew. The sweet fruit stayed safely on its branch through the long hours of darkness. Morning would soon arrive with a new day and fresh challenges for all living things. Until then, the world rested in deep silence under the bright light. ### C1
One fine afternoon, a solitary fox padded softly through a sun-drenched field, his amber eyes scanning the tall grass in search of sustenance. His belly felt hollow, a dull ache reminding him that hours had passed since his last meal, and his palate craved something rich and rewarding. As he rounded a cluster of wild shrubs, a sudden flash of vibrant green caught his attention against the pale expanse of the meadow. Dangling from a heavy, leafy vine were clusters of magnificent fruit, swollen with sweet juices and glowing like dark jewels in the slanted sunlight. The sight instantly quickened his pulse, flooding his senses with an intense desire to claim those luscious morsels for himself.

Impelled by sudden hunger, the fox sprinted across the dry earth toward the trellis, his paws kicking up small clouds of dust. He leaped upward with explosive power, extending his sharp black claws to catch the lowest hanging branch of the laden vine. Yet, despite his fierce momentum, his paws swept through empty air, missing the foliage entirely by a matter of agonizing inches. Gravity reclaimed him swiftly, dropping him back onto the hard soil with a sharp thud that rattled his bones. Had he possessed even a few more inches of height, his initial bound might have secured the prize on the very first attempt.

Refusing to yield to discouragement, the clever creature backed away, took a deep breath, and calculated a fresh approach. He launched himself into the air once more, his muscles bunching and stretching to their absolute limits as he fought upward. Every fiber of his agile body strained toward the target, his front paws batting frantically at the mocking green leaves. Nevertheless, the sweet fruit remained tantalizingly aloft, swaying gently out of reach as if indifferent to his desperate efforts. No matter how high he projected his frame, the distance proved insurmountable, keeping the feast securely guarded by empty air.

After countless exhausting leaps, the fox finally abandoned his assault, sliding back to the dirt with slumped shoulders and heavy breathing. He stood motionless on the parched ground, his chest heaving rapidly as he tried to drag cool oxygen into his burning lungs. The sheer physical exertion had drained his reserves, leaving his legs trembling and his joints aching from the repetitive impacts. Tilting his head upward one final time, he gazed at the unreachable clusters with a mixture of disbelief and profound fatigue. He understood with stark clarity that persistence alone could not alter the cruel geometry of his physical limitations.

Slowly, the weary animal turned his back upon the vine and began his long trudge down the dusty path leading away from the field. The warm afternoon sun beat down relentlessly upon his fur, amplifying the discomfort of his empty stomach and sore limbs. As he walked, a quiet frustration gnawed at his composure, making the bitter taste of failure even harder to swallow. To soothe the sharp sting of his wounded pride, his mind began to weave a comforting narrative to mask the reality of his defeat. He murmured softly to himself that the grapes were undoubtedly sour, ruined by rot and utterly unfit for any refined palate.

He convinced himself that consuming such unwholesome fruit would have brought him sickness rather than satisfaction. This mental fabrication allowed him to reframe his defeat, transforming an embarrassing inability into a wise and deliberate choice. By dismissing the object of his desire as worthless, he successfully shielded his ego from the painful admission of weakness. Hours drifted by as he continued his solitary journey across the sprawling landscape under the shifting afternoon light. The memory of the gleaming fruit lingered at the edge of his consciousness, a symbol of a goal that proved simply too high.

It struck him as peculiar that such a magnificent prize could exist merely to mock the ambitions of passing travelers. He briefly wondered if returning at dawn might yield better fortune, perhaps when his muscles had recovered their lost vigor. Yet deep inside, he knew that wishing for longer legs was entirely futile and that no amount of morning light would change reality. His weakened frame argued strongly against such foolish optimism, urging him to press onward toward the safety of his den. With a final, resigned sigh, he quickened his pace, determined to leave both the field and the frustrating memory behind.

The golden sun began its slow descent toward the western horizon, casting long, dramatic shadows across the quiet meadows. A gentle breeze rustled the treetops, carrying the cool scent of approaching evening through the darkening grass. Small birds drifted silently between the branches, seeking shelter as the vibrant colors of the day faded into twilight. The entire landscape grew still and hushed, wrapping the weary traveler in a profound and restorative blanket of silence. He discovered a secluded hollow beneath a sheltering thicket, perfectly suited for resting his aching joints until morning.

Curling his tail tightly around his nose, the fox settled into the dry leaves and closed his heavy eyelids against the gathering dark. Though his stomach remained empty, his mind felt strangely lighter, comforted by the clever fiction he had constructed about the grapes. This subtle defense mechanism of the intellect demonstrated how easily living creatures protect their pride from the sting of disappointment. People and animals alike often disparage what they cannot attain, shielding their self-image from the harsh friction of failure. As the first pale stars pierced the velvet expanse of the night sky, the solitary creature drifted into a deep and dreamless sleep.

The warm field remained entirely deserted, bathed in the soft, silvery illumination of a rising moon that touched every leaf and blade. No other creatures wandered past the quiet spot where the heavy vine still clung to its sturdy wooden support. The sweet fruit remained untouched on the branch, enduring the long hours of darkness without a single disturbance. Morning would soon arrive with a fresh dawn, bringing new trials and opportunities for every living soul across the land. Until that awakening, the world rested in absolute peace, wrapped in the quiet wisdom of an ancient and timeless lesson.

## st-0130 — The North Wind and the Sun
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
The North Wind and the Sun talk. They talk about power. Each one think he is strong. They want a test. They see a man on a street. The man put a large piece of cloth on.

They agree on a test. The man must take the piece of cloth off. The North Wind go first. He moves with force and power. The man hold the piece of cloth close. The North Wind try everything. The man do not take it off. The North Wind stop.

Now the Sun come out. The Sun light the day well. The man feel good. The Sun light more. The man feel very small and not good. He takes off the piece of cloth.

The Sun win the test. Small light is strong. The Sun win the game. The Sun is very big. The North Wind is not good. They stop the test now. The man walk away home. He feels fine and well. The day is very good.

### A2
The North Wind and the Sun had a very long argument. They wanted to know who had more power. They decided to test their big power today. They saw a man walk down a road. The man wore a large dress because it was very cold. Both agreed on a test. The winner would make the traveler take off his dress. The North Wind went first in the morning. He began to push very hard. The man only pulled his dress close around himself. The North Wind tried everything, but he could not force him. The bad wind finally stopped after some time.

Then the Sun came out from behind white things. He began to light the day. The man felt the good fire and smiled. The Sun grew hotter and hotter. The man could not bear the fire. He took his dress off by himself. The Sun won the test easily. He proved that a quiet hand can do more than force. The North Wind learned a study about being good. The end of the story came with quiet. Everyone was happy now. They were happy the test was over.

The test was long and hard. The North Wind blew with all his power. The man held his dress tight. He did not want to lose his clothes. The Sun took another way. He gave warmth to the world. The traveler felt the change immediately. He opened his heart to the heat. Soon the dress became too hot for him. The man took it off right away. The Sun won because of smart power. The story shows that kindness wins over force every time.

### B1
The North Wind and the Sun had a long argument. They argued about which of them was stronger. Each character believed that his own power was greater. After talking for some time, they decided to have a test. They saw a traveler walking down a road. The traveler wore a heavy coat because the weather was cold. If the North Wind had tried, he would have blown hard. The North Wind and the Sun have talked about this for hours. The two friends agreed on a simple test. The winner would be the one who made the traveler take off his coat.

The North Wind said that he would go first. He began to blow as hard and as cold as he could. However, the harder the wind blew, the closer the man held his coat. The poor traveler felt very cold. He pulled his clothes closer to his body. The North Wind tried everything, but he could not force the man. Finally, the angry wind stopped blowing. He realized that his plan had failed completely. If the wind had succeeded, he would have won the test.

It was now time for the Sun to show his power. The Sun came out from behind the air. He began to send heat warmly upon the earth. At first, the traveler felt the nice heat. He smiled at the lovely change in the weather. As the Sun sent heat brighter, the man began to feel very warm. Soon, he could not bear the heat at all. If the Sun had not shined, the man would have kept his coat. The Sun has shown his power successfully today.

He took his coat off himself. The Sun won the argument easily. He proved that soft heat can do what force cannot. If the Sun had not tried, he would not have won. They have completed the test successfully today. The North Wind and the Sun have learned a big lesson about power.

### B2
The North Wind and the Sun were having a long argument about which of them was stronger. Each character believed that his own power was greater than the other power in the world. After talking for some time, they decided to have a test to find the true winner. They saw a traveler walking down a road wearing a heavy coat because the weather was cold. The two friends agreed on one simple rule. If any person among them could make the traveler take off his coat, that one would be the winner. The North Wind said that he would go first and show his great strength. He began to blow as hard and as cold as he could. However, the harder the wind blew, the tighter the man wrapped his coat around himself. The poor traveler felt very cold, so he pulled his clothes closer to his body. The North Wind tried everything, but he could not force the man to remove the coat. Finally, the angry wind stopped blowing and realized that his plan had failed completely.

It was now time for the Sun to show what he could do. The Sun came out from behind the clouds and began to shine upon the earth. At first, the traveler felt the pleasant heat and smiled at the lovely change in the weather. As the Sun shone brighter and hotter, the man began to feel very hot. Soon, he could not bear the heat anymore, so he took his coat off himself. The Sun won the argument easily and proved that gentle care can do what force cannot. The North Wind learned that being aggressive is often less effective than being nice. If the wind had known the truth, he would not have tried such a poor plan. The traveler was happy that the weather changed. He walked away with a smile on his face. The two powers watched him go down the road. They understood that gentle actions achieve more than violent force. Every day is a new lesson for those who live in this world.

Let us think about why this story is important. Many people try to use force when they want to change something. However, pressure usually makes people hold onto their positions more tightly. If you want someone to agree with you, you should be nice and friendly. Good connections open doors that force can never open. The Sun knew this secret well. He did not shout or blow hard. He just shone bright and let his natural heat do the work. The man on the road felt the change and made his own choice. Nobody told him what to do. He decided to remove his coat because he wanted to feel cool. This is a great lesson for everyone. We should always remember that being nice is better than being strong.

Long ago, the world was full of such arguments between different powers. People talked about how to solve problems without fighting. Stories like this one were passed down from parent to child. They taught children how to live together in harmony. Even today, we can use these lessons in our daily lives. When we meet people who have different ideas, we should listen to them. If we are nice, we will win their hearts. The North Wind and the Sun will always be remembered for this famous test. It is a story that people will tell for many years to come. We must keep learning from the past so that we can build a better future. That is the end of our tale about the wind and the sun.

Many years passed after that famous day on the road. Parents told the story to every new generation of children. They wanted young people to understand the value of calm actions. When problems appeared between groups, leaders thought about the Sun and the wind. They knew that shouting did not bring good results. Instead, calm talk and friendly support helped people find common ground. If a government wanted to change a law, leaders used careful discussion. They did not force citizens to accept rules without hearing their voices. This approach led to lasting peace across the country. Every citizen felt respected when leaders listened to their concerns. Thus, the old lesson remained true in every modern society. People continued to share the tale during family meals and community events. Teachers used the story in schools to guide young minds. Students learned that power alone is not enough to solve difficult issues. True leadership requires care, understanding, and a gentle touch. Whenever you face a hard situation, remember the Sun. Let your actions be warm and welcoming to everyone around you. You will see how much more you can achieve through friendship. The world becomes a better place when we care for one another.

As time went on, society changed in many ways. New technologies emerged, and cities grew larger. People traveled across oceans and built international networks. Yet, the core message of the story remained completely valid. Human nature stayed the same despite all external changes. Individuals still faced challenges that required a gentle response. Whenever arguments happened, leaders looked back at ancient wisdom. They knew that force only created resistance. True cooperation always required mutual trust and respect. Thus, the tale of the wind and the sun survived through the ages. It lived in books, songs, and classroom lessons. Children grew up hearing about the traveler and his heavy coat. They understood why the Sun succeeded where the wind failed. This knowledge shaped their behavior as they entered adulthood. Communities prospered when leaders chose dialogue over conflict. Families thrived when parents practiced kindness at home. Every single person held the power to influence others positively. By acting with a warm heart, anyone could change the world. Let us carry this wonderful message with us every single day. May we always choose kindness over strength in our daily lives. The story ends here, but its lessons will last forever.

### C1
Between the restless North Wind and the radiant Sun, a protracted debate had smoldered for weeks. Each celestial power maintained with absolute conviction that his own dominion over the elements surpassed the other. Neither was willing to yield, insisting that true strength manifested in entirely different forms. Seeking a definitive resolution, they decided to put their competing philosophies to the test. Their eyes soon fell upon a solitary traveler traversing a winding country road below. The man was wrapped snugly against the chill of the morning air. A heavy wool coat protected him from the biting wind that swept across the open fields. Observing this, the two immortals established a single, unambiguous rule for their wager. Whichever of them could compel the traveler to strip off his heavy garment would be crowned the undisputed victor. The North Wind stepped forward eagerly, confident that his brute force would settle the matter swiftly.

Drawing in a vast lungful of frosty air, the North Wind unleashed a devastating gale. He hurled freezing gusts across the landscape, intending to rip the protective wool straight from the traveler s shoulders. Whistling savagely through the bare branches, the tempest struck the man with sudden fury. Yet, far from surrendering his coat, the traveler hunched his shoulders and pulled the thick fabric tighter around his chest. Astonished by this stubborn resistance, the wind redoubled his efforts with mounting frustration. He drove sleet and freezing rain against the traveler, howling with immense and terrible force. The miserable wanderer shivered violently, pressing his arms against his sides to trap his body heat. Every fierce gust only convinced the man that letting go of his coat would mean certain freezing. Realizing at last that his aggression was utterly futile, the exhausted wind fell silent.

It was now the turn of the Sun to demonstrate the efficacy of his quiet approach. Emerging gradually from behind a heavy veil of grey clouds, he cast a golden and benign light upon the earth. At first, the traveler merely registered a welcome cessation of the freezing gale and offered a grateful smile. The air grew progressively milder, transforming the bleak morning into an unexpectedly pleasant afternoon. As the Sun continued to beam down with steady, unhurried warmth, the atmosphere shifted dramatically. The traveler began to perspire under the weight of the heavy wool that had previously been his salvation. He unbuttoned the top fasteners, letting the gentle breeze cool his heated skin. Soon, the heat became too intense to bear, prompting the man to slide his arms entirely out of the sleeves.

Wiping the sweat from his forehead with a handkerchief, the traveler heaved a sigh of profound relief. He folded the heavy coat neatly and laid it across his arm as he resumed his journey. The Sun had achieved victory without unleashing a single violent storm or uttering a harsh command. Observing this quiet triumph, the North Wind conceded that gentle persuasion had accomplished what sheer force never could. The traveler walked onward down the winding road, entirely unaware that he had decided the outcome of a celestial dispute. He felt only a deep sense of contentment as the warm sunlight bathed his shoulders. The two powers watched him disappear into the hazy distance, fully cognizant of the profound lesson they had just witnessed. Actions rooted in patience and warmth invariably achieve far more than those driven by anger.

In the centuries that followed, this legendary contest became a foundational parable for societies striving to live in harmony. Ordinary people and community leaders alike reflected upon the wisdom demonstrated by the Sun. Whenever disputes arose between neighboring towns or factions, wise elders reminded the restless youths of the frozen road. They argued persuasively that shouting and threats only hardened opposition, whereas calm dialogue melted resistance away. Governments learned that lasting laws could never be forced upon reluctant citizens through intimidation alone. Instead, listening carefully to the grievances of the populace created a stable and enduring peace. Citizens who felt respected by their leaders were far more likely to cooperate willingly. Thus, the ancient lesson of the wind and the sun permeated every aspect of daily civic life.

Parents recited the tale to their children during evening meals, hoping to instill a deep appreciation for diplomacy. Teachers integrated the story into school curricula to guide young minds away from aggressive confrontation. Students learned early that genuine authority derived from empathy rather than dominance. Whenever a difficult personal conflict arose on the playground, children were encouraged to channel the gentle patience of the Sun rather than the blustery temper of the wind. This transmission of wisdom across generations ensured that the core message remained vital and universally understood. Human nature rarely changes, and individuals frequently face situations where pressure fails to produce the desired result. Recognizing this limitation, people continually turned back to the story of the traveler and his coat. They understood that mutual trust and respect formed the bedrock of any thriving community.

As societies evolved and civilizations expanded across oceans, the fundamental truths of the parable retained their relevance. Modern institutions adopted the philosophy of gentle cooperation in handling complex international negotiations. Diplomats realized that heavy-handed tactics typically provoked stubborn defiance among rival nations. By opting for patient listening and supportive dialogue, leaders achieved breakthroughs that once seemed utterly impossible. Even within the quiet confines of family households, the lesson continued to shape interpersonal relationships. Parents who practiced kindness rather than harsh discipline discovered that their children responded with greater honesty and warmth. Every individual retained the capacity to influence others simply by choosing a welcoming demeanor over a hostile posture. The world gradually transformed into a kinder place as more people embraced this ancient wisdom.

Reflecting upon the famous test on the road, one realizes that true strength is quiet and self-assured. It needs no roaring storms or dramatic displays of dominance to prove its worth. The Sun simply shone according to its nature, allowing the natural environment to shift organically. Similarly, authentic leadership encourages others to arrive at the right conclusions independently, without coercion. The traveler had made his own voluntary decision to shed the heavy coat because the conditions invited him to do so. Nobody had screamed orders at him or tried to rip the garment from his body by force. This subtle distinction formed the very heart of the victory achieved by the golden orb. It was a testament to the fact that internal motivation always outperforms external pressure.

Generations would pass, and new technologies would alter the landscape of human existence beyond recognition. Cities of glass and steel would rise where ancient forests once stood, yet the essence of human interaction would remain unaltered. People would still grapple with stubborn pride and the temptation to force their will upon others. Whenever such moments of temptation arose, the memory of the wind and the sun would serve as a timely anchor. It was a story designed to outlast empires, passed down through spoken word and ink upon parchment. Children would always grow up hearing about the man on the road and his frustratingly resilient coat. They would smile knowingly as they grasped the underlying truth long before reaching adulthood. This shared cultural heritage bound communities together through a common understanding of human psychology.

Let us carry this enduring insight forward into our own daily interactions and endeavors. When faced with stubborn opposition or difficult personalities, we must resist the urge to meet resistance with immediate hostility. Instead, let our approach be characterized by warmth, patience, and genuine understanding. We will inevitably discover that friendship opens doors which locked barriers and harsh demands could never breach. The fable of the North Wind and the Sun remains a timeless reminder of how we ought to treat one another. Though the original traveler completed his journey long ago, the road he walked stretches onward through time. Every passerby who encounters a chilly day faces the same subtle choice between force and gentle persuasion. May we always possess the wisdom to follow the shining example of the sun.

## st-0131 — The Goose That Laid the Golden Eggs
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A man lives in a house. His life is very hard. He has little money for food. Then a special friend comes to him. He is glad to have a friend. The friend is a special bird.

Every morning the man goes to a place. He finds a special thing. He takes it to the town market. He sells it for much money. He gets rich fast. Days pass and the man grows big. The friend makes one thing per day. He buys good food and a home. Yet the man becomes very much into more. He wants all the money at once. He thinks the friend is full of money. He wants to cut the friend open. He believes he finds a big thing.

The man takes a big tool. He cuts the friend open. He finds just a normal friend. The friend is gone now. The man sits down in a room. He cries over his bad path. His action costs him everything. He is as little as before. He has no money now. He loses his good home.

### A2
Once upon a time, a poor man lived near a town. His life was hard. He had little money for food. However, a special animal came to his house. He did not know what to expect. The animal was a white white animal. It looked like a normal house animal. The man hoped for a change in his life. He watched the animal every single day. He wanted a better future for himself.

Every morning, the man walked to the little place. He found a special item there. It was a bright product. He took the item to the market. He sold it for money. He bought good food with the money. He also bought new clothes. He moved to a better home. His days without food were finally gone. Life felt very good for the person.

As days passed, the man grew well with money. The animal laid one bright item each day. Yet, the man became very low inside. He wanted all the money at once. He thought the animal was full of money. He believed he would find a huge amount. He thought about it all the time. He could not wait for the next morning. His mind was full of dark thoughts. He wanted everything right now.

He believed he would find a large place. He thought he would see a massive stock. He wanted to cut the animal open. Without thinking, the man took a tool. He cut the animal open with his hand. It was just an animal inside. There was nothing special within the body. Now the animal was gone. There were no more bright items left. His poor choice brought a bad result.

The man sat down and was not happy. His bad choice cost him everything. He was poor again. He learned a hard answer about life. His fast way ruined a great thing. He lost the source of his money. He had no more bright items. He stayed alone in his empty room. He thought about his bad choice. He wished he could change the past.

Many days passed after that event. The man worked hard every single week. He had no more special help. He ate simple food again. He wore his old clothes. He remembered the animal with deep care. He knew his own act caused the loss. Patient ways are very important values. People should always care for what they have. Bad acts often break good support.

### B1
Once upon a time, a poor farmer lived in a small house near a quiet village. His life was very hard. He had little money to buy food. However, everything changed when a special bird arrived at his farm. Every single morning, the man walked to the place. To his great surprise, he found a fine egg waiting there. He took the important object to the town market. He sold it for money. As days passed, the farmer slowly grew rich because the bird laid one egg each day. He was able to buy good food, new clothes, and a better home. Yet, despite having so much money, the man became very angry and fast. He wanted to have all the money at once instead of waiting. Thinking that the bird must be full of money, a strange idea came into his mind. If he cut the animal open, he would find a huge amount of money. Without thinking, the man took a sharp tool and cut the animal open. To his bad surprise, he found that it was just an ordinary bird. Now the bird was gone, and there were no more eggs left.

The sad farmer sat down in deep pain and cried over his mistake. His own bad mind had cost him everything. He was left as poor as before. From that day forward, he learned that people must not act so fast. He lost everything because he wanted more and more every single day. If he had been happy, he would have lived a long and good life. Instead, he destroyed his own future with his hands. Now he has nothing left in his empty home. He sits alone every night and thinks about his big mistake. He remembers the wonderful eggs that he sold at the market. He wishes that he had not cut the special bird open. If he had waited, he would have received more money. Now his life is hard again, and he has no money for food. He looks around the empty room and feels deep pain. The poor man understands that wanting too much brings only trouble and pain. He promises to be patient if he ever receives another chance.

However, time does not turn back for anyone. He must live with the result of his poor action. The sun sets behind the hills, and dark night covers the small house. He closes his eyes and tries to sleep through the cold hours. Every morning brings a new day, but no shiny egg waits for him now. He works in the fields from morning until evening to earn a little food. His friends feel sorry for him, but they cannot fix his problem. He has learned a hard lesson about the danger of wanting too much. Life continues to move forward, and the farmer remains poor until the end of his days. He has worked hard for many years, but the past cannot change. He has lost his best friend, and he has found only regret. If he had taken care of the animal, he would have lived a rich life. Instead, his own action has brought him back to the beginning. He looks at the empty place where the animal used to stay. He hopes that someone might help him, but no one comes. The quiet village stays the same while the poor man sits and thinks about his lost future.

### B2
Once upon a time, a poor farmer lived in a small house near a quiet village. His life was very hard, and he had little money to buy food for himself. However, everything changed when a special bird arrived at his farm. Every single morning, the farmer walked to the place where the animal stayed. To his great surprise, he found a bright golden egg waiting there for him. He took the valuable object to the town market and sold it for a lot of money. As days passed, the farmer slowly grew rich because the bird laid one golden egg each day. He was able to buy good food, new clothes, and a better home for his life. Yet, despite having so much wealth, the man became very rushed and hurried. He wanted to have all the gold at once instead of waiting for one egg per day. Thinking that the bird must be full of gold inside, a strange and silly idea came into his mind. If he cut the animal open, he believed that he would find a huge collection of gold. Without thinking about the consequences, the man took a sharp knife and cut the creature open. To his absolute shock, he found that it was just an ordinary bird with nothing special inside. Now the animal was gone, and there were no more golden eggs left for him. The silly farmer sat down in deep sadness and cried over his terrible mistake. His own desire had cost him everything, leaving him as poor as he was before. From that day forward, he learned that being hurried and wanting too much destroy wonderful gifts.

The farmer had lived in that small house for many years. His daily routine was simple before the bird arrived. He worked hard every day just to get enough food. When the first golden egg appeared, his entire world changed. He could not believe his eyes when he saw the bright object. Every morning brought a new chance to improve his poor life. The local market was full of people who wanted to buy things from him. He received a large amount of money for each sale. Soon, he bought nice clothes and good food for his daily meals. His neighbors watched him become rich in a short period of time. They wondered how a poor man could suddenly afford such a nice house. The farmer kept the secret of the bird to himself. He did not want anyone else to know about the golden eggs. However, money often changes how a person thinks and acts. The farmer grew more and more rushed with every passing week. He felt that one egg per day was simply not enough for his growing needs. His mind was filled with thoughts of incredible wealth. He imagined that the inside of the bird must contain a mountain of gold. If he waited any longer, he feared that he might lose his chance to be very rich. That silly thought made him take the sharp knife from his kitchen table. He did not listen to any voice of reason in his head. His hands were shaking as he approached the animal in the small space. He made the terrible cut without a single second of delay. Blood and hair were everywhere, but there was no gold to be seen. Only an ordinary creature lay dead on the floor of the room. The terrible truth hit him like a heavy blow from above. He realized that his own actions had destroyed his only source of wealth. No more eggs would ever be found in that place. His brilliant future was gone in less than a minute. He sat on the ground and cried for a very long time. The sadness stayed with him until the end of his days. People in the village talked about his big mistake for generations. Everyone agreed that being too rushed always leads to complete ruin. The farmer remained poor for the rest of his life. He never forgot the terrible lesson that he had learned.

Stories like this are told to children all across the country. They teach important lessons about life and human behavior. When people want too much, they often lose what they already have. Time is a quality that helps individuals make better decisions. If the farmer had been quiet, he would have lived a comfortable life. Instead, his mind was clouded by a strong desire for rapid wealth. We should always appreciate the good things that come to us naturally. Trying to force success usually results in total failure. The poor farmer learned this truth the hardest way possible. His story remains a clear warning for anyone who feels too rushed. May we all remember to value what we have today. Tomorrow is never promised, so we must be careful with our choices.

### C1
For many long years, a humble farmer inhabited a modest dwelling on the quiet outskirts of a forgotten village. His existence was defined by relentless labor, as he toiled from dawn until dusk merely to secure his daily bread. Poverty was a heavy cloak that he wore through every changing season, leaving him little hope for a better future. Yet fate is often capricious, and the entire trajectory of his life shifted the moment an extraordinary avian creature arrived at his farm. He had never witnessed such brilliant plumage, nor had he ever imagined that a simple animal could alter his lonely world so completely.

Every single morning without fail, the farmer walked toward the shadowed corner of the yard where the creature rested. To his absolute astonishment, a gleaming orb of pure gold lay nestled upon the straw, radiating a warm and impossible light. He would pick up the heavy, precious object with trembling fingers, marveling at the sheer fortune that had literally been deposited at his feet. Before this miraculous arrival, his daily routine had been remarkably simple and utterly devoid of any financial relief. Now, however, he possessed a treasure that defied explanation and promised to lift him far above his previous struggles.

Carrying the radiant prize concealed beneath a worn woolen coat, he made his way to the bustling town market whenever the sun climbed high. The local merchants were immediately captivated by the exceptional purity of the metal he offered for exchange. He received a remarkably large sum of money for each individual sale, transforming his financial standing almost overnight. Within a remarkably short period, he was able to acquire fine garments that replaced his threadbare rags and purchase provisions that far exceeded his basic needs. His neighbors watched his sudden ascent with growing fascination, wondering how a man who had been destitute for decades could suddenly afford such comfortable living conditions.

Wisely choosing to keep the origin of his newfound wealth entirely to himself, the farmer offered vague explanations for his sudden prosperity. He feared that any intrusion from the outside world might jeopardize the singular source of his fortune. As weeks melted into months, the steady accumulation of gold began to work a subtle and corrosive change within his mind. Money has a peculiar way of altering human perception, and the farmer soon found himself consumed by an obsessive urgency. The steady rhythm of receiving one magnificent egg each morning no longer satisfied the growing hunger that echoed in his chest.

He grew increasingly impatient with the passage of time, feeling that the natural pace of his enrichment was far too slow. His thoughts became entirely dominated by the vast fortune that he imagined must be resting just beneath the surface of the bird's quiet exterior. It seemed entirely illogical to him that such a miraculous creature should only yield a single treasure per day when a vast reservoir of wealth must surely be hidden inside. If he waited any longer, he convinced himself, some unforeseen misfortune might rob him of the opportunity to claim his entire inheritance at once. His mind was completely clouded by a frantic desire for immediate and absolute abundance.

One fateful morning, as the pale sunlight filtered through the cracked window of his small kitchen, the temptation reached an intolerable peak. Without pausing to entertain a single sensible doubt, his trembling hand reached for the sharpest steel blade resting upon the wooden table. He ignored the quiet voice of reason that whispered from the depths of his conscience, choosing instead to listen only to the demands of his relentless greed. His footsteps were heavy and hurried as he crossed the small space that separated him from the unsuspecting creature. Not a single second of hesitation delayed the devastating movement of his hand.

The sharp metal cut swiftly through the air, bringing an abrupt and tragic end to the quiet life that had sustained his own. As the terrible deed was done, the farmer stared in mounting horror at the harsh reality revealed before his wide eyes. There was no towering mountain of gold hidden within, nor any secret chamber filled with glittering treasures. Instead, he found only an ordinary bird, identical to any other creature that roamed the fields beyond his fence. The heavy realization of his own destructive folly struck him with the force of a falling stone.

A profound and suffocating silence descended upon the small room as the full magnitude of his error became unmistakably clear. His brilliant future, built upon patience and daily grace, had vanished entirely in less than a single minute of reckless haste. No more golden eggs would ever grace his property, and the generous gift he had so foolishly abused was gone forever. He sank slowly to the dusty wooden floor, burying his face in his hands as bitter tears of regret began to fall. The sadness that washed over him was deep and permanent, promising to accompany him through every remaining day of his life.

Word of the tragic event spread quickly through the village, becoming a cautionary tale told and retold across generations. The neighbors who had once envied his sudden wealth now shook their heads in sorrow at the extent of his self-inflicted ruin. Everyone in the community agreed that an uncontrolled desire for rapid gain invariably leads to total destruction. The farmer remained as poor for the rest of his days as he had been before the strange bird ever crossed his threshold. He never forgot the harsh lesson he had taught himself through an act of supreme impatience.

Tales of this nature are frequently shared with younger listeners across the country to impart vital truths about human behavior. When individuals allow themselves to be consumed by the desire for more than they truly need, they frequently forfeit the blessings they already possess. Time is a gentle quality that allows thoughtful people to make balanced and beneficial decisions. Had the farmer chosen to remain content with his daily fortune, he would have lived a remarkably comfortable and peaceful life. Instead, his vision was entirely obscured by the blinding mist of sudden greed.

We should always cultivate a deep appreciation for the natural gifts that come to us without the need for force. Attempting to rush success or demand immediate rewards usually results in complete and irreversible failure. The impoverished farmer absorbed this painful reality in the most direct and unforgiving manner possible. His unfortunate experience serves as a clear warning for anyone who feels pressured by the frantic pace of modern ambition. May we all remember to cherish what we hold today, knowing that tomorrow depends entirely upon the wisdom of our choices.

## st-0132 — The Town Mouse and the Country Mouse
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A town little animal goes to visit country friend. The country little animal shares small food. The town little animal does not like food. He says country life is not good. He tells family member to come town. He wants him see big city. They walk together for long time. They get to town. They walk and walk. They see many things. The town is very big.

The town little animal shows large house. Good food is on big table. They sit and eat big food. They eat and they feel good. They think food is very good. The town little animal is very happy. The country little animal looks at food. They eat more and more food every minute. They eat food all day. They have a lot of food.

A big wild animal comes on table. The small little animals run away. They run away very quick. They stay behind big place. People come into room to talk. The bad small little animals run and stay. They run again to stay away. They are very scared in room. They hide from big wild animal.

The country little animal looks at family member. He wants small food in peace. He wants peace all day. He does not want big city. He leaves city and goes home. He goes back to country house. He is happy be home. He never wants go town again. He stays in country forever.

### A2
Once upon a time, a town person went to visit his friend. The friend lived in the country. The country friend welcomed him. He shared his simple food of basic things. However, the town person found the food very boring. He looked at the food with a little smile. He said that life in the country was too hard. You should come with me to the city. The town person said this to his friend. I will show you a big house. Good food is eaten every single day there. The country friend was very interested. So he agreed to make the trip. They traveled together until they reached a huge house. This house was in the city.

Inside the big house, food was prepared on a large table. There was fine food for everyone. The country friend had never seen such food before. The two friends sat down to eat. They began to eat the good food with great joy. Suddenly, a big animal moved onto the table. The animal let out a high sound. The poor friends ran away as fast as they could. They ran to the back of the room. They stayed behind a dark wall. After the animal left, they went back out to the table. However, another problem soon came up.

People came into the room to talk and laugh. This forced the poor friends to run and stay in fear again. The country friend moved with fear. He looked at his friend with wide eyes. He realized that bad risk made the good food totally bad. I would rather eat simple food in rest. The country friend said this before leaving. He said farewell to his friend. He left the city behind. He returned home to the low country.

Days passed by after that big trip. The country friend stayed in his home. He ate his basic food every single day. He felt happy and sure in his small place. He did not want any more big trips to the city. He knew that rest was better than risk. His life was calm and good. The town person stayed in his big city house. He lived his fast life every single day. Both friends stayed where they felt best. They remembered the past day they spent together. That day changed their minds about many things in life. And both friends lived happily after that time.

### B1
Once upon a time, a town mouse went to visit his relative. The relative lived in the country. The country mouse welcomed him warmly. He shared simple food like roots and seeds. However, the town mouse found the meal very boring and dry. He looked at the food and said country life was too slow. You should come with me to the town, said the town mouse. I will show you a big house with rich food every day. The country mouse was interested, so he agreed to make the trip. They have traveled together many times. If they walk fast, they will reach the city soon. The country mouse has never seen such a place before. He has always lived in the quiet country. The town mouse has promised him a wonderful experience.

They traveled together until they reached a huge house in the city. Inside, a wonderful meal had been prepared on a large table. There was sweet fruit, fine food, and meat. The two relatives sat down and began to eat the rich food with great delight. They have eaten much food already. If the food is good, they will eat more. The town mouse has shared his favorite dishes. The country mouse has tasted many new things today. They have enjoyed the meal very much. Every piece of food looks wonderful to them. The table has been covered with special items. They have spent a long time at the table.

Suddenly, a big cat jumped onto the table and made a big sound. The worried mice ran away as fast as they could. They hid behind a wall. After the cat left, they moved back out to the table. Soon, people came into the room to talk and laugh. This forced the poor mice to run and hide in fear again. The cat has made the poor mice feel worried. If the cat returns, they will run away again. The mice have hidden behind the wall many times. They have experienced too much danger now. The people have made a lot of noise in the room. The mice have realized that danger is everywhere here. They have decided that this place is not safe.

The country mouse shook with fear and looked at his relative. He realized that constant danger made the rich food completely bad. I would rather eat simple food in peace than live in fear, he said. He said a final word to his relative, left the city, and returned home. He went back to the quiet country. The country mouse has made his final choice. If he stays here, he will lose his peace. He has learned an important lesson today. The town mouse has understood his point of view. They have said a final word to each other. The country mouse has returned to his home. He has found his quiet life again. He has never wanted to visit the city since that day.

### B2
Once upon a time, a town mouse went to visit his cousin who lived in the country. The country mouse welcomed him warmly and shared his simple food of plain roots and seeds. However, the town mouse found the meal very boring and dry. He looked at the plain food with a little smile and said that life in the country was too slow. You should come with me to the town, said the town mouse to his cousin. I will show you a grand house where rich food is eaten every single day. The country mouse was very curious, so he agreed to make the trip. They traveled together until they reached a huge, beautiful house in the city. Inside the grand house, a wonderful meal had been prepared on a large table. There was sweet fruit, fine cheese, and meat which the country mouse had never seen before. The two cousins sat down and began to eat the rich food with great joy.

Suddenly, a big cat jumped onto the table and let out a loud sound. The frightened mice ran away as fast as they could and hid behind a dark wall. After the cat left, they walked back out to the table, but another problem soon appeared. People burst into the room to talk and laugh, forcing the poor mice to run and hide in fear again. The country mouse shook with fear and looked at his cousin with wide eyes. He realized that constant danger made the rich food completely bad for him. I would rather eat simple food in quiet than live in fear every day, said the country mouse. He told his cousin farewell, left the city behind, and returned home to the quiet country. The mouse family lived in the quiet country for many years. They had many days when the sun shone brightly over the green grass. Each morning, the country mouse walked through the green fields to find fresh roots. His cousin from the town often wrote letters to ask about his quiet life. The town mouse understood that safety was much better than danger.

Life in the city was full of rich food, but it was also full of constant fear. The two cousins remained good friends even though they lived in very different places. They often remembered the day when the big cat jumped onto the table. Whenever the country mouse thought about that trip, he felt happy to be home. He knew that true happiness came from peace and quiet. The simple food of the country was always enough for him. He never wanted to see another big cat in his life. His little home under the ground was safe and warm. Every evening, he sat with his family and shared a quiet meal. The story of their great adventure was told to the younger mice many times. They learned that simple things are often the best things in the world.

Life in the country continued for many seasons after that great adventure. The country mouse spent his days walking under the bright sun and finding fresh food. His home was small, but it was safe from any big cat. Every night, he slept without any fear in his heart. His cousin in the city often thought about the simple meals they used to share. The town mouse wrote another letter to ask if he could visit the country again. The country mouse was happy to receive the letter and replied quickly. He told his cousin that he was always welcome to come back. When the town mouse arrived, he brought small gifts from the city. They sat together outside their small home and ate simple food. The town mouse looked around at the green grass and the tall trees. He agreed that this quiet place was much better than the dangerous city.

They talked about old times and laughed about the big cat. The sun went down slowly behind the hills, turning the sky into a bright red color. Both cousins felt very calm as the evening air grew cool. The town mouse stayed for several weeks before he returned to his own home. He learned a valuable lesson about the true meaning of a good life. Safety and quiet are worth much more than expensive food and constant danger. The story of the two cousins was passed down through the family for generations. Young mice would listen closely when their parents told the tale. They understood that a simple life is often the happiest life of all. Nobody in the family ever wanted to leave the safe country home. They lived happily for the rest of their days, far away from any city danger.

### C1
Once upon a time, a town mouse undertook a long journey to visit his cousin who lived in the quiet country. The country mouse welcomed him warmly into his modest burrow and shared his simple food of plain roots and seeds. However, the town mouse found the meal profoundly boring and dry, chewing each bite with a slight look of pity. He looked at the plain food with a little smile and said that life in the country was far too slow for anyone with ambition. You should come with me to the town, said the town mouse to his cousin, offering a grand invitation. I will show you a magnificent house where rich food is eaten every single day without fail. Driven by curiosity, the country mouse agreed to make the trip.

They traveled together across fields and roads until they finally reached a huge, beautiful house in the bustling city. Inside the grand house, a wonderful feast had been prepared on a large table laden with heavy silver and crystal. There was sweet fruit, fine cheese, and thick meat which the country mouse had never even seen before in his life. The two cousins sat down among the crumbs and began to eat the rich food with great joy and satisfaction. The sheer abundance of the flavors made the country mouse feel as though he had entered a different world entirely. Not a single worry crossed his mind as he tasted the delicacies laid out before them on the linen tablecloth.

Suddenly, a big cat jumped onto the table and let out a loud, terrifying sound that shattered the quiet atmosphere of the room. The frightened mice ran away as fast as they could and hid behind a dark, dusty wall where their hearts hammered wildly against their ribs. After the cat finally left the area, they walked cautiously back out to the table, hoping to finish their interrupted meal. Scarcely had they reached the food when another problem abruptly appeared to disrupt their fragile peace once again. People burst into the room to talk and laugh loudly, forcing the poor mice to run and hide in sudden fear.

The country mouse shook with fear and looked at his cousin with wide, terrified eyes that pleaded for an explanation. He realized instantly that the constant danger made the rich food completely bad for him, poisoning every pleasant taste with terror. I would rather eat simple food in quiet than live in fear every single day, said the country mouse with firm resolve. He told his cousin a hasty farewell, left the dangerous city behind, and returned home to the safety of the country. Not once did he regret turning his back on the glittering halls and the endless anxiety of the wealthy human household.

The mouse family lived in the quiet country for many years, enjoying the peaceful rhythms of the changing seasons. They had many days when the sun shone brightly over the green grass and warmed the earth beneath their paws. Each morning, the country mouse walked through the dew-covered green fields to find fresh roots and tender shoots for breakfast. His cousin from the town often wrote letters to ask about his quiet life, wondering how he endured such monotony. In his replies, the town mouse understood that true safety was much better than the dangerous excitement of the city.

Life in the city was undoubtedly full of rich food, but it was also undeniably full of constant fear and sudden shocks. The two cousins remained good friends even though they lived in very different places and valued completely opposite things in life. They often remembered the dramatic day when the big cat jumped onto the table and scattered their feast. Whenever the country mouse thought about that terrifying trip, he felt profoundly happy to be safely back home. He knew deep down that true happiness came exclusively from peace of mind and quiet surroundings.

The simple food of the country was always more than enough to satisfy his hunger and keep him healthy. He never wanted to see another big cat in his entire life, preferring the company of harmless earthworms and beetles. His little home under the ground was remarkably safe and warm, shielding him from the harsh winter winds. Every evening, he sat with his family and shared a quiet meal without looking over his shoulder in dread. The story of their great adventure was told to the younger mice many times around the glowing embers of their nest.

Life in the country continued for many seasons after that great adventure, bringing steady peace and predictable routines. The country mouse spent his days walking under the bright sun and finding fresh food without any interference from predators. His home was small, but it was completely safe from any big cat or bustling crowd of humans. Every single night, he slept soundly without any fear lingering in his heart or disrupting his dreams. His cousin in the city often thought about the simple meals they used to share before the grand invitation.

The town mouse wrote another letter to ask if he could possibly visit the country again to escape the city noise. The country mouse was delighted to receive the letter and replied quickly, eager to show his hospitality once more. He told his cousin that he was always welcome to come back whenever he needed a respite from danger. When the town mouse finally arrived, he brought small, thoughtful gifts from the city to show his appreciation. They sat together outside their small, earthen home and ate simple food under the open sky.

The town mouse looked around peacefully at the green grass and the tall trees swaying gently in the breeze. He readily agreed that this quiet place was infinitely better than the dangerous and stressful city he had left behind. They talked about old times and laughed heartily about the infamous day of the big cat. The sun went down slowly behind the rolling hills, turning the evening sky into a brilliant red color. Both cousins felt extremely calm as the evening air grew cool and the stars began to appear above them.

The town mouse stayed for several weeks as an honored guest before he finally returned to his own home. He had learned a truly valuable lesson about the true meaning of a good life and what matters most. Safety and quiet are worth much more than expensive food paired with constant, heart-stopping danger. The story of the two cousins was passed down through the family for generations with great care. Young mice would listen closely when their parents told the tale of the city feast and the country burrow.

They understood clearly that a simple life is almost always the happiest life one could ever hope to experience. Nobody in the family ever wanted to leave the safe country home to seek fortunes elsewhere. They lived happily and peacefully for the rest of their days, far away from any city danger or looming threat. The green fields provided all they could ever need, and their nights were filled with unbroken silence. Contentment settled over the burrow like a warm blanket, lasting until the end of their story.
