## st-0142 — The Legend of Sleepy Hollow
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
In a small town, Ichabod lives. He is a school man. He wants to win Katrina. Katrina has a big family land. Brom is another man. Brom wants Katrina too. Brom plays games to make Ichabod move. Brom wants Ichabod to go away. Ichabod tries to get the land. He thinks about the money. He wants a big life. He works every day at school. He wants to see Katrina often. Brom sees this and feels bad.

One night, a big party takes place. Many people come to the house. They talk and eat food. They hear music and play games. Ichabod tries to win Katrina. He wants her to look at him. Brom looks at Ichabod too. Late at night, the party ends. Everyone leaves the large house. Ichabod goes home on an animal. He must go through wood and plant life. The night is black and not warm. He hears something behind him.

A big man appears behind him. The man has no face part on top. He follows the school man. Ichabod goes very high speed on his animal. The big man stays behind him. Ichabod feels very bad and is not brave. He wants to get home now. He moves his animal fast. The man follows him all the way. Ichabod tries to keep going. He does not look back. The night is very long for him. He wants the morning to come.

By morning, Ichabod is not in town. People look for him all over. People find only his old head covering. A broken food part is on the dirt. People find nothing else. Is it a real person? Or is it Brom? Ichabod is never seen again. People still talk about this story. The thing is a big question. No one knows the answer. People remember the story every year. They talk about it at night.

### A2
Deep in a small village, a teacher named Ichabod lived. He hoped to marry pretty Katrina. He wanted to win her family land. However, a happy man named Brom wanted Katrina too. Brom played many games to make poor Ichabod go away. One evening, a big party took place at a large house. Many people came to dance, talk, and enjoy food and music. Ichabod tried to win Katrina heart during the party. Late at night, the party ended. Ichabod went home alone through the dark road. He walked step by step in the night. He thought about the lovely music. He felt happy about the evening. The light above the trees was strong. The wind moved cold air across the field. Ichabod did not know what was going to happen next. He just wanted to reach his bed and sleep.

As he traveled down the road, a big figure appeared. The unusual man had no head. The bad person followed the poor teacher through the dark space. Ichabod went as fast as he could. The dark man stayed right behind him. By morning, Ichabod had left the village completely. Local people found only his old clothes and a broken food piece. They also saw a broken plant on the ground. No one knew if a real person or Brom followed him. From that day forward, Ichabod was never seen again. People talked about the unusual story for many years. The problem of that dark night remained open. Children played near the road every single day. They always looked for the missing teacher. They never found any sign of him. Brom married Katrina after a long time. Everyone tried to forget the bad event. Yet, the memory stayed within the village.

### B1
Deep in a quiet village known for old stories, a tall teacher lived for many years. His name was Ichabod. He hoped to marry pretty Katrina and win her family rich farm one day. However, a happy person named Brom wanted Katrina too. Brom played many games to push poor Ichabod away. Brom always tried to win her love and beat Ichabod every single day. If Brom saw Ichabod, he would make fun of him. Ichabod had felt troubled around Brom for many months. He had always worried about what Brom might do next. The village was full of life, but Ichabod felt alone inside his mind. He walked down the old roads with care and kept his head low.

One evening, a lively party took place at the big house. Many people gathered there to dance and listen to music. Ichabod enjoyed food and music while he tried to win Katrina heart. Late at night, the party ended. Ichabod rode home alone through dark woods. He had never liked the dark trees at night. The wind blew cold air through the branches above him. If he rode fast, he would reach his house soon. The night was very dark, and the road was long. He wanted to see his warm bed and rest his tired feet.

As he traveled down the quiet road, a strange figure suddenly appeared behind him. The unusual rider had no face at all. The dark man followed the worried teacher through dark places. Ichabod rode as fast as he could go. The dark rider stayed right behind him the whole time. Ichabod had never run so fast in his whole life. If he stopped, the rider would catch him. His heart beat fast against his body with fear. He could hear the sound of the other horse getting closer. The dark woods seemed to close in around the poor man.

By morning, Ichabod had left completely from the village without any sign. The people found only his old hat on the ground. They also found a broken round yellow fruit near the old tree. No one could ever be sure whether a real spirit or strong Brom had followed him. If people look for him today, they will find nothing. The village has changed a lot since that night. The children still play near the place where he was last seen. Every person in the town remembers the old story of that dark morning.

From that day forward, Ichabod was never seen in the village again. People still talked about the strange story for many years. The dark event remained open and secret. Everyone remembered the terrible night when Ichabod disappeared into the dark woods. If someone asks about him, local people will share the old story. The memory of the teacher lives on in every corner of the town. Visitors often come to hear the story of the man without a face. They look at the trees and wonder what truly happened to poor Ichabod.

### B2
Deep in a quiet village known for ancient tales, a tall and careful teacher named Ichabod lived. He hoped to marry pretty Katrina and win her family's rich farm one day. However, a happy rival named Brom wanted Katrina too and played many tricks to make poor Ichabod leave. Ichabod was a man who loved food and books very much. Every single day, the teacher worked hard and walked through the dark forest. He often thought about the future and how he could change his life completely. The village was full of mystery, and people always spoke about strange things happening after dark. Many years ago, a story began about a figure that rode without a head. If Ichabod could win the heart of Katrina, he would become the owner of the big farm. Brom knew this plan and wanted to stop it by any possible method. The rival was strong and played games to make the teacher feel very afraid. Ichabod tried to stay calm, but he was always worried about the dark stories. Every evening, the wind blew through the trees and made strange noises around the old school house. The teacher tried to focus on his work, but his mind was full of worry. Katrina was a lovely girl who enjoyed attention from both men. She smiled at Brom because he was strong and funny, but she also listened to Ichabod's long stories. The competition between the two men grew stronger as the months passed by. Brom would often laugh when Ichabod made mistakes during their talks. The poor teacher felt that he had no real support in the village. Yet, he continued to hope that his dream would eventually become true. Life in the country was simple, but the local tales made every night feel heavy and long. People would sit together and talk about the past while the fire burned low. They shared opinions about whether unusual things were real or just part of someone's imagination. Ichabod listened closely to every word, though he tried not to show his fear. He knew that the road home was long and dark, especially when the moon was hidden behind heavy clouds. Still, his desire to win the rich farm kept him moving forward through the difficult days.

One evening, a lively party took place at the big farm house where many people gathered to dance and talk. Ichabod enjoyed the food and music while he tried to win Katrina's heart. The room was bright, and everyone seemed happy during the celebration. Brom was also there, watching every move that Ichabod made. The music played loudly, and couples danced across the floor until late into the night. Ichabod tried to dance with Katrina, but his steps were strange. People looked at him and smiled, while Brom laughed openly from across the room. The party continued for several hours, and the food was served in large amounts. Ichabod ate and drank until he felt quite full and happy. He thought that maybe his luck was finally changing for the better. Late at night, the celebration ended, and the guests began to leave the house. Ichabod said farewell to Katrina and her family with a positive smile on his face. Then, he got on his horse and rode home alone through the dark woods. The night air was cold, and the trees cast long shadows across the road. As he traveled down the quiet path, a strange figure suddenly appeared behind him. The unknown rider had no head and chased the frightened teacher through the dark shadows. Ichabod rode as fast as he could, but the dark rider stayed right behind him. His heart beat fast, and fear filled his mind completely. The horse ran quickly over the ground, but the rider behind them did not fall back. Every time Ichabod looked back, the figure without a head was still there, closing the distance between them. The wind blew hard, and the trees seemed to reach out toward the scared man. He shouted for help, but no one could hear him in the middle of the dark woods. The chase continued for a long time until Ichabod reached the old wooden bridge near the church. He hoped that the rider would stop at the water, but the dark figure kept coming. With a final, hard effort, the rider threw something heavy right at poor Ichabod. The blow knocked the teacher off his horse, and everything went black.

By morning, Ichabod had disappeared completely from the village without a single trace. The villagers found only his old hat and a broken plant on the ground. No one could ever be sure whether a real spirit or bold Brom in special clothes had chased him. People searched the woods for days, but they found nothing else belonging to the missing teacher. Some individuals thought that he had simply run away because of fear and debt. Others believed that something dark had taken him away forever. Brom married Katrina soon after the event and looked very happy whenever the story was mentioned. From that day forward, Ichabod was never seen in the village again. People still talked about the strange tale for many years. The mystery of what happened that dark night remained real history. Children grew up hearing the tale from their parents and older family members. Whenever anyone traveled down that same road after dark, they walked a little faster and looked behind them. The old school house stayed empty for a long time, and new teachers refused to work there. The story became a central part of local history, passed down from generation to generation. Whether the truth would ever come out was a question that no one could answer. Life in the village returned to normal, but the memory of Ichabod Crane lived on. People remembered the tall teacher who wanted a rich farm and a happy marriage. He found only mystery in the dark woods.

### C1
Deep within the somber valley known for its ancient Dutch superstitions, Ichabod Crane made his meager living as the local schoolmaster. Tall, remarkably lanky, and possessed of a crane-like frame, he took meticulous care of his charges while nursing grander ambitions. His eyes were fixed upon Katrina Van Tassel, the lovely daughter of a wealthy farmer, whose hand promised not only affection but the rich family estate. Yet, standing formidably in his path was Brom Bones, a robust and mischievous young man who viewed Ichabod with open disdain. Every single day, the teacher worked hard and walked through the dark forest, his mind racing with possibilities of upward mobility.

The village itself was a crucible of mystery, where locals perpetually whispered of strange occurrences after the sun dipped below the horizon. Generations had passed down the terrifying legend of a headless Hessian trooper who prowled the midnight roads in search of his lost cranium. If Ichabod could successfully navigate this social labyrinth and win Katrina, the sprawling Van Tassel farm would finally belong to him. Brom Bones understood this calculated ambition intimately and sought to dismantle it by any creative method at his disposal. The local rival was physically imposing and constantly played elaborate, humiliating games to make the schoolmaster feel deeply insecure.

Ichabod attempted to maintain a facade of calm, but he remained profoundly haunted by the grim stories circulating the countryside. Every evening, the autumn wind blew through the skeletal trees and manufactured ghostly moans around the old schoolhouse. The teacher tried to focus on his ledger and texts, but his imagination was thoroughly saturated with dread. Katrina remained an elusive prize, a young woman who thoroughly enjoyed the competing attentions of both suitors. She smiled brightly at Brom because his athleticism and boisterous humor amused her, yet she willingly listened to Ichabod's endless folklore.

The intense rivalry between the two men escalated steadily as the harvest months bled into crisp October nights. Brom would frequently erupt into loud laughter whenever Ichabod stumbled over his words during their polite village conversations. The poor teacher often felt entirely isolated, recognizing that the rustic community offered him no genuine institutional support. Still, he clung stubbornly to the hope that his grand aspiration would eventually materialize into reality. Life in the countryside was undeniably simple, yet the pervasive local folklore made every night feel heavy, foreboding, and impossibly long.

In the evenings, neighbors would gather by hearths, speaking in hushed tones about the past while the wood fires burned low. They debated endlessly whether unusual nocturnal phenomena were tangible realities or merely products of an overactive imagination. Ichabod listened intently to every syllable, desperately trying to conceal the trembling of his hands. He knew intimately that the road home was treacherous and completely devoid of light whenever heavy clouds obscured the moon. Nevertheless, his relentless desire to claim the Van Tassel estate propelled him forward through each exhausting day of teaching.

One particularly festive evening, a magnificent harvest party took place at the sprawling Van Tassel homestead. A multitude of local farmers and their families had gathered to dance, converse, and feast upon the bountiful spread. Ichabod relished the rich food and lively music, using the vibrant atmosphere as an opportunity to court Katrina earnestly. The grand room was brilliantly illuminated, and the assembled guests seemed enveloped in genuine merriment. Meanwhile, Brom Bones lingered in the periphery, watching every calculated movement the schoolmaster made with hawk-like intensity.

The fiddlers played furiously, and couples spun across the wooden floor until the hour grew exceptionally late. Ichabod bravely attempted to waltz with Katrina, though his limbs moved with an awkward, angular stiffness. Observing bystanders smiled at his peculiar form, while Brom openly guffawed from across the crowded room. The celebration endured for several hours, featuring an abundance of roasted meats, pies, and sweet ciders. Having eaten and drunk to excess, Ichabod felt a warm, buoyant optimism that his fortune was finally turning.

Long after midnight, the grand festivity finally concluded, and the drowsy guests dispersed into the chilly night air. Ichabod offered a warm, hopeful farewell to Katrina and her indulgent parents before mounting his lanky horse for the solitary ride home. The nocturnal atmosphere was biting, and the gnarled autumn trees cast monstrous, elongated shadows across the winding dirt path. Scarcely had he traveled a mile down the silent trail when a terrifying realization gripped his chest. An enormous horseman, completely lacking a head, detached himself from the shadows and began an immediate pursuit.

Panic instantly paralyzed Ichabod, yet survival instinct forced him to kick his mount into a frantic gallop. The headless specter matched his pace effortlessly, closing the physical distance with terrifying, unrelenting speed. Every time the desperate teacher dared to glance backward, the looming figure without a head was still relentlessly there. The wind shrieked through the branches, and the overarching trees seemed to claw maliciously at the fleeing man. He cried out for divine intervention, but his desperate shouts were utterly swallowed by the vast, indifferent woods.

The wild chase continued unabated until horse and rider finally clattered over the old wooden bridge near the ancient church. Ichabod desperately hoped that the traditional boundary of running water would halt the supernatural pursuer. With a sudden, violent motion, the dark horseman reared up and hurled a heavy, spherical projectile directly at the schoolmaster. The massive blow struck Ichabod squarely with tremendous force, unseating him instantly and plunging his world into absolute darkness.

When the morning sun finally broke over the valley, Ichabod Crane had vanished entirely without leaving a single trace. Astonished villagers searched the area and discovered only his trampled hat lying beside a shattered pumpkin on the road. Debates raged fiercely over whether an authentic malevolent spirit or merely Brom Bones in elaborate disguise had engineered the disappearance. Search parties scoured the dense woods for days, yet no other personal belongings of the missing educator ever surfaced. Some cynics insisted that Ichabod had simply fled the district to escape overwhelming debts, while others whispered of darker fates.

Brom Bones married Katrina shortly thereafter, wearing a knowing, triumphant grin whenever the local legend was brought up in conversation. From that fateful dawn onward, Ichabod was never sighted in the settlement again, cementing his status in local folklore. Children grew up absorbing the terrifying tale from their parents, walking noticeably faster whenever they traversed that haunted road after dark. The old schoolhouse remained abandoned for years, as successive generations of teachers refused to occupy the accursed building. Though ordinary life eventually resumed in the valley, the chilling memory of the ambitious schoolmaster endured forever.

## st-0143 — The Cop and the Anthem

### A1
It is a bad night. An old man named Soapy sits. He sits in a public place. The year is late. The night is not well. He needs a room to stay. He wants to go into a house. He wants free food. He wants a bed.

Soapy walks into a big shop. He eats a lot of food. He has no money. He tells the man. The people make him move out. He falls down on the street. His first plan does not work.

Soapy tries to break a door. A man comes fast. The officer thinks Soapy is well. Soapy tries another idea near a room. He calls out. No man takes him away.

Soapy walks away from the light. He finds an old house. Good music comes through the open way. The sound does something to him. He looks at his life. He knows he spends many years.

Soapy makes a plan to change. He wants to find work. He wants a new life. Then, a man puts a hand on him. The officer takes him. Soapy goes to a room for three months.

### A2

Soapy tried to break a store window with a big piece. A police officer came running over quickly. The officer thought Soapy was just a happy customer. The man tried another kind of work near a movie house. He spoke out like a bad person. Again, no police officer would take him.

Soapy felt very bad and walked away. He reached a low street with an old church. Great music came out through the open door. The nice sound touched his heart deeply. He looked at his past life. He realized that he had spent many years.

Soapy made a promise to change tomorrow. He decided to become a new person. At that moment, a police officer put a hand upon him. The officer took him for staying too long in one place. Soapy was sent to court for three months.

### B1
On a cold night, Soapy sat on a park seat. Winter was coming fast, and he needed a warm place. His plan was simple. He wanted to be arrested so he could go to prison. The sweet sound touched his heart deeply. He looked at his past life and realized something. He has heard many songs in his days. If he listens closely, he will feel different inside. The music brings a sense of peace to his mind. If he stays by the church door, he will hear more notes. He has remembered his family and his old home. The church stands quiet in the dark night.

The officer arrested him for standing around. Soapy was sent to prison for three months. He has finally found what he wanted. If he stays in prison, he will be warm. He has achieved his goal after many hard tries. If winter stays long, he will be safe inside. The prison door closes, and his long journey ends here today.

### B2
On a cold autumn night, an old man named Soapy sat on a stone seat in a public park. Winter was coming fast, and he needed a warm place to stay during the freezing months ahead. His plan was simple and clear. He wanted to be arrested so that he could spend the season in a comfortable prison with free food. He knew that the city jail would provide him protection against the bad weather. The man hoped that his simple scheme would work immediately. He walked away from the park and looked around the streets. Every year, many people faced similar problems when the cold season arrived. Soapy wanted to make sure that he would not suffer during the dark nights. He knew that the police would keep him safe if he broke a small rule. Therefore, the old man prepared himself for the next part of his plan. He walked slowly through the cold air while he thought about his future in the jail.

Soapy stood up and walked into a bright restaurant on a famous street. He ate a large meal without any money, and then he calmly told the waiter that he could not pay. However, the manager did not call the police. Instead, the workers simply threw him out onto the cold ground. His first small scheme had failed completely, but he did not give up yet. He stood on the stone street and thought about what he should do next. The bright lights of the restaurant made him feel even more tired. Many customers looked at him with strange expressions. The manager told the staff to ignore the poor man. Soapy realized that he needed a different approach to solve his problem. He walked away from the building and started to look for another opportunity. He hoped that his next attempt would bring better results.

Next, Soapy tried to break a big glass window in front of a store using a heavy rock. An officer came running over quickly, but the man thought that Soapy was just a happy customer who had made a mistake. The person tried another silly trick near a theater, shouting loudly and acting like a crazy person. Again, no police officer would arrest him, because they believed that university students were only playing games. Soapy felt very sad and upset by these strange failures. He wondered why the officers refused to take him away. The heavy rock was still in his hand, but he dropped it on the ground. People walked past him without paying any attention to his loud shouts. He felt that the whole world was working against his simple plan. The theater lights shone brightly above the street. He left the area and continued his journey through the town.

After several strange and difficult attempts, Soapy felt very tired and walked away from the busy lights of the town. He reached a quiet street where a beautiful old church stood against the dark sky. Wonderful church music flowed out through the open door, and the sweet sound touched his heart deeply. He looked at his past life and realized that he had wasted many years. The sweet song reminded him of the days when his life was different. He remembered his mother and the home where he grew up as a child. Those memories made him feel a strong emotion inside his chest. The music continued to play softly through the night air. He stood very still and listened to every single note. His mind changed as he thought about his future. He decided that he would leave his bad habits behind.

Standing there in the quiet night, Soapy made a strong promise to change and find honest work tomorrow. He decided that he would become a new person and build a better future for himself. At that very moment, strangely, an officer put a heavy hand upon his shoulder. The officer finally arrested him for standing around. Soapy was sent to jail for three months. The judge decided that the man must stay in the prison for the entire season. Soapy accepted his sentence with a quiet mind. He knew that his new life would start after he left the jail. The cold winter would no longer trouble him inside the comfortable prison walls. He felt that everything happened for a reason. His long journey of change had finally begun.

### C1
On a biting autumn night, when dead leaves whispered across the asphalt like restless ghosts, an old man named Soapy sat upon a curved stone seat in Madison Square. Winter was approaching with an unyielding swiftness, and the bitter air demanded a refuge for the freezing months that lay ahead. His plan was deceptively simple, born of necessity and quiet desperation. He wanted nothing more than to be arrested, trusting that the city jail would provide him with dependable shelter, three meals a day, and protection from the frost. Every year, as the temperature plunged, countless destitute souls faced this very same dilemma, seeking a sanctuary behind iron bars. Soapy preferred the stone walls of a prison cell over the charity of missions, which always demanded a degrading moral accounting in exchange for a cot.

Determined to execute his simple scheme, Soapy stood up from the cold bench and walked out of the park into the bustling stream of the avenue. He made his way toward a brightly illuminated restaurant on Broadway, a famous street where wealthy diners gathered to escape the autumn chill. The establishment was known for its lavish cuisine, catering to patrons who never worried about the cost of a single meal. Soapy felt entirely confident that his worn coat and frayed trousers would invite suspicion the moment he crossed the threshold. He stepped through the heavy glass doors, enveloped by the rich aroma of roasted meats and expensive wine, and walked straight to a table near the window. Without hesitation, he ordered a large, expensive meal, planning to consume every morsel before revealing his penniless state to the management.

With deliberate slowness, Soapy ate every bite of the feast, savoring the food while his mind calculated the inevitable confrontation with the staff. When the plates were finally empty, he looked up at the head waiter with a calm expression and stated quite plainly that he had not a single penny in his pockets. He invited the man to call for an officer of the law without delay. Yet, to Soapy s profound astonishment, the manager did not summon the police to escort him away in chains. Instead, two large waiters silently seized him by his collar and the seat of his trousers, lifting him effortlessly off the ground. They unceremoniously threw him out onto the cold stone pavement, leaving him to nurse his bruises in the gutter.

Stunned and humiliated, Soapy stood on the dark sidewalk and tried to formulate a new approach to his persistent problem. The bright lights of the restaurant only served to emphasize his exhaustion and his miserable failure. Passing customers glanced down at him with strange, pitying expressions, while the manager instructed the staff to ignore the poor wanderer entirely. Soapy realized that the law treated him with a frustrating indifference, refusing to acknowledge his deliberate provocation. He brushed the dust from his thin coat and walked away from the building, determined to find another opportunity to secure his winter lodging. His first scheme had collapsed completely, but he refused to abandon his quest for a warm prison cell.

A few blocks away, Soapy noticed a large plate-glass window shining brilliantly in front of a prominent luxury store. Seizing a heavy paving stone from the gutter, he crept toward the glass with a sudden burst of aggressive energy. He hurled the stone directly at the window, expecting the shattering crash to bring a frantic police officer running instantly. The glass broke with a loud, satisfying explosion that echoed sharply down the quiet street. Moments later, a policeman came running over quickly, his heavy boots clattering against the pavement. However, the officer took one look at Soapy s calm face and immediately concluded that the man was merely a merry customer who had made a foolish mistake.

Furious that his second attempt had failed, Soapy spotted a crowded theater district where people were laughing and talking loudly. He decided to try another silly trick near the box office, shouting nonsense at the top of his lungs and acting like a raving lunatic. He danced, he yelled, and he waved his arms wildly in the air, creating a chaotic scene in front of the startled ticket buyers. Yet, once again, no police officer would arrest him for his bizarre public disturbance. The local authorities simply believed that lively university students were only playing harmless games before the evening show. Soapy felt deeply defeated by this infuriating display of leniency from the men in uniform.

He dropped the heavy rock from his tired hand and watched it roll silently into the gutter. People walked past him without paying the slightest attention to his angry shouts or his pathetic appearance. He felt as though the entire world was conspiring against his simple, practical plan. The theater lights shone brilliantly above the street, casting long, mocking shadows across the pavement. With a heavy heart, he left the busy area and continued his lonely journey through the labyrinth of the town. The night grew colder, and his bones ached with a fatigue that went far beyond mere physical tiredness.

After several strange and difficult attempts, Soapy found himself walking away from the bright lights and the noisy crowds of the commercial district. He reached a quiet, residential street where a beautiful old stone church stood proudly against the dark winter sky. Wonderful, swelling organ music flowed out through the wide-open door into the crisp night air. The sweet, melancholic sound touched his weary heart deeply, arresting his steps and demanding his full attention. He looked back upon his wasted past life and realized with sudden clarity how many precious years he had thrown away. The sacred song reminded him of a time when his existence was guided by different principles and higher hopes.

He remembered his mother, the peaceful countryside, and the modest home where he had grown up as an innocent child. Those long-forgotten memories stirred a powerful emotion within his chest, bringing a rare moisture to his tired eyes. The gentle music continued to play softly through the night, creating a bridge between his present misery and his vanished youth. He stood very still in the shadow of the stone wall, listening intently to every single rising and falling note. A profound mental shift occurred within his mind as he contemplated the bleak reality of his future. He made a quiet resolution that he would finally leave his bad habits behind and redeem his broken life.

Standing there in the quiet night, Soapy made a firm, unshakeable promise to change his ways and find honest work the very next morning. He decided that he would become a completely new person and build a respectable future through his own genuine effort. He felt a sudden surge of optimism, believing that it was not too late to reclaim his dignity. At that very moment, strangely, a heavy hand descended firmly upon his shoulder. He turned around to see a stern police officer looking down at him with an expression of official authority. The officer finally decided to arrest him simply for loitering idly on the sidewalk.

Soapy was promptly marched down to the local station house and formally charged before the magistrate the following morning. The judge listened to the brief report and decided without hesitation that the man must stay in prison for the entire three-month winter season. Soapy accepted his unexpected sentence with a remarkably calm and peaceful mind. He knew that his ambitious new life of honest labor would simply have to wait until he was released from custody. Sitting quietly in the transport wagon, he realized that the cold winter would no longer trouble him inside the comfortable prison walls. As the heavy iron gates closed behind him, he felt that everything had happened for a reason, and his long journey of change had finally begun.

## st-0144 — The Celebrated Jumping Frog
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
An old man sits with me. He tells a long story. His name is Jim Smiley. Smiley plays games on everything. He plays on running things. He plays on small things too. Smiley finds one little thing. He names him Dan'l Webster. He trains the thing every day. Dan'l goes higher than any thing. Smiley keeps him in a small place. Smiley loves his little thing.

One person comes to see them. Smiley talks about his thing. The person looks at the thing. He does not believe Smiley. He wants to make a play. Smiley goes to find another thing. The person has a bad plan. He puts much lead into Dan'l. He puts the thing back down. Then the person waits there. Smiley comes back with a thing. Both things are on the land.

The new thing goes away fast. Sad Dan'l cannot move at all. The big lead stops him. The person takes the money away. Smiley finds the bad plan late. He looks at the sad thing. He tries to reach the person. The man is already far away. Smiley sits still with his problem. He thinks about the bad day. He still loves his little thing. The story ends right here.

### A2
An old man sat close to me one afternoon. He had a long story to tell about Jim Smiley. Smiley would pay money on anything. If a dog ran, Smiley paid on it. If an animal sat on a line, he paid. Smiley once caught a small animal called Dan'l Webster. He trained the animal every single day. Dan'l could move higher than any other animal. Smiley kept his best animal in a small box. A new person came along one day.

Smiley told him about the fine moving animal. The person looked closely at the animal. He did not believe the story. He said he would pay if Smiley found another animal. Smiley went down the road to catch one. The person had a plan. He took Dan'l and filled him with much lead. The poor animal was very bad to move. The person put him back into the box. Smiley returned with a nice animal soon.

Both animals were placed upon the ground. The person's animal moved away immediately. Poor Dan'l could not move at all. He only gave a bad little movement. The person took the money and left. Smiley finally discovered the bad plan. He looked down at the animal. He realized why the move failed. The person was already far away.

Smiley was going to find that person again. He wanted his money back from him. That game was very bad for Smiley. He learned a fact about new people. He was going to be sure next time. Animals were fun to train in life. Dan'l was still a special friend to him. The old man finished his long story. I sat down and thought about every word. It was a nice story to hear today.

### B1
An old man with a long story sat near me one afternoon. He began to speak about a fellow named Jim Smiley. Smiley would bet on absolutely anything that could happen. If a dog ran down the street, Smiley would bet money on which animal was faster. If two birds sat on a wall, he would bet. He bet on which one would fly away first. Smiley once caught a tiny animal and named him Dan'l Webster. He trained that good animal every day until it could jump higher than any other animal. Smiley was very proud of his best jumper and always kept him in a small box. One day, a smart man came along and looked at the jumping animal. Smiley told him that Dan'l was the finest jumper in the whole country. The man carefully examined the animal, but he did not believe the story. He finally said that he would accept a bet if Smiley could provide another animal. Smiley went off down the road to catch a new animal. While Smiley was away, the man had a secret plan to win the money. He took the poor Dan'l Webster and filled him completely with heavy metal. The man put the poor animal back inside the box and waited. Smiley soon returned with a fresh animal and handed the race over to the man. Both animals were placed upon the ground to start the big competition. The man's animal moved away immediately, but poor Dan'l could not move at all. Down by the heavy metal inside, the tired animal only gave a sad little shake. The man took the money and walked away with a smile. Only after the man left did a confused Smiley discover the heavy plan. He looked down at the strange animal and realized why the jump had failed. Although he tried to catch the smart man, the man was already far away.

The old man finished his story and smiled at me with a friendly face. I listened to every word because the old man was very funny and kind. Life in this small town always brings wonderful stories about people and their animals. Smiley remained a famous person in the town for many years after that event. People still talk about the day when Dan'l Webster lost the big jumping race. Nobody ever forgot how the smart man won the money with heavy metal. That old story remains an important part of our local history today. I always remember the funny details if I walk down that old road. We should always be careful when we make bets with strange people. Sometimes things do not happen the way we want them to go. However, a good story is always worth sharing with friends and family members. The old man thanked me for my time and walked slowly down the street. I sat there for a few more minutes and thought about the great book. That afternoon became one of my favorite memories from my time in this town. I hope that I will meet another man just like him very soon. Until then, I will keep this funny memory safe inside my mind. I have heard many stories in this town since I arrived here. The old man has told me several nice things about the local past. If you visit this place, you will find many friendly people everywhere. Everyone likes to talk about the old days and famous local events. That is why this town remains special to me and my friends. I will always remember the man who spoke to me on that bright afternoon.

### B2
An old man with a long story to tell sat beside me one afternoon. He began to speak about a fellow named Jim Smiley. This man would bet on absolutely anything that could happen. If a dog was running down the street, Smiley would bet money on which animal was faster. If two birds sat on a fence, he would bet on which one would fly away first. This man loved to place money on every small event that occurred around the town. People knew him well because his bets were always ready. Nobody could stop him from finding a new challenge every single day. He lived for the excitement of the competition and the chance to win cash.

Smiley once caught a tiny animal and named him Dan'l Webster. He trained that smart creature every single day until it could out-jump any other animal around. Dan'l Webster was so special that his jump could reach higher than anyone might expect. Smiley was very proud of his champion jumper and always kept him in a small box. The little creature grew stronger with every single practice session. Smiley spent hours working with the animal to ensure success in every race. Every person in the region knew about the famous jumper. The animal became a symbol of pride for its owner. Many people came from far away to see the jumping frog perform.

One day, a smart stranger came along and looked at the jumping animal. Smiley told him that Dan'l was the finest jumper in the whole country. The stranger carefully examined the animal, but he did not believe the story. He finally said that he would accept a bet if Smiley could provide another animal for him. The stranger looked at the box with deep interest and considered his options. He wanted to win the game by any means possible. Smiley smiled and accepted the challenge without a single moment of doubt. He felt certain that his champion would win again. The stranger waited patiently while thinking about his master plan.

Smiley went off down the wet road to catch a new animal for the stranger. While Smiley was away, the stranger had a secret plan to win the money. He took the poor Dan'l Webster and filled him completely with heavy lead shot. When the heavy work was finished, the stranger put the poor creature back inside the box and waited. The metal made the animal extremely heavy and unable to move properly. The stranger knew that his own animal would easily win the race now. He waited quietly for Smiley to return with the fresh competitor. The secret plan was fully complete and hidden from view. Nothing could stop the stranger from taking the cash.

Smiley soon returned with a fresh animal and handed the race over to the stranger. Both animals were placed upon the ground to start the big competition. The stranger's animal leaped away immediately, but poor Dan'l could not move at all. Weighed down by the heavy metal inside, the tired animal only gave a sad little shake. Smiley watched the scene with total surprise and confusion. He could not understand why his champion failed to jump. The fresh animal crossed the finish line while Dan'l stayed firmly on the ground. The race was over in a matter of seconds.

The stranger took the money and walked away with a smile on his face. Only after the man had left did a confused Smiley finally discover the heavy trick. He looked down at the strange creature and realized why the jump had failed. Although he tried to catch the smart stranger, the man was already far away. Smiley opened the animal and found the hidden metal inside. He realized that he had been tricked by an opponent who played a bad game. The old man told me this story to show how things can change in a moment. Even the best plans can fail when someone acts with a bad trick. Life is full of strange events that teach us important lessons about people.

### C1
It was a heavy, languid afternoon in the decaying mining camp when an old man with a famously exhaustive memory cornered me. His name was Simon Wheeler, and he possessed the tranquil gravity of a man who believed every trivial detail of existence deserved a solemn chronicle. Settling beside me with a worn pipe, he began to unspool the endless history of a fellow named Jim Smiley. To hear Wheeler tell it, Smiley was a man whose entire philosophy of life could be summarized in a single, compulsive urge to gamble. No sooner had the old man set the scene than he launched into illustrations of Smiley's staggering audacity. If a stray dog was trotting down the dusty street, Smiley would immediately wager on which direction it might turn or which animal was faster.

Nowhere was his obsession more pronounced than in his treatment of ordinary domestic fowl and livestock. Should two ordinary birds happen to alight upon a rustic fence rail, Smiley would quietly calculate the odds and bet on which one would take flight first. He would follow a parson to church, wagering on whether the man would take the high road or the low. If the local minister fell gravely ill, Smiley would immediately bet on whether he would recover or pass away. People knew him intimately because his bets were always ready in his pocket, waiting for any conceivable event to unfold. Not a soul in the settlement could ever dissuade him from discovering a fresh challenge every single day.

The man practically lived for the electric thrill of competition and the modest satisfaction of winning cash from his neighbors. His ambition was not merely financial, but aesthetic; he craved the tactical supremacy of being right. Smiley once spent three months training a common bull pup, affectionately naming him Andrew Jackson, to fight anything that barked. Yet his masterpiece, the crowning achievement of his betting career, was an entirely different sort of athlete. Smiley managed to capture a modest, unassuming frog from a nearby marsh and brought him back to his quarters. With infinite patience, he christened the creature Dan'l Webster and resolved to transform him into a sporting legend.

He trained that remarkably focused creature every single day until it could effortlessly out-jump any other amphibian in the county. Dan'l Webster possessed a peculiar athletic gift, and his vertical propulsion could reach heights that astonished even experienced local observers. Smiley was immensely proud of his champion jumper, guarding him jealously and keeping him confined in a small lattice box. Under a strict regimen of education, the little creature grew noticeably stronger with every single practice session. Smiley spent countless hours working with the animal to ensure absolute success in every scheduled contest. He would catch a fly, release it just above the box, and watch with profound admiration as the frog launched itself like a miniature cannonball.

Every single person in the region knew about the famous jumper and spoke of his astonishing athletic feats with hushed reverence. The animal eventually became a powerful symbol of civic pride for its enthusiastic owner. Many curious travelers came from far away just to witness the jumping frog perform its legendary aerial acrobatics. Smiley would sit on his porch, holding the box, waiting for anyone foolish enough to doubt his champion. It seemed nothing could disrupt his steady accumulation of local prestige and occasional silver coins. His faith in Dan'l Webster was absolute, unshakable, and entirely devoid of reasonable doubt.

One particularly warm afternoon, a stranger with a smooth face and calculating eyes walked past Smiley's cabin. Smiley intercepted the traveler, thrust the small box into his hands, and casually remarked that Dan'l was the finest jumper in the whole country. The stranger carefully examined the humble box, lifted the lid, and looked down at the quiet amphibian with a neutral expression. He poked the creature gently, but he did not outwardly believe the extravagant story the proud owner was spinning. After a long moment of deliberate silence, the stranger finally stated that he would gladly accept a wager if Smiley could provide a suitable competitor. He looked at the box with deep, analytical interest, quietly weighing his strategic options.

The traveler desperately wanted to win the game by any means possible, driven by a cold desire to outwit the local champion. Noticing the stranger hesitation, Smiley smiled broadly and accepted the challenge without a single moment of doubt. He felt entirely certain that his undefeated champion would triumph once again and humiliate the newcomer. The stranger waited patiently on the porch while pretending to ponder his master plan for the upcoming race. He asked Smiley to hold his own box for a moment while he walked down to the swamp to find a rival frog. Smiley readily agreed, completely unaware of the malicious strategy forming behind those calm eyes.

Smiley went off down the muddy, wet road with high hopes, eager to catch a fresh animal for the stranger. While Smiley was away, the stranger executed his secret, calculated plan to secure the money by dishonest means. He reached into the lattice box, lifted poor Dan'l Webster out by his slippery legs, and held the creature firmly in his palm. Pulling a small bag of heavy lead shot from his pocket, the stranger forced the metal pellets down the frog throat. He filled the poor animal completely with heavy lead shot until the creature was packed tightly from chest to belly. When the difficult work was finished, the stranger put the heavy creature back inside the box and waited quietly.

The metal made the animal extraordinarily heavy and utterly unable to move its muscular legs properly. The stranger knew that his own newly caught frog would easily win the race now against such a disabled opponent. He sat back on the wooden bench, waiting calmly for Smiley to return with the fresh competitor. The secret plan was fully complete, neatly executed, and completely hidden from casual view. Nothing could stop the cunning stranger from taking the cash and walking away victorious. He suppressed a smug grin as the sound of Smiley boots echoed up the muddy path.

Smiley soon returned, carrying a modest green frog in his hand, and immediately handed the race over to the stranger. Both animals were placed side by side upon the dry ground to start the big competition. Smiley shouted the signal, and the stranger's animal leaped away immediately in a powerful arc. Poor Dan'l, however, could not move an inch from his starting position. Weighed down by the heavy metal inside, the exhausted animal only gave a sad, pathetic little shake of its head. Smiley watched the entire scene with total surprise, profound shock, and lingering confusion.

He could not understand for the life of him why his once invincible champion had suddenly failed to jump. The fresh animal crossed the finish line with ease while Dan'l stayed firmly anchored to the ground. The race was over in a matter of seconds, leaving Smiley staring down in sheer disbelief. The stranger collected the money immediately and walked away with a triumphant smile on his face. Only after the man had vanished down the road did a deeply confused Smiley finally discover the heavy trick. He bent down, picked up the strange creature, and suddenly realized why the crucial jump had failed so miserably.

Although he tried to run after the smart stranger, the dishonest man was already far away and out of sight. Smiley pried the animal open and found the hidden lead shot rolling out onto the wooden floor. He finally understood that he had been ruthlessly tricked by an opponent who played a dishonest game. The old man Wheeler paused here, looking at me with a steady gaze to ensure I grasped the moral of the lengthy narrative. He told me this story simply to illustrate how rapidly fortunes can change when someone relies on deception. Life, according to Wheeler, is full of strange events that teach us unforgettable lessons about human nature.

## st-0145 — The Open Road
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Water Rat goes to visit Mr. Toad. His friend goes too. Mr. Toad has a lot of money. He is very happy now. He does not like water. He does not like boats. He loves a small house on land. Toad wants his friends to go. They move on the open land. They have a large horse and a small cart. They stop and talk every day. The sun is clear. They look around the place.

A big car comes past them. The car is very big and quick. The big car makes a very big sound. The sound scares the large horse. The small cart falls down. It goes right into a low place with water. Toad sits on the ground. He looks at the big car. He loves the big car now. He wants a car of his own. He thinks about the car all day. He has no interest in anything else. Toad is very happy.

The car is very quick. The cart is in the water. Water Rat helps his friend. They look at the car. Toad wants to drive. He thinks about the car every day. He wants to move fast. He looks at the road. He feels very good now. The friends stand by the water. Toad smiles at everyone. He wants a new car today.

### A2
One day, a small friend went to visit his water friend. They went to see a very major man named Toad. Toad grew quite bored of his old water travel. He fell in love with a red color box house. This house stood upon several large things. This painted house was a fine thing to see today. Toad wanted the two friends to travel with him now. They set off down a country road with a strong animal. The animal pulled the house along the way every day. They spent time singing songs in the light. All was well until a fast car came down the road. The bad car made a big sound near them.

The poor animal felt bad and tried to run away. The fast movement made the house turn over completely. The little house fell down into a deep water place. Toad sat in the road and felt quite happy. He could think of nothing else in the world now. He wanted to own a car right then. More trouble was going to come to them very soon. Toad would face many problems because of this new car idea. His friends tried to tell him to be good. However, Toad did not want to listen to them at all. The friends tried to help him see the fact.

Toad only wanted to sit in his new car. He did not care about the trouble he caused. His friends felt bad because he did not listen. Life went on for the animals in the country. Toad dreamed about driving fast on every street. He wanted to buy another car very soon. His mind was full of cars all the time. Nothing else could make him feel so happy. The story of Toad and his cars would continue. He would always want to drive fast cars.

### B1
One day, a small animal named Water Rat went to visit Mr. Toad. Mr. Toad had grown very bored of boats recently. Instead, he had fallen completely in love with a painted vehicle. Toad happily invited the two friends to travel with him. They set off together with a strong horse and a small vehicle. Camping and singing filled their days along the path.

The sun looked down on them during the journey. All was happy until a fast machine moved past them very quickly. The large vehicle made their poor horse feel bad. Then, the movement tipped the little vehicle right into a deep hole. Toad sat in the road and felt glad instead of sad. From that single moment, he could think of nothing else.

He wanted nothing more than owning a car of his own. His friend watched him with care and doubt. Toad had a big dream about the machine. He would buy one if he could find a way. The day was long and full of light. They had started their trip early in the morning.

Every moment felt special to Toad. He did not care about the accident at all. His mind was fixed on the new vehicle. Water Rat tried to speak to him. Toad did not listen to any advice. He wanted to drive a car down every street.

The friends walked back home together after the event. Toad talked about the car the whole way. He could not stop smiling at the thought. His heart was full of hope and joy. The experience changed him completely. Boats were no longer important to him.

Only cars mattered in his mind now. He would spend all his money on a car soon. That was his only plan for the future. Water Rat knew that trouble would come. Toad was too excited to listen to reason. They finally reached home as the sun went down.

Night came over the small village. Toad went to bed with great dreams. He saw cars moving fast in his sleep. The next morning, Toad woke up early. He had made up his mind about the future. He would find a car dealer in town.

Nothing could stop him from his goal. His friends hoped he would forget the idea. However, Toad never forgot anything he loved. His strong feeling grew stronger with each passing day. The painted vehicle was forgotten now. Only the car mattered to him.

Life would never be the same again. He looked forward to his next trip. The road called his name in the distance. He wanted to see the world from a car. Every day would bring a new chance to drive. He knew that he would be happy.

Water Rat stayed at home and shook his head. He worried about his silly friend. Toad did not care about any warning. He packed his bag for the road. The bright sun rose high in the sky. Toad started his new life with a smile.

### B2
The friend known as Rat and his good friend went to visit a wealthy man. This man had a big house near the river. His name was Toad. Toad had grown very bored of small boats during recent months. Instead, he had fallen completely in love with a large, beautiful vehicle. This vehicle was painted in many bright colors. It was a house on wheels which people usually pull with a strong horse. Toad happily persuaded his two friends to take to the open road with him. They set off together with a strong horse and a small van. Camping and singing filled their days as they traveled along the bright country path. All was happy until a fast machine passed them very quickly. The loud vehicle frightened their poor horse significantly. Then, the sudden movement tipped the little van right into a deep hole beside the path. Rather than being upset, Toad sat in the middle of the road completely happy and full of interest. From that single moment, he could think of nothing else. He wanted nothing more than owning a fast machine of his own. He gave a small hint about the trouble still to come in the future. The open road was calling him toward new adventures. He knew that his life would change forever. His friends looked at Toad while he sat on the ground. They knew that something strange had happened to him. Toad did not care about the broken van at all. He only thought about the fast machine that had passed them. The beautiful vehicle had gone away down the road. Toad stayed in the same place for a long time. His friends tried to talk to him, but he did not listen. He dreamed about the road and the fast machines. Life would never be the same for him again.

They had to leave the broken van behind them. Walking back home took many hours. During the walk, Toad spoke only about fast machines. He decided that he must buy one as soon as possible. His friends tried to stop him from having such ideas. They knew that fast machines brought danger and trouble. However, Toad would not listen to any advice. His mind was completely changed by that single afternoon. Days passed, and Toad stayed inside his large house. He refused to see any visitors or friends. Everyone in the village wondered what had happened to him. The truth was simple. Toad was planning to buy a fast machine. He had already written letters to several people who sold such vehicles. Money was not a problem for a wealthy man like him. Soon, a large delivery would arrive at his front door. The quiet life of the river bank was about to end. New adventures waited for them just around the corner. Toad would soon discover the power of the open road. Nothing could stop him from reaching his new goal. The sun shone down upon the green fields while he planned his future. He imagined himself driving very fast across the whole country. His friends hoped that he might change his mind again. Yet they knew that hope was entirely dead. Toad wanted only speed and excitement. No power on earth could hold him back now. The wonderful car would soon become his most prized possession. He smiled at the thought of the coming journey.

### C1
The morning air carried the crisp scent of damp earth and river reeds as the Rat and his companion made their way across the green meadows. They were bound for the grand estate of their wealthy acquaintance, a restless individual known to all as Toad. Toad possessed a magnificent house situated conveniently near the winding river, yet his temper was notoriously fickle. During the preceding months, he had grown profoundly weary of small river craft and ordinary boating excursions. A sudden and total infatuation had seized his imagination, displacing every prior hobby with absolute finality.

This new obsession took the physical form of a remarkably large and colorful vehicle parked near the stables. Painted in brilliant hues of yellow and red, it resembled a miniature house mounted securely upon heavy wooden wheels. Such conveyances were ordinarily utilized by travelers and were designed to be drawn at a steady pace by a robust draught horse. Toad had already acquired the necessary animal along with the gleaming van, leaving only companions missing from his grand design. With characteristic enthusiasm, he immediately set about persuading his two visitors to abandon their quiet routines for the open road.

Not without a degree of quiet apprehension, the friends allowed themselves to be convinced by his energetic rhetoric. They set off together on a bright morning, the strong horse stepping out willingly while the painted van creaked behind them. Days of unalloyed pleasure followed as they meandered along sunlit country lanes beneath a vast and cloudless sky. Cooking over open fires and singing traditional songs filled their afternoons with a rustic charm that felt entirely timeless. Had any observer asked, they would have declared this nomadic existence to be the very pinnacle of earthly contentment.

All such idyllic notions, however, were shattered in less than a single breathless second by the approach of modernity. A monstrous machine came roaring around a sharp bend in the lane, moving with terrifying speed and a deafening mechanical howl. Before anyone could issue a warning, the magnificent vehicle rushed past in a dense cloud of dust and acrid blue smoke. The massive draft horse reared violently in sheer terror, its eyes wide with panic as it threatened to snap the heavy traces. The sudden, chaotic movement proved fatal to their peaceful journey, tipping the little van completely onto its side.

There it lay, ignominiously tilted into a deep drainage ditch beside the road, its cheerful paint scratched and dull. Yet it was not the damage to his prized possession that occupied the owner's wandering attention at this critical juncture. Far from being upset or dismayed, Toad sat squarely in the middle of the dusty road with a vacant, beatific smile upon his face. He remained utterly transfixed, his entire soul absorbed by the vanishing cloud of dust left behind by the mechanical apparition. From that single, defining moment, his capacity for rational thought was entirely captured by the concept of velocity.

He could think of nothing else, desiring above all else to possess a powerful motor car of his own design and speed. As he gazed down the empty lane, he let slip a vague hint concerning the profound trouble that awaited them all in the future. The open road was no longer a pleasant path for camping; it was now a summoning call toward untried and dangerous adventures. Deep down, he understood with absolute certainty that his quiet existence had altered irreversibly within the space of a heartbeat. His friends stood watching him from the grass, recognizing instantly that some fundamental shift had occurred within his volatile mind.

Toad showed not the slightest concern for the ruined van or the cargo scattered haphazardly across the grassy verge. He remained seated in the dirt long after his companions began urging him to stand and inspect the structural damage. To all spoken questions and practical suggestions, he offered only silence, lost entirely in feverish daydreams of grease and horsepower. Life along the peaceful river bank would never resume its familiar rhythm once this fever had taken root in his spirit. With heavy hearts, the two friends realized they had no choice but to abandon the broken vehicle right where it had fallen.

The long walk back to the river bank occupied many hours, punctuated only by Toad's incessant monologue concerning gears and acceleration. He had already decided that a proper machine must be purchased before the current week drew to its inevitable close. Knowing his impulsive nature intimately, his companions attempted to inject doses of caution and common sense into the conversation. They pointed out the obvious hazards, the exorbitant costs, and the general unreliability of such complicated mechanical contrivances. Not a single word of their prudent advice registered upon a mind completely sealed by the glamour of sudden speed.

Days bled into one another, and Toad remained strictly confined within the high walls of his expansive country house. He steadfastly refused to admit visitors, barring even his closest friends from entering the grand front doors. Throughout the neighboring village, speculation ran rampant regarding the mysterious seclusion of the local squire. The reality, of course, was far more mundane than the rumors suggested; Toad was simply engrossed in correspondence with vehicle manufacturers. He had already dispatched urgent letters backed by substantial deposits of money to various dealers operating in distant cities.

For a man of his vast financial resources, acquisition was merely a matter of writing the correct figures upon heavy paper. Soon enough, a massive delivery would rattle up the gravel driveway, changing the domestic atmosphere of the estate forever. The tranquil, predictable days of the river bank were drawing to a close, replaced by the imminent roar of cylinders. New and turbulent adventures waited just around the corner, promising excitement that would surely test the patience of everyone involved. Nothing on earth possessed the power to halt his headlong rush toward this glittering, mechanical obsession.

Outside his study window, the afternoon sun cast long golden shadows across the impeccably manicured lawns and green fields. Inside, Toad smiled broadly as he imagined himself hurtling across the countryside at speeds that would shock the local farmers. Though his friends privately hoped that exhaustion might cause him to abandon his reckless scheme, they knew such hope was entirely futile. Speed had become his new religion, eclipsing every other interest he had ever cultivated during his comfortable life. The magnificent car would soon arrive to become his most cherished possession, setting the stage for chaos upon the highway.
