## st-0150 — The Boy Who Cried Wolf
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago, a young child lives near a small town. He takes his little animal up to a high place. The child feels quite bored. He wants some game. He decides to play a bad thing. He stands high on the land. He states that a big wolf comes for his animal. The people hear his sound. They run up the high place to help. They find no big wolf. They see only a glad child. The people go back down. The child feels glad. The people go away. The child watches the land. He stands on the high place. He looks around every day. He likes to play this game.

The child plays the same thing again. The good people run up the high place. They find a bad thing. They are very cross. They look at the child. They do not like this game. They go back down. Later, a real big wolf truly appears. The small child watches the bad wolf. He runs. He states out for help. He wants someone to help him. The people hear his sound. They leave him. They think it is just a bad thing. No one moves to help. The child learns a hard term. He knows the fact now. He stays home every day. He is good from then on. The wolf does not take him. The child stays in his house. He never plays that game again. He learns his lesson well.

### A2
Long ago, a young boy lived near a small town. Every day, he took his animals up a high area. The work was very simple, and he felt bored. He wanted some fun, so he made a game. He thought of a plan to play with the local people. He wanted to see if they would come to help him.

Standing high on a rock, he spoke out. He said a bad animal attacked his animals. The local people heard him from down below. They dropped their daily work and ran up fast. However, they found no animal, only a laughing boy. Bad about the game, they went back home. They were not happy that he spent their time.

A short time later, the boy played the game again. Once more, the people ran up the land. They discovered that he told a bad thing to them again. They were very bad because he spent their time. They felt not happy and went back down. The boy just laughed at them once more.

Later that week, a real animal truly did appear. The bad boy watched the big animal approach. He did not know what to do. He called out for help with all his power. He spoke very strong so people could hear him. He hoped someone would come to save him from trouble.

However, the people in the town just left him. They were sure it was another bad story. No one moved to help him at all. The poor boy learned a hard time. He learned that no one believes a person. That person tells a bad story. He was very not happy because of his past things.

### B1
Long ago, a young boy lived near a small town. Every day, he took young animals up to a high land. The work was quiet, and the child felt very bored. He wanted some fun, so he made a bad game for local people. He had already decided to play with them. If he made a noise, someone would come up quickly. He always liked to see people run. This life was too slow for him. He wanted something different to happen today. He looked around the quiet place and smiled. He wanted to break the long silence with a loud sound. He thought about the people in the town below. They were always busy with daily work. He wondered if they would run up the hill again.

Standing on a rock, he shouted that a wild animal was attacking. Local people heard him and left their work right away. They rushed up the land to help him save the animals. When they arrived, they found no danger, only a laughing boy. Sad by the joke, the people walked back down. They did not like such a game. The boy had told a big lie to them. Still, he thought it was very funny. He did not care about their lost time. He just wanted to laugh more. The people shook their heads at his silly behavior. They returned to their homes with angry feelings. The boy watched them leave and felt quite happy. He had made them run all the way up the high ground for nothing.

Soon, the boy played the same game a second time. Again, helpful people ran up the high land. They discovered that he had lied to them. They were very angry because he wasted their time twice. They told him to stop this behavior. The boy just smiled and said nothing. He believed he could do it again. The people went back to their houses with bad thoughts. They did not trust him now. Every person in the area knew his bad habit. No one wanted to listen to him anymore. They felt that he was just a bad child who loved trouble. The boy did not care about their warnings at all. He thought they were simply too serious about life.

Later that week, a real dangerous animal truly appeared among the animals. The child watched the bad animal approach. He cried out for help with all his strength. If people had known the truth, they would have rushed up immediately. He had never felt so afraid before. He wanted someone to save his life. He needed help from anyone who could hear him. His heart beat fast against his body. Tears came to his eyes as he waited. The huge beast came closer and closer to him. He screamed again and again with all his power. His voice echoed across the empty hills. He hoped that someone would finally hear his desperate cries.

However, people in the town heard shouts and ignored them. They were sure it was just another lie from the child. No one moved to help him, and they stayed in their homes. The boy learned that no one believes someone who tells lies. Even when he tells the truth, no one will trust him. He realized his mistake too late. The animal came closer and closer to him. He had brought this problem upon himself. Now he was completely alone on the high ground. This hard lesson stayed with him for the rest of his life. He finally understood why trust is so important. He promised himself that he would never tell a lie again. The sun went down as he sat alone in the dark. He knew he had made a terrible mistake that could not be undone.

### B2
Long ago, a young person lived near a small town. Every day, the child took the sheep up to a green area to watch them eat grass. The work was very quiet, and the individual often felt quite bored. Because the child wanted some fun, he decided to play a little trick on the local people. This choice led to trouble later. People in the town were busy with their daily lives. They had much work to do every single morning. The child knew this very well. He wanted someone to come up and talk to him. Life on the green ground was slow. Days passed quickly, but nothing new ever happened there. Therefore, the mind of the boy turned toward a funny plan. He thought that a game might bring some excitement into his day. Without any real danger present, he decided to create a strange emergency. It seemed like a simple idea at first. He did not know how serious the result would eventually become. Every afternoon looked the same until that moment. The sun shone brightly over the grass. The sheep moved slowly across the field, eating day after day. Nothing unusual disturbed the peace. The quiet environment made the hours feel extremely long. The child wanted a change, no matter what it took. He looked down toward the houses far away. He imagined the surprise on the faces of the people if they ran up to help him. The thought brought a smile to his face, though it was a bad choice. He prepared his voice to shout as loud as he could. He wanted everyone to hear his call for help. The trick was ready to begin. He took a deep breath and opened his mouth. The silence of the afternoon was about to end in a very big way.

Standing high upon the rock, the boy shouted with all his might. He cried that a wild animal was attacking his sheep. The local people heard his cry from down below. They immediately dropped their daily work. They rushed up the hill as fast as they could to help him save the animals. However, when they arrived, they found no threat at all, only a laughing boy. They looked around the area with care, but saw nothing dangerous. The animals were eating grass quietly. The boy stood there, laughing at the crowd. The people felt quite upset about the joke. They had left important tasks behind to answer his call. Such behavior made everyone very sad. They walked back down to their homes with heavy steps. A short time later, the boy played the exact same trick a second time. Once again, the helpful people ran up the green area. They discovered that they had been cheated. They were very angry because he had wasted their valuable time twice. The situation was clear to everyone involved. No one likes to be made a fool of by a child. The crowd spoke strong words to the boy before leaving again. They warned him not to do it ever again. The boy just smiled and did not listen to their advice. He thought the game was still very funny. He did not understand the damage he was causing to trust. Trust is a hard thing to build and an easy thing to lose. The people went back to their normal routines. They tried to forget the incident and focus on their jobs. However, the memory remained in their minds. They felt that the child could not be trusted anymore. The stage was set for a real disaster.

Later that same week, a real animal truly did appear among the sheep on the green area. The frightened boy watched the dangerous creature approach his group. He became completely afraid. He cried out for help with all his strength. He was asking someone to come and save him. If the villagers had known the truth, they would have rushed up immediately. However, the people in the town heard his shouts and simply ignored them. They were completely sure that it was just another bad trick from the bad child. No one moved to help him. They all stayed safely in their homes. The poor boy learned the hard way that no one believes someone who tells lies. Even when he is finally telling the truth, no one listens. The danger was real this time, but help never came. The animals ran in fear as the creature drew closer. The boy realized his massive mistake too late. He stood alone against the threat. His voice grew weak from shouting so much. No friendly faces appeared on the path below. The windows of the houses remained closed. The townspeople stayed inside, talking among themselves. They assumed he was just playing his game once more. The consequences of his past actions caught up with him fully. He watched quietly as the situation slipped out of his control. The story ends with a clear lesson about truth. People must always tell the truth if they want to be believed. A person who makes a habit of bad behavior will find no support when a real crisis occurs. This fact remains true across all times and places. The boy stood on the rock, regretting every part of his foolish game. The sun went down, and the day ended in sorrow. He took his remaining animals and walked home slowly. He knew that things would never be the same again.

### C1
In an era forgotten by historians, a young shepherd boy resided on the rustic periphery of a modest, industrious town. Every single morning, his monotonous routine demanded that he shepherd his flock up to the verdant upland pasture to graze upon the lush grass. The solitude of the hills was absolute, and the boy frequently found himself consumed by an overwhelming, suffocating boredom. Yearning for some diversion to break the endless quiet, he eventually resolved to fabricate a malicious trick at the expense of the local townsfolk. This fateful choice would inevitably cultivate a harvest of bitter trouble down the road.

Down in the valley, the villagers remained perpetually occupied with the strenuous demands of their daily labor. They possessed an abundance of heavy responsibilities every single morning, leaving little room for idle amusement. The boy, watching from his elevated vantage point, was acutely aware of their relentless industry and rigid schedules. What he craved above all else was human contact, someone to ascend the steep path and converse with him. Existence upon the sunlit green meadows felt unbearably slow, stretching outward without a single ripple of novelty to disturb the calm.

Days slipped past seamlessly, yet nothing extraordinary or unexpected ever occurred within his isolated domain. Consequently, the restless mind of the boy naturally gravitated toward a mischievous and deceitful plan. He persuaded himself that an elaborate game might inject a much-needed pulse of excitement into his otherwise stagnant afternoon. Devoid of any actual danger or pressing threat, he deliberately manufactured a fictitious emergency out of thin air. At its inception, the idea seemed harmless enough, a clever antidote to the stifling weight of absolute silence.

He failed entirely to comprehend how deeply serious the ultimate consequences of his petty deception would eventually become. Every afternoon followed the exact same predictable pattern until that fateful moment disrupted the natural order. The sun beat down with steady warmth, illuminating the vast expanse of green grass where the sheep drifted slowly in loose formations. Nothing unusual ruffled the profound peace of the countryside, making the passing hours feel agonizingly prolonged. Desperate for any form of disruption, the boy cast his gaze downward toward the distant cluster of houses far below.

He vividly imagined the sudden astonishment etched upon the faces of the hardworking citizens if they suddenly scrambled up the hill to rescue him. The mental image coaxed a wide, knowing grin across his youthful face, even though he knew instinctively that it was a deeply flawed decision. He cleared his throat and prepared his vocal cords to shout with every ounce of physical power he could muster. Determined to ensure that everyone heard his urgent plea for assistance, he rehearsed the performance in his mind. With a sharp intake of breath, he parted his lips, and the serene afternoon air braced itself for a dramatic shock.

Perched high upon a prominent rocky outcrop, the boy released a piercing cry that echoed across the valley with astonishing force. He shrieked frantically that a ravenous wild animal was descending upon his defenseless flock to tear them apart. Below in the town, the hardworking residents caught the desperate sound drifting down the wind and dropped their heavy tools without hesitation. Driven by a sense of civic duty, they scrambled up the steep incline as fast as their legs could carry them to protect the animals. However, upon cresting the hill and reaching the pasture, they discovered no trace of danger, only a grinning child who found their exhaustion hilarious.

The villagers scanned the perimeter with intense scrutiny, yet they found nothing threatening in the tranquil landscape. The docile sheep continued to graze peacefully, completely undisturbed by any predator. Standing amidst the animals, the boy openly mocked the breathless crowd with unrepentant amusement. A bitter wave of resentment washed over the townspeople as they realized they had been played for fools. They had abandoned critical daily responsibilities simply to answer a frantic call that turned out to be a cruel joke.

Such disgraceful behavior filled the adults with profound disappointment, prompting them to turn around with heavy, exhausted footsteps. Scarcely a few days had elapsed before the incorrigible boy decided to execute the exact same despicable trick a second time. Once more, the well-meaning villagers dropped everything and raced up the green slope in a panic. Realizing instantly that they had been deceived yet again, their faces flushed crimson with righteous fury. They were furious that he had deliberately squandered their precious time twice within such a brief window.

The gravity of the situation was unmistakable to every single participant involved in the unfortunate spectacle. No rational person enjoys being humiliated and manipulated by a dishonest child seeking cheap entertainment. Before marching back down the hill, the exasperated crowd sternly lectured the boy, warning him never to repeat such reckless behavior. The stubborn youth merely smiled dismissively, entirely deaf to their wise counsel and impervious to their warnings. Convinced that his game was still brilliantly funny, he remained blissfully ignorant of the invisible foundation he was actively destroying.

Trust is notoriously difficult to construct over time, yet it can be shattered entirely in a fraction of a second. The villagers retreated to their ordinary routines, desperately trying to sweep the irritating incident under the rug and focus on their livelihoods. Nevertheless, the bitter memory lingered persistently at the back of their minds, poisoning any remaining goodwill. They concluded unanimously that the young shepherd could no longer be relied upon under any circumstances. Unbeknownst to them, the stage had been meticulously prepared for a genuine catastrophe that would brook no second chances.

Later that very week, a formidable predator genuinely materialized among the sheep grazing peacefully upon the upper meadow. Paralyzed by sudden, icy terror, the terrified boy watched the massive beast prowl ominously toward his vulnerable flock. Instinctively, he screamed out for help with every remaining ounce of strength in his aching lungs. Had the villagers known the absolute truth, they would have charged up the hillside immediately without a second thought. Instead, the townspeople heard his familiar cries from afar and simply chose to ignore them completely.

Convinced beyond a shadow of a doubt that it was merely another fabricated prank from the malicious child, no one stirred. They remained securely inside their homes, assuming the boy was staging yet another tiresome performance for his own amusement. The unfortunate youth finally learned the harshest lesson life had to offer: habitual liars find no credence even when they speak the absolute truth. As the ferocious creature drew dangerously near, the helpless flock scattered in blind panic across the trembling earth. The sun dipped below the horizon, and the boy trudged home in utter despair, carrying the heavy burden of a lesson learned far too late.

## st-0151 — The Selfish Giant
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A large man lives in a big house. The house has a good place. Every day little people come to play. They look at the place. They play in the big house.

One day the man comes home. He sees people in his place. He is very bad. He says that the place is his. He does not like them.

The man builds a high wall. He puts up a big sign. The little child feels very down. The land has no life now. Bad wind covers the whole place.

Bad wind moves through the land. Water covers the ground every day. The man sits by his room. He feels very not good and no friend. He does not have any friend.

One morning people find a small opening. They go back in. Land comes to life again where people go. Bad wind stays in one room. A small child cannot reach the top.

The man looks out. He understands his work causes the bad wind. He feels much care. He helps the little child up. He takes down the wall.

The place is for the people. The man is very kind now. He plays with child and girl. He finds true love at last. He is very happy now.

### A2
Once upon a time, a big man lived in a house. The house had a large, beautiful garden. Every afternoon, little children came to play in the green land. They listened to small music in the trees. The garden was full of lovely plants. The children always felt very happy there. They ran around the soft grass every single day. They laughed and sang while the sun shone brightly above. The flowers grew tall and sweet in the warm air. Every single moment felt like a wonderful dream for them.

One day, the man returned home from another country. He was very bad when he saw children in his garden. He spoke out high and clear to everyone. He told them the place was for him only. The man built a high wall around the garden. He put up a big sign for all to see. The poor children had no place else to play. They felt very bad about the change. Soon, the year changed. An old time of cold air and white water covered everything.

The cold wind moved through the trees day and night. Water covered the ground day after day. The man could not understand why the year never came back. He sat by his window alone. He felt very cold and sad. Snow fell upon the high wall and the empty paths. No little children came near his big house anymore. He looked outside at the white ground for hours. He wondered if spring would ever return to his home. His heart felt heavy as the dark winter stayed.

One morning, the children found a small opening in the wall. They went back inside the garden. The trees grew again. Lovely life returned to that part of the garden. Only in one section, the cold remained. A small boy could not reach up to the tree parts. He stood under the branches and cried. He could not climb the old wood by himself. The rest of the land was green and warm once more. Only this small corner stayed frozen in time.

When the man looked outside, he understood his own mind. He felt deep trouble in his heart. He walked out of his house. He helped the little boy up into the tree. He decided to break down the high wall. He took a heavy tool and hit the stones hard. The wall fell down piece by piece onto the ground. The boy smiled and gave the man a sweet hug. Spring came back to the final corner of the land. Birds began to sing their happy songs once more.

From that day forward, the garden was for the children. It was for them now and later. The man became very kind. He played with the boys and girls every single afternoon. He found true love and peace at last. The children brought great laughter to the large house. They shared their games and stories day after day. The man smiled as he watched them run around. His life was full of wonderful friends now. He knew that he would never be lonely again.

### B1
Once upon a time, a huge man lived in a wonderful house. The house had a large, beautiful garden. Every afternoon, little children came to play in the green land. They listened to sweet birds sing in the trees. The garden was full of lovely flowers. The children always felt very happy there. They loved the sun and the warm air. The birds flew above the nice plants. Every single day brought new joy. The children ran around with big smiles. They played games until the evening came. The man was away in a distant country. No person told the children to stay out. The wonderful garden belonged to the kids. They shared the space and had great fun. The flowers grew tall and bright. The sky stayed blue and clear. Nothing bad happened during those peaceful hours.

One day, the man returned home after a long absence. He was very angry when he saw children playing in his garden without a word. He shouted and told them that the place belonged only to him. To keep everyone out, the man built a high stone wall around the garden. He put up a big sign on the wall. The poor children had no other place to play. They felt very sad about the change. Soon, spring left the land. A long winter of cold ice and white snow covered the garden. The cold wind blew through the trees. Ice covered the ground day after day. The man could not understand why spring never came back. He sat by his window, feeling very cold. He felt sad and had no friends. The white snow stayed all year. The plants died in the cold ice. No birds sang in the bare trees. The man looked out at the dead land. He wished for warm weather to return. The heavy snow covered every single path.

One morning, the children found a small gap in the wall. They slipped back inside the secret garden. The trees grew green leaves again. Lovely spring returned to that part of the garden. Only in one corner, winter remained. A tiny boy could not reach up to the tree branches. He stood under the cold bough and cried. The little child had no way to climb up. The snow and ice stayed on that single branch. The other children tried to help him. They could not reach the high wood. The boy looked up with tears in his eyes. He wanted to sit on the strong limb. The cold wind blew around his small body. He shivered in the freezing air. The winter refused to leave that corner. The other kids watched him with sad faces.

The man looked outside. He finally understood that his own mind had caused the long winter. Filled with deep sorrow, he walked out of his house. He helped the little boy up into the tree. He used his hands to lift the child. The man saw this lovely scene. He decided to tear down the high wall. He took a heavy tool and broke the stone. The wall fell down piece by piece. The children watched the man with wide eyes. They smiled as the stone tumbled to the ground. The man felt a warm feeling in his chest. He knew that he had done a good thing. The sun shone down upon them. The cold ice melted away into water. The small boy hugged the kind man. Spring filled the entire garden at last.

From that day forward, the garden belonged to the children. The man became very kind to everyone. He played with the boys and girls every single afternoon. Surrounded by happy friends and beautiful flowers, the man found true joy. The children laughed and ran across the green land. Birds sang sweet songs in the tall trees. The sun warmed the earth every single morning. No wall stood in the way of the kids. The man sat under the green branches. He watched the little ones play games. Life was good and full of peace. The dark days of winter were gone. The wonderful garden stayed bright and full of life. Every person in the village knew about the kind man. The children visited him day after day. They shared food and stories in the warm sun. The happy man smiled at his young friends. They lived in peace for the rest of their days.

### B2
Once upon a time, a huge giant lived in a wonderful castle which had a large, beautiful garden. Every afternoon, little children came to play in the green grass, listening to sweet birds sing in the trees. The garden was full of lovely flowers, and the children always felt very happy there. The sun shone brightly upon the green grass every day. The birds flew from tree to tree, singing songs that filled the air with joy. The children ran around the paths, playing games until the evening came. They laughed and talked while they enjoyed the beautiful flowers. Every single day was full of fun for both the boys and the girls. The castle stood tall above the ground, watching over the lovely place. No one ever disturbed the peace of this wonderful garden. The giant had been away for a very long time in another country. He stayed there because he had business to finish with some friends. When his work was finally finished, he decided to return home. He packed his bags and traveled across the land until he saw his castle again. He was very angry when he saw children playing in his garden without permission.

He shouted loudly and told them that the place belonged only to him. His voice was so strong that the children felt afraid. They stopped playing immediately and ran away as fast as they could. To keep everyone out, the giant built a high stone wall around the garden and put up a big sign. The poor children had nowhere else to play, and they felt very sad about the change. They walked home with heavy hearts, wondering where they would go next. Soon, spring left the land, and a long winter of cold air and white snow covered the giant garden. The cold wind blew through the trees, and ice covered the ground day after day. The giant could not understand why spring never came back to his property. He sat by his window, feeling very cold and upset while the white snow stayed all year. He looked outside at the empty yard, but nothing grew in the soil. The trees remained open, and the ground was hard like stone. He wished that the warm weather would return to his land. However, the snow continued to fall from the sky every single day. The cold weather stayed for months, making the giant feel even more isolated.

One morning, the children found a small gap in the wall and slipped back inside. They moved through the opening very quietly so the giant would not hear them. Wherever the children went, the trees grew green leaves again and lovely spring returned to that part of the garden. Only in one corner, winter remained because a tiny boy could not reach up to the tree branches. He stood under the big tree, crying softly because he could not climb it. The snow and ice stayed around him, and the wind blew cold air upon his face. When the giant looked outside, he finally understood that his own bad actions had caused the long winter. Filled with deep regret, he walked out of his house and gently helped the little boy up into the tree. As soon as the giant saw this lovely scene, he decided to tear down the high wall. He took a large tool and began to break the stones apart. The wall came down piece by piece until nothing was left of it. From that day forward, the garden belonged to the children forever after. The giant became very kind, and he played with the boys and girls every single afternoon. Surrounded by happy friends and growing flowers, the giant finally found true happiness at last. The birds came back to sing in the branches, and the sun warmed the earth again.

### C1
High upon a windy hill stood a magnificent castle surrounded by a garden of extraordinary beauty. Every afternoon, as the golden light began to soften, little children would slip through the iron gates to play on the emerald grass. They spent hours listening to sweet birds sing their melodious songs from the high branches of ancient trees. The garden was a paradise filled with countless flowers that bloomed in shades of crimson, gold, and violet. Every single day was a symphony of laughter and joy for both the boys and the girls who ran along the winding paths. No one ever disturbed the profound peace of this enchanted sanctuary, and the towering castle walls watched over the children with a protective grace. For seven long years, however, the master of this domain had been entirely absent from his estate. The giant had traveled far across the mountains to visit an old friend who lived in a distant country, intending to stay only a few weeks. Yet, conversations and endless affairs of property kept him away far longer than he had initially planned. When his business was finally concluded, he packed his heavy leather bags and began the long journey home across the rugged valleys. As he crested the final hill, his majestic castle loomed against the twilight sky, and a sharp frown crossed his weathered face. To his utter astonishment, the sound of children laughing drifted merrily over the stone wall. Striding furiously through the arched gateway, the giant beheld the intruders trampling his precious turf and scattering petals across the path. He shouted in a voice like rolling thunder, demanding to know what right they had to trespass upon his private property. His formidable presence struck instant terror into the hearts of the little ones, who froze in sheer panic. Not daring to look back, they scrambled frantically toward the gates and fled as fast as their trembling legs could carry them. The giant watched them scatter into the distance with a grim satisfaction, convinced that his domain was now safe from disturbance. To ensure no one would ever return, he immediately hired masons to construct a massive, jagged stone wall around the perimeter. Upon completion of the barrier, the giant hoisted a large wooden sign warning all intruders of severe consequences. The poor children, having nowhere else in the surrounding countryside to play, were left with heavy hearts and a profound sense of loss. They wandered disconsolately along the dusty country roads, wondering where they might find another patch of green earth. Meanwhile, within the confines of the newly fortified garden, the atmosphere shifted with chilling abruptness. Spring departed from the land, yielding to the harsh dominion of a prolonged and bitter winter. A cruel frost settled deep into the soil, and howling winds swept relentlessly through the bare, skeletal branches of the trees. Layers of thick white snow blanketed the earth day after day, turning the once-vibrant landscape into a monochromatic wasteland of ice. The giant sat alone by his frost-rimed window, wrapped in heavy furs, watching the desolate expanse with growing bewilderment and dismay. He could not comprehend why spring refused to visit his property when the rest of the kingdom was bursting with fresh blossoms. The bitter cold gripped the castle grounds with an unyielding tenacity, and icicles hung like crystal daggers from the eaves. He wished fervently that the warm sun would finally melt the frozen crust and restore color to his lifeless garden. Yet the snow continued to fall from the gray sky week after week, deepening his sense of isolation and regret. One crisp morning, after the frost had relented just enough to create a tiny fracture in the aging mortar, the children found a secret gap. They slipped through the narrow opening with cautious footsteps, whispering amongst themselves so the forbidding master of the house would not hear them. Wherever their small feet touched the frozen ground, a miraculous transformation occurred before their eyes. The trees immediately burst into pale green leaves, and delicate crocuses poked their heads through the melting snow to greet the returning warmth. It was as if spring had been waiting just outside the gate, longing for the touch of innocent laughter to unlock the frozen earth. Only in the furthest, most secluded corner of the sprawling garden did winter maintain its icy grip. There stood a magnificent pear tree completely covered in frost, beneath which a tiny boy stood weeping bitterly. Because he was exceptionally small, he could not reach the lowest branches to pull himself up into the welcoming shelter of the foliage. The bitter wind swirled around his small shoulders, and stinging snowflakes gathered in his golden hair as he gazed up with tearful eyes. The giant watched this touching scene from his high window, feeling a sharp pang of remorse pierce his hardened heart. He finally understood the true nature of his selfishness and how his own bitter actions had locked out the joy of the world. Overwhelmed by deep contrition, the giant pushed open his heavy oak door and hurried down the winding stone staircase into the courtyard. He stepped out onto the grass with hesitant steps, careful not to frighten the other children who immediately scattered out of sheer habit. He walked softly across the lawn toward the shivering child in the corner, his expression softening into a gentle, unfamiliar smile. Kneeling down beside the pear tree, the giant lifted the little boy with immense care and placed him gently upon a high branch. At that exact moment, the tree broke into a sudden cascade of fragrant white blossoms, and a flock of singing birds descended to flutter around them. Seeing that the giant was no longer cruel, the other children ran back from the shadows with joyful shouts and eager, smiling faces. They climbed up the trunks and swung from the branches, filling the air once more with the music of their carefree laughter. The giant watched them with a warm, contented glow in his chest, realizing that his grand castle had been terribly empty without them. Without a moment's hesitation, he fetched a heavy iron sledgehammer from the toolshed and marched purposefully toward the perimeter. With mighty blows, he began to shatter the stones of the wall, reducing the forbidding barrier to rubble piece by piece. By midday, the high wall had vanished entirely, leaving the garden open and welcoming to the surrounding meadows and countryside. From that day onward, the children played freely every single afternoon, turning the vast lawn into a shared playground of endless delight. The giant, once bitter and isolated, found his heart transformed by the simple presence of his young companions. He spent his remaining years joining in their games, listening to their stories, and sharing in their boundless enthusiasm. Surrounded by blossoming flowers, singing birds, and ringing laughter, the giant finally discovered the true meaning of happiness at last.

## st-0152 — The Ugly Duckling
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One day, a big house has a family. One large thing open up. A little child come out. He looks very different. He is big and not good. Everyone see him right away. Other people do not like him. His family make him go down. His mother want him go away. He feels bad and small.

The bad little child leave the house. He walks far across the land. Time turn into a long time. The day is very bad. Food is little for the little man. He lives through the bad time. Then time come with good light. The child stay on the water. He sees white things near. He looks at them. He wants to be like them.

He looks down at the water. He get a big change. He is no longer not good. He becomes a white thing. The other white things take him in. He finds a new home. He feels good every day. His mother is not here now. He has new friends. He lives a long life. He looks at the water again. The day is very good now. He feels well now. The story end here.

### A2
On one fine summer day, a big land place welcomed new animals. One small child animal came from a large thing. He looked very different from the other small animals. He was big and quite difficult to like. Everyone on the land place noticed his different look right away.

Because he looked different, the other animals were bad to him. They were bad to him every day. His brothers and sisters pushed and hit the little animal. Even his mother grew full of the trouble. She wished he would just go away. The poor small animal felt completely without anyone and without happiness. He decided to leave the land place behind.

He walked far across the country. The year turned into a long, hard, and cold period. The water and air became very cold. Food grew very hard to find for the single traveler. He had to live all on his own. Deep white water drops and bad air made the period difficult. However, he managed to live through the bad period.

A new period finally arrived after the cold. The good light made the hard water clear across the water place. The animal went out over the big water place. He saw a group of great white water animals close by. He liked their high quality, wishing to be like them. He expected the white water animals to drive him away. He put his head down because of his look.

He looked down at his own water image in the water place. He received a big surprise. He had grown up into a beautiful white water animal. He was no longer a difficult large animal. The other lovely white water animals welcomed him into their group. He was happier than he ever thought to dream. He would stay there for a long time.

### B1
On a warm summer day, a farm welcomed new animals. One unusual bird came from a large egg. He looked very different because he was big, gray, and strange. Everyone noticed his appearance right away. The farm was a busy place for all the animals. Many things lived around the old red building. The sun gave bright light upon the green land. Every day brought new times for the young farm animals. The mother bird had waited a long time for her family. She felt glad to see the little ones running about the yard. However, this new bird did not look like the others. He was much bigger than his brothers and sisters. His body had a strange color that people spoke about. The farmer looked at him with a strange expression. No one had ever seen such a strange animal on this farm before. The days were long and full of bright light. Little did the bird know about the hard times ahead.

Because he looked unlike the rest, other animals began to bother him. The little bird was frequently hit and pushed around. Even his mother grew tired and wished he would leave. Feeling completely blue, the poor young bird decided to go away from the farm. He walked far until the warm season turned into a long, hard, and cold winter. The weather became freezing, and food grew very low. He had to survive all on his own through the deep snow. It was a difficult time, but he kept going through the terrible season. The wind blew cold air across the white fields every night. He searched everywhere for something small to eat. Sometimes he found nothing for days and days. His legs grew weak from walking through the heavy snow. He missed the comfort of the farm and his mother care. Yet, he kept moving forward because he wanted to live. The cold days tested his strength in every possible way. He hoped that tomorrow might bring better weather and some food.

When spring arrived, the warm sun made the ice go away. The bird swam out onto the lake and saw white birds. He looked at their beauty, wishing that he could be like them. Expecting to be sent away, he slowly put his head down. However, when he looked down at the water, he received a huge surprise. He had grown up, and he was no longer a strange bird. Instead, he had become a beautiful white bird. The other birds welcomed him warmly, and he was very glad. He swam across the clear water with his new family. The sun warmed his body as he moved well. He realized that his hard life was finally over. A bright future stood before him on the blue lake. All the trouble of the past winter disappeared completely. He sang a quiet song of joy to the spring morning. The other birds stayed close to him throughout the day. He had finally found a place where he truly belonged.

### B2
On a warm summer day, a little farm welcomed several new animals into the world. Among them, one unusual bird came from a large egg. He looked very different from the other children because he was big and quite strange. Everyone on the farm noticed his strange appearance right away. Because he looked unlike the rest, the other animals began to bother him constantly. The little bird was frequently hit and pushed around by his brothers and sisters. Even his mother grew tired of the constant trouble and wished he would just go away. Feeling completely sad, the poor bird decided to leave the farm behind. He wandered far across the land until autumn turned into a long, hard, and cold winter. The weather became freezing, and food grew very low for the single traveler. He had to survive all on his own through the deep snow and bitter winds. It was a difficult time, but he managed to live through the terrible season. Every single day brought new challenges, yet he kept moving forward through the snow. He had no friends to help him when the night became dark and cold. The wind blew fiercely around his tiny body while he searched for something to eat. Many hours passed slowly as he tried to keep warm under the trees. He often thought about his old home and wondered if life would ever improve. The harsh weather tested his strength more than he had ever expected before. Still, he refused to give up hope because he wanted to live. Months went by slowly until the freezing air finally started to change into something milder.

When spring finally arrived, the warm sun made the ice water soft across the lake. The beautiful bird swam out onto the water and saw a group of wonderful white birds moving nearby. He admired their beauty, wishing that he could be like them. Expecting to be chased away because of his ugly look, he sadly bent his head down. However, when he looked down at his own reflection in the water, he received a huge surprise. He had grown up, and he was no longer a strange bird. Instead, he had transformed into a beautiful bird himself. The other lovely birds welcomed him warmly into their group, and he was happier than he ever dared to dream. He stayed with them day after day, and his new life was full of joy. The people on the farm never saw him again, but he finally found his true home. He swam near the edge of the water and watched his new friends play. They accepted him without any questions about his past or his appearance. His heart felt light and free as he moved smoothly across the blue surface. Every moment spent with the group brought him peace that he had never known before. The sun shone brightly upon the lake, making the water sparkle like silver. He felt proud of the journey that had brought him to this wonderful place. Nothing could take away the happiness that now filled his daily life. He knew that his difficult days were finally over for good. The other birds shared food with him and taught him how to dive for fish. Life by the lake was everything that he had ever hoped to find.

Every single morning brought a new reason to smile as he swam across the blue water. His family far away could never imagine how well things turned out for him. Winter came and went again, but he was strong enough to face any cold wind. Spring returned with more warm sun and bright flowers along the edge of the lake. Other animals often stopped by to watch the wonderful white birds play in the water. No one could remember the strange little creature that had left the farm so long ago. He was now a proud and strong member of the group, loved by everyone around him. His heart was full of quiet, and he knew that he would never feel sad again. Years passed quietly as he lived a long and calm life with his new friends. Whenever the sun went down, they rested together under the bright evening sky. The seasons continued to change, bringing new flowers and green leaves each year. He watched the young birds learn how to swim and fly across the open sky. Sometimes visitors from nearby towns walked along the shore to admire the beautiful scene. They pointed at the white birds and spoke about how graceful the creatures looked. He heard their kind words and felt a deep sense of pride inside his chest. His old life on the farm felt like a distant dream from another lifetime. He had grown strong and wise through all the experiences that he had survived. The lake became a safe haven where peace and friendship ruled every single day. He shared many quiet afternoons resting beside the water with his closest companions. Their company made every hour special and full of quiet joy.

It was a happy ending for the bird who had survived such a difficult beginning. He often thought about the past, but those bad memories had lost their power over him. Life by the water was everything that he had ever wanted or hoped for. The warm summer days returned each year, bringing new joy and continuous freedom. He swam proudly day after day, showing his great beauty to the world. Nothing could ever take away the quiet that he had finally found. His story became a wonderful memory shared among the birds living by the lake. And so, the beautiful creature lived happily for the rest of his days. The wind whispered softly through the trees while the sun set behind the hills. He closed his eyes and thanked the world for the wonderful life he now had. No trouble could ever reach him here in this peaceful home by the water. He was surrounded by love and friendship that would last forever. The other birds gathered around him as night fell across the calm lake. They rested together in complete safety, knowing tomorrow would bring more beauty. His heart remained full of gratitude for the wonderful turn his life had taken. The farm where he suffered was just a faded memory in his mind. He was a new creature now, strong and proud in every way possible. The story of his journey lived on among those who knew him best. He smiled as he drifted peacefully into a deep and restful sleep. And so the beautiful bird found his true place in the world at last.

### C1
The summer air hummed with a heavy warmth as the old farm welcomed a new generation into the world. Among the broken shells of a clutch that had taken far too long to hatch, one unusually large egg remained. When it finally splintered open, out tumbled a creature whose proportions defied every standard of the barnyard. He was ungainly, uncomfortably massive, and possessed a raw, awkward appearance that immediately set him apart from his nestmates.

Word of the strange hatchling spread through the poultry yard like wildfire, breeding an instant and casual cruelty. From his very first hours, the other animals made it clear that he did not belong among them. His siblings nipped his heels relentlessly, while the older ducks shoved him aside at every feeding trough. Even his mother, exhausted by the constant friction and the harsh whispers of the flock, wished aloud that the shadows would simply take him away. Overwhelmed by a profound and chilling loneliness, the young outcast finally turned his back on the only home he had ever known.

He wandered across the broad, indifferent fields until the golden hues of autumn curdled into the bitter gray of winter. The landscape hardened into a cruel maze of ice and biting wind, offering neither shelter nor sustenance for a solitary traveler. He scraped through the deep drifts on his own, dodging freezing gusts that threatened to snap his fragile bones. Every waking hour demanded a desperate struggle just to secure enough warmth to survive the coming night. Shivering beneath the skeletal roots of ancient trees, he often closed his eyes and dreamed of a place where cruelty did not rule the air.

Months dragged past in a relentless sequence of frost and hunger, testing his endurance to its absolute limit. Yet beneath his fragile exterior, a stubborn spark of life refused to be extinguished by the winter gales. He kept moving forward through the snow, driven by an instinctive hope that he could not quite articulate. When the ice at last began to fracture under the persistence of a milder breeze, his heart gave a hopeful beat. The long, agonizing trial of winter was finally yielding to the gentle promise of a changing season.

With the arrival of spring, the surface of the lake thawed into a brilliant mirror reflecting the azure sky. Drawn by the sunlight, the young bird ventured toward the water and caught sight of a magnificent flock drifting near the reeds. They were creatures of breathtaking elegance, possessing a pristine white plumage that put his own gray feathers to shame. Certain that they would drive him away with sharp beaks, he lowered his head in resignation and prepared for rejection. He braced himself for the familiar sting of hostility as he drifted closer to the water edge.

Instead of the expected violence, a sudden quiet descended upon the shallows as he gazed down at his own reflection in the water. The creature looking back at him was no longer the awkward, gray-feathered outcast of the winter snows. Months of quiet survival had reshaped his frame into something sleek, powerful, and undeniably radiant. He had transformed into a swan, sharing the very same breathtaking grace that he had admired from afar. The majestic birds glided toward him, welcoming him into their circle with gentle dips of their long, curving necks.

Surrounded by his new companions, he felt a profound sense of peace wash over his weary spirit. The farm where he had suffered such relentless abuse now felt like a distant memory from another lifetime entirely. Here by the edge of the lake, no one asked about his past or judged the awkwardness of his early days. The other swans shared their food with him, teaching him how to dive smoothly beneath the glittering surface. Every sunlit afternoon brought a quiet joy that he had never thought possible during those dark winter nights.

Time flowed onward as gently as the currents of the lake, bringing new blossoms each spring and golden leaves each autumn. Other animals often gathered on the shore to watch the graceful flock move in effortless harmony across the water. No one among the onlookers could have ever guessed that this proud creature was once the despised duckling of the farm. He grew wiser with every passing season, finding deep contentment in the simple rhythm of daily life. Sometimes, travelers from nearby villages paused along the path to admire the breathtaking scene before them.

Hearing their words of praise, a quiet swell of pride warmed his chest without a trace of arrogance. He knew the exact value of the peace he now enjoyed because he had tasted the bitter dust of rejection. The lake had become an impenetrable sanctuary where friendship and security defined every passing hour. Beside his closest companions, he spent long afternoons resting beneath the shifting shade of willow trees. Their silent understanding made every single moment feel purposeful and completely complete.

The harsh beginnings of his life had faded into the background, stripped of their power to cause him pain. Each morning presented a fresh reason to smile as he pushed off into the cool, crystal water. Nothing could ever dismantle the profound tranquility that had finally taken root within his heart. Surrounded by love that asked for nothing in return, he drifted into a state of permanent grace. The wind whispered softly through the reeds as the sun dipped below the distant hills in a blaze of gold.

He closed his eyes against the fading light, feeling entirely safe within the embrace of his chosen family. The bitter winds of winter held no terror for him now that he possessed both strength and companionship. His journey had been arduous, but every single trial had led him precisely to where he was meant to be. Drifting peacefully into a deep sleep, he rested knowing that his wandering days were finally over. The beautiful bird had found his true home in the world, and nothing would ever disturb his rest again.

## st-0153 — The Emperor's New Clothes
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago, a man is a ruler. The ruler wants new things to put on. He tries new things every day. Two men visit the large house. They say they make the best things. They make the best things to put on. The things have a power. Bad people do not see it. The ruler thinks this is good. He wants to find bad men. He gives the two men much money.

The two men set up open places. The old man goes to see them. He sees nothing at all. He is very without help. He does not want to look bad. The ruler visits the room too. He sees nothing on the open places. He says the colors are good. Everyone says the things are great. The ruler puts on his new set today.

The ruler walks in the town game. People look and say yes. No one wants to look bad. A small child calls out now. He says the ruler has no things on. The true words go out fast. The ruler hears them now. He knows the child is right. He still walks to the end.

### A2
Long ago, a person loved nice clothes. The man spent every day trying on new dress. One morning, two men came to the large home. They said they were special men. They told the man that they could make good clothes. The men explained that the clothes had a special power. The clothes would remain not able to be seen by anyone who was not good.

The man thought this was a good idea. He gave the men a lot of money to start work. The two men set up open spaces. They acted like they worked hard. Soon, the man sent an old friend to see the product. The old man saw nothing at all. He was too full of fear to say it.

Soon after, the man visited the room with other leaders. They also saw nothing. Yet they all spoke well of the colors. Everyone agreed that the clothes were very good. The man decided to wear his new clothes during the big walk.

On the day of the walk, the man went through the streets. He wore his clothes. All the people spoke well and acted like they saw his clothes. No person wanted to look bad in front of other people. Suddenly, a small child spoke out that the man wore nothing.

The true words went fast through the group. Soon everyone was talking. The man heard them. He finally knew that he had been bad. He kept walking until the end of the street.

### B1
Long ago, a king cared about nothing except fine clothes. His whole life was spent trying on new items every single day. One fine morning, two men visited the town. They claimed to be master workers. They told the king that they could make the most wonderful clothes in the whole world. The men explained that the material had a special power. It would remain completely hidden to anyone who was bad or wrong for their job. The king thought this was a good idea. He could easily find out which leaders were poor at their work. He gave the men a lot of money to start working immediately. The king has always loved fine clothes. If the king wears the clothes, he will see who is nice. He has never seen such wonderful material before today. The king has spent much money on nothing. He wants to find out the truth about his workers.

The two men set up empty tables. They made movements to look like they worked very hard. Soon, the king sent his old minister to see how the product was coming along. When the old man looked at the empty room, he saw nothing at all. He was too afraid to admit it. He did not want anyone to think he was bad or wrong for his position. Soon after, the king visited the room himself with other people. They also saw nothing on the tables. Yet they all spoke well of the wonderful colors and patterns. Everyone agreed that the clothes were great. They told the king they looked good. The old minister has visited the room already. If the minister looks closely, he will see nothing. He has not told the truth about the empty room. The minister has kept the secret from the king.

The king felt very glad. He decided to wear his new suit during the big town street show. On the day of the show, the king walked through the streets wearing his hidden suit. All the people standing along the road made sounds of joy. They looked at him and spoke well of his fine new clothes. Nobody wanted to look bad or unusual in front of their neighbors. Suddenly, a small child cried out that the king was wearing nothing at all. The true words spread quickly through the crowd until everyone was talking about the secret. The red-faced king heard them and finally realized that other people had missed the truth. He knew the child was right. He kept walking proudly until the end of the long street. The small child has shouted out the truth. If the king finishes the walk, everyone will know the secret. The king has walked through the long street. He will remember this strange day for a long time.

### B2
Long ago, a very proud ruler cared about nothing except fine personal items. His whole life was spent trying on new clothing every single day. One fine morning, two smart men visited the big home and claimed to be master makers. They told the ruler that they could make the most wonderful clothing in the whole world. The men explained that the material had a special power.

It would remain completely hidden to anyone who was silly or unable to do their job. The ruler thought this was a brilliant idea because he could easily find out which leaders were silly. He gave the men a lot of money to start working immediately. The two men set up empty frames and pretended to work very hard. Soon, the ruler sent his old minister to see how the product was coming along.

When the old man looked at the empty frame, he saw nothing at all, but he was too afraid to admit it. He did not want anyone to think he was silly or unable for his position. Soon after, the ruler visited the room himself with other leaders. They also saw nothing on the frames, yet they all praised the wonderful colors and patterns. Everyone agreed that the clothing was great and told the ruler they looked wonderful.

The ruler felt very pleased and decided to wear his new suit during the big town event. On the day of the event, the ruler walked through the streets wearing his hidden suit. All the people standing along the road shouted and pretended to admire his fine new clothing. Nobody wanted to look silly or stupid in front of their neighbors. Suddenly, a small child cried out that the ruler was wearing nothing at all.

The true words spread quickly through the crowd until everyone was whispering the secret. The red-faced ruler heard them and finally realized that he had been tricked. He knew the child was right, but he kept walking proudly until the end of the long street. He continued walking while people whispered behind him. He knew that he made a big mistake.

The men were already far away with the money. They left the town before anyone could catch them. The ruler felt sad inside his big home. He realized that pride is a very bad thing. From that day, he tried to be a better person.

Many people talked about the strange event for a long time. They remembered the small child who spoke the truth. Children played games in the streets and told the story to their friends. Nobody ever forgot the day the ruler walked without clothing. It became a famous lesson for everyone in the country.

The ruler often thought about his past mistakes. He changed his daily habits and tried to help the citizens. No longer did he care only about bright personal items. His new attitude brought peace to the land. The story remained in the hearts of everyone for generations.

People everywhere discussed the day of the hidden suit. Parents told the tale to their young daughters and sons. They wanted the children to grow up with truth. Pride could ruin anyone if it went too far. This simple truth became the best lesson of all.

Many years passed after that strange morning. The ruler lived a quiet life until his final day. He never forgot the lesson taught by the small child. The country grew strong through fair laws and good work. And so the memory of the silly ruler faded slowly into history.

Many other days passed by in the large town. The leaders talked about the strange event often. People learned that a good person must be real. No man wanted to make the same mistake again. Every single citizen remembered the child who spoke.

The country became peaceful and very safe. Citizens worked hard to build a strong community. The ruler helped every person who needed support. He knew that true power came from helping others. Life went on happily for all the people there.

### C1
In an era when a sovereign measured his worth solely by the sumptuousness of his garments, a vain monarch resided in his opulent palace. He cared for nothing in the world except adorning his person in fine textiles, squandering the treasury on daily attire. One fine morning, two cunning fraudsters arrived at the grand gates, posing as master weavers of peerless skill. They brazenly declared to the monarch that they could fabricate the most magnificent raiment imaginable. The impostors explained that the cloth possessed a miraculous property beyond mere beauty. It would remain completely invisible to anyone who was either hopelessly foolish or fundamentally unsuited for their official post. The sovereign found this proposition utterly brilliant, imagining the profound utility of such a garment. He reasoned that by wearing it, he could readily discern which of his ministers lacked competence. Without hesitation, he advanced a massive sum of gold to the scoundrels so they might commence their labor immediately. The two swindlers erected twin wooden looms, stringing them with absolute nothingness while pretending to labor with ferocious intensity. They demanded the finest silks and the purest gold thread, tucking the precious materials secretly into their own satchels while working late into the empty night. Soon, the sovereign felt an irrepressible surge of curiosity regarding the progress of the wondrous fabric. Wishing to assess the craftsmanship himself, he determined to dispatch his most trusted senior minister to the workshop. The venerable official ascended the stairs with a fluttering heart, pausing outside the chamber before gently opening the heavy oak door. When the old man peered intently at the vacant wooden frames, his breath caught in his throat because he saw nothing at all. He rubbed his rheumy eyes in panic, yet the empty air remained stubbornly bare before his trembling gaze. Terror gripped his soul, for he feared that admitting his blindness would brand him as a fool or an unworthy servant.

The crafty weavers looked up with feigned innocence, inviting the minister to step closer and admire the exquisite pattern and vibrant hues. They pointed dramatically at the empty frames, chattering endlessly about imaginary shades of azure and crimson. The poor old man broke out in a cold sweat, nodding vigorously while desperately squinting to catch a glimpse of the phantom textile. Not for the world would he confess his perceived deficiency to anyone. Returning to the monarch, the trembling minister praised the marvelous colors and intricate designs with trembling enthusiasm. He described the nonexistent fabric with such vivid conviction that the sovereign felt his heart swell with eager anticipation. Soon afterward, the monarch visited the workshop in person, accompanied by a retinue of proud advisors and high-ranking officials. They expected to behold a marvel, yet upon entering, they stared in utter bewilderment at the empty looms. Not a single soul among the assembled dignitaries could discern even a thread of cloth stretching across the wooden beams. Yet, remembering the terrible warning whispered by the weavers, every man kept his lips firmly sealed. They praised the magnificent colors and the dazzling patterns with unctuous smiles, urging the monarch to commission a full suit for the upcoming grand procession. Everyone agreed that the invisible attire was nothing short of breathtaking. The highly gratified sovereign beamed with pride, presenting the impostors with honorary titles and heavy purses of silver as a reward. On the eve of the festival, the swindlers burned midnight oil, pretending to stitch the final garment with microscopic needles. They finally informed the ruler that his celestial suit was complete, light as a spiderweb and comfortable as summer breeze. The monarch raised his arms, and the scoundrels pretended to drape the invisible trousers and coat over his bare shoulders.

On the morning of the grand town event, the sovereign stood before his mirror, turning this way and that to admire the phantom tailoring. His courtiers knelt on the marble floor, pretending to hold up the invisible hem of his magnificent robe so it would not drag. The monarch walked through the vaulted corridors with an exaggerated posture of supreme majesty, utterly convinced of his own splendor. All the palace guards bowed deeply as he passed, whispering words of breathless adoration to the empty air. The magnificent procession stepped out into the sunlit streets, where a massive crowd of eager citizens had gathered behind wooden barriers. Thousands of people craned their necks to catch a glimpse of the ruler in his legendary new suit. All along the processional route, spectators shouted and clapped, pretending to admire the fine tailoring and the dazzling colors. Nobody dared to voice the truth that was quietly dawning in their minds, for fear of being branded a fool by their neighbors. Whispers rippled through the front rows of the assembly, but each person quickly covered their hesitation with loud applause and cheerful cheering. The sovereign felt a warm glow of absolute triumph wash over him as he paraded beneath the fluttering banners. Suddenly, amidst the roaring applause, a small child perched upon his father shoulders cried out in a clear, ringing voice. The boy asked why the ruler was wearing nothing at all. A startled hush fell over the immediate vicinity like a heavy blanket dropping upon the festive square. The innocent words spread through the breathless crowd with astonishing velocity until a murmur of realization echoed from every corner. People began to exchange nervous glances, whispering the shocking secret from ear to ear until the truth became undeniable. The crimson-faced monarch heard the growing chatter and finally recognized the bitter reality of his profound humiliation.

He knew in his heart that the child was entirely correct, yet a stubborn surge of pride forbade him from showing weakness. Straightening his posture even further, he kept walking proudly down the center of the long street, pretending that everything was as it should be. The courtiers behind him continued to hold up imaginary trains, their faces burning with shared embarrassment and silent dread. The procession slowly reached its terminus, leaving the citizens to murmur behind his retreating figure. Meanwhile, the clever swindlers had already vanished from the town long before the parade reached the central square. They had loaded their heavy satchels onto fast horses and galloped toward the distant horizon under the cover of morning mist. By the time angry guards arrived at the workshop, only empty looms and discarded spools remained. The elusive makers were completely beyond the reach of justice. Locked within the sprawling chambers of his grand palace, the sovereign sat in heavy silence, confronting the wreckage of his own vanity. He realized with stinging clarity that pride had blinded him far more effectively than any trick could ever hope to achieve. The bitter taste of foolishness lingered in his mouth, replacing the arrogant certainty that had defined his youth. From that fateful day onward, he resolved to tear down the walls of his superficial existence and become a genuinely better person. For weeks on end, the inhabitants of the capital discussed the bizarre event with a mixture of amusement and lingering disbelief. They constantly retold the story of the brave little child whose unfiltered honesty had pierced the veil of collective delusion. Children transformed the tale into a game in the sunny streets, reenacting the procession with great theatrical flair. No citizen of the realm ever forgot the historic morning when the sovereign paraded through the capital clad in absolute transparency.

The sovereign underwent a profound internal transformation, systematically altering his daily habits and turning his attention to the welfare of his subjects. No longer did he spend his afternoons obsessing over bright textiles or squandering public funds on frivolous personal luxuries. Instead, he walked among ordinary farmers and merchants, listening to their grievances and ensuring that the laws were applied fairly. His newfound humility brought lasting peace and stability to the entire realm. Generations passed, yet the memory of the hidden suit remained deeply embedded in the cultural fabric of the nation. Parents routinely gathered their sons and daughters by the hearth fire to recount the timeless parable of human vanity. They taught their offspring that truth is infinitely more valuable than the desperate desire to fit in with the crowd. This simple yet profound lesson became the cornerstone of moral education throughout the country. As the years drifted by, the aging monarch lived a quiet, purposeful life dedicated entirely to the service of his people. He never once forgot the piercing words of the small child that had shattered his lifelong illusion. Under his wise and compassionate administration, the kingdom grew prosperous, safe, and united through honest labor. Ultimately, the memory of the once-vain ruler faded into gentle history, leaving behind a legacy of enduring wisdom. The town councilors frequently reflected upon the strange event during their seasonal assemblies, marveling at how a single moment of honesty changed a nation. They recognized that true leadership requires authenticity and the courage to acknowledge one's own limitations. No citizen wished to repeat the collective folly that had made them the laughingstock of neighboring lands. The memory of the child who spoke out remained a shining beacon of moral integrity for all.

Peace settled deeply over the fertile valleys and bustling market towns as neighbors worked cooperatively to strengthen their community. The sovereign, now beloved rather than feared, personally inspected public works and assisted anyone facing hardship. He understood at last that true power derives not from lavish appearances, but from lifting up those in need. Life moved forward harmoniously, bathed in the quiet dignity of a population anchored in reality. And so the kingdom flourished, untouched by vanity, forever guided by the honest voice of a child.

## st-0154 — The Elves and the Shoemaker
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, a man lives in a town. A woman lives with him. They work hard each day. They have no money for food. Times are very difficult now. The man has only one set. He cuts the parts before night. He leaves the set on a table. He wants to work in the morning. Next day, he sees the product. The set is all done. A customer buys them from him. The customer pays much money.

The man buys more for work. Night after night, this happens. The family become very glad. They get much money now. The two want to know someone. They want to find the small people. They stay behind a door at night. Two small people come into the room. The little people sit down. They start making things until morning. The two want to give thanks.

They make little things for them. They leave the things on the table. The small people see the things. They move away into the night. The work is all done. The small people are never seen again. The man and his woman live well.

### A2
Once upon a time, a poor man lived in a small town. He was a man who made shoes for people. He lived with his wife. They worked hard every day. However, they became very poor. Times were difficult for them. At last, the poor man had only enough material. He had material for one final set. He had material for only two items left.

He cut the pieces down before he went to sleep that evening. He left the material upon his table. He wanted to build everything in the morning. He went to sleep. When he opened his eyes early next day, he found the shoes finished well. The customer who bought them paid much money. The man could buy more material now.

Night after night, the same good thing kept happening while they slept. Help continued to arrive. The family began to grow. They lived happily together. The couple wanted to know who their friend was. They decided to stay up one night. They waited quietly behind a door.

Two small people entered the room. The little ones sat down immediately. They started working until morning light. The happy couple wanted to thank them well. They made little clothes and small shoes. They left these nice presents upon the table.

The people found the presents. The happy people saw the clothes. They danced away into the night. Their work was finally done. The small group were never seen again. However, the man and his wife lived well. They lived well for the rest of their lives. They had a good home and much food. They were very happy each day. Their life was full of quiet.

### B1
Once upon a time, a poor man lived in a small town with his wife. Although they worked very hard, they became extremely poor because times were difficult. The poor man had only enough paper and material left for one final pair of shoes. He cut the pieces out carefully before he went to sleep that evening. He left the material upon his table so that he could make everything in the morning. When he woke up early next day, he found the shoes finished perfectly. If the customer who bought them paid money, then the man could buy more stuff. Night after night, the same wonderful thing kept happening while they slept. Because this nice support continued, the family began to grow and live well. Strange to know who their secret friend was, the couple decided to hide one night. They waited quietly behind a door until two tiny small people entered the room.

The little people sat down immediately and started working busily until morning. To thank them properly, the good couple secretly made little clothes and tiny shoes. They left these nice gifts upon the table instead of material for the visitors to find. When the delighted visitors saw the clothes, they danced away into the night. Their work was finally done, and the tiny helpers were never seen again. However, the man and his wife have lived well for the rest of their lives. If they had not received that support, they would not have survived the cold winter. They always remembered the small friends who brought much joy into their home. Every single day, the couple felt good for the wonderful change in their situation. No one in the town ever forgot the strange story of the tiny visitors.

Many months passed by while the family enjoyed their new life. The man made many shoes every single week. People came from far away to buy his wonderful work. The business grew larger and larger each month. They never forgot the tiny people who came during the dark night. Their house became full of light and sound. They always kept the little shoes on a special board. Every visitor looked at the shoes with great wonder. The couple told the story to all their children. Thus, the memory stayed alive in the town. Nobody ever saw the small visitors again, but their gift remained in everyone heart.

### B2
Once upon a time, an honest maker of shoes lived in a small town with his wife. Although they worked very hard, they became extremely poor because times were difficult. Eventually, the poor man had only enough leather left for one final pair of shoes. He cut the pieces out carefully before he went to sleep that evening. He left the leather upon his wooden table so that he could make everything in the morning. When he woke up early next day, he found the shoes finished completely and perfectly. The customer who bought them paid so much money that the worker could buy more leather. Night after night, the same wonderful thing kept happening while they slept. Because this incredible help continued, the family began to grow and live happily. Curious to know who their secret friend was, the couple decided to hide one night. They waited quietly behind a door until two tiny people without shoes entered the room. The little creatures sat down immediately and started working busily until the sun came up. To thank them properly, the grateful couple secretly made little clothes and tiny shoes. They left these nice gifts upon the table instead of leather for the tiny people to find. When the delighted people saw the clothes, they moved away into the night. Their work was finally done, and the tiny supporters were never seen again. However, the maker of shoes and his wife lived well for the rest of their lives.
