### C1
In a winding cobblestone street of a quiet town, there once lived an honest shoemaker and his devoted wife. Although they labored from dawn until dusk with admirable diligence, relentless economic hardship reduced them to extreme poverty. Times were profoundly difficult, and every passing week stripped away another layer of their modest security. Eventually, the poor man found himself with barely enough quality leather left for one final pair of shoes. Desperation hung heavily in their small workshop, yet he resolved to maintain his professional pride until the very end.

With steady hands and a heavy heart, he measured and cut the remaining leather pieces out carefully before he went to sleep that evening. The task was solemn, reminding him of all that he and his wife stood to lose if their fortunes failed to turn. He left the prepared leather upon his wooden workbench so that he could assemble everything swiftly in the morning. The workshop grew cold as the embers in the hearth faded into grey ash, leaving the room wrapped in profound silence. They retired to their meager bed, praying silently for a miracle that neither dared to genuinely expect.

When he woke up early the next day, a strange sense of anticipation drew him immediately toward the workshop. Upon entering the dusky room, he rubbed his eyes in sheer disbelief at the transformation before him. He found the shoes finished completely and perfectly, every stitch executed with an elegance that surpassed his finest craftsmanship. Not a single thread was out of place, and the leather gleamed with a mysterious, captivating luster. He called for his wife in a hushed whisper, and together they stared at the miraculous artifact in absolute wonder.

Later that morning, a discerning customer entered the humble shop and noticed the exquisite footwear displayed in the window. Struck by the unparalleled quality of the construction, the buyer insisted on paying an extraordinarily generous price. The customer who bought them paid so much money that the astonished worker could immediately buy enough raw materials for two new pairs. The shoemaker hurried back home, his spirits lifted by a renewed hope that had eluded them for months. He carefully measured twice as much leather that evening, filled with an inexplicable sense of comfort.

Night after night, the same wonderful thing kept happening while they slept, defying all logic and reason. Every morning brought a new pair of masterfully crafted shoes, leaving the workbench pristine and empty of materials. The local townspeople soon began to talk, and customers lined up outside the door for a chance to purchase the miraculous creations. Because this incredible help continued week after week, the family began to grow more stable and live happily. The constant shadow of hunger lifted, replaced by the warm hearth fire of modest prosperity.

Curious to know who their secret benefactor was, the couple decided to hide one night and uncover the truth. They extinguished the primary lamp and waited quietly behind a heavy wooden door that offered a clear view of the bench. The hours stretched slowly as the grandfather clock ticked rhythmically against the stillness of the midnight house. Just as the clock struck midnight, a soft patter of footsteps echoed faintly across the wooden floorboards. They held their breath as two tiny people without shoes entered the room through a sliver of moonlight.

The little creatures were clad in ragged, threadbare garments that offered little protection against the midnight chill. Without a moment of hesitation, the little creatures sat down immediately at the workbench. Their nimble fingers started working busily, stitching, hammering, and polishing with astonishing speed and coordination. The shoemaker and his wife watched from their hiding place, entirely mesmerized by the sheer joy radiating from the tiny workers. The miniature artisans continued their labor without pause until the first pale fingers of dawn stretched across the sky.

As soon as the sun came up, the finished shoes stood neatly aligned upon the table, and the little figures vanished into the morning shadows. The shoemaker and his wife looked at each other, their hearts overflowing with profound gratitude for the help they had received. To thank them properly for saving their livelihood, the grateful couple decided to take immediate action. They spent the entire afternoon sewing and crafting with immense care and devotion. Secretly, the couple made little coats, tiny trousers, miniature waistcoats, and small, delicate shoes.

They wanted every garment to fit the tiny helpers perfectly, tailoring them with the finest cloth they could find. They left these nice gifts upon the table instead of leather for the tiny people to find before nightfall. Instead of raw materials to be shaped into commodities, the workbench now held tokens of pure appreciation. They stepped back into the shadows of their bedroom, eager to witness the reaction of their nocturnal guests. The workshop waited in breathless anticipation as the evening shadows lengthened once again.

When midnight arrived, the two tiny figures skipped into the room, expecting to find their usual piles of leather. Instead of work to be done, they discovered the exquisite miniature garments tailored specifically for them. When the delighted people saw the clothes, they gasped with pure joy and quickly tried them on with great excitement. They danced around the workshop, admiring their new attire and clapping their hands together in sheer delight. Singing a merry tune, they moved away into the night, stepping gracefully out the door and into the starlit street.

Their work was finally done, and the tiny supporters were never seen again by the mortal couple. Though the mysterious visitors did not return, the memory of their kindness remained deeply etched in the hearts of the household. The shoemaker continued his craft with his own hands, sustained by the skill and confidence he had regained during those miraculous months. However, the maker of shoes and his wife lived well for the rest of their lives, never forgetting the lesson of gratitude. The small shop remained a place of warmth, prosperity, and enduring wonder in the heart of the grateful town.

## st-0155 — How the Camel Got His Hump
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
At the start, a man has help. A man lives in a place. The place has no water. A big worker lives there. He does not want to work. He has very low power. He only says one word. He says, Humph.

The man has other help. One help is a big worker. Another help is a small friend. A new help takes things. These three friends are bad. They are not happy at all. They do the bad worker job. They feel bad all day long. The bad worker just sits down. He does not help them.

A big man hears them. He goes to the place. He speaks to the bad worker. He asks him about the work. The bad worker only says Humph again. The big man is not happy.

The big man gives him a change. He gives him a part on his back. He gives him a big part. The big part is on his body. Now he can work without much food. He can walk for a long time.

Workers carry things across the world. The part helps them in the place. Food is little there. The worker gets his right job. He learns to work every day.

### A2
At the very beginning of the world, animals worked for man. A large animal lived alone in a dry place. He did not want to do any work. When people asked him for help, he only said one word. He simply said humph. The other animal, the dog, and a friend grew very bad. They had to do the work of the slow animal. They also did their own daily tasks. Each animal felt less while watching the quiet animal rest. A great person was in charge of all dry places.

He heard their talk from far away. He went across the ground to speak with the animal. However, the animal only looked at him. He said humph once again. The great person became full of feeling with the big animal. He decided to give him a trouble. He gave the animal a big part upon his back. This new shape would help him work without food for days. Since that day, animals have carried their parts around. The part helps them live when food is less.

That is how the animal received his right load. Let us think about the story. The animal lived in a dry place. He did not want to move or help. He only said humph to everyone. The other animal and the dog were working hard. They worked hard every day. They felt bad about the situation. Then a great person came to see them. He wanted to fix the problem.

He spoke to the animal with care. The animal did not listen to him. He only said humph again. So the great person made a plan. He gave the animal a big part on his back. The animal was going to live with it all the time. Now animals carry this part all around. They can live through hard days. They do not need much food. The story shows how things change. Everyone must work together in the world.

### B1
At the very beginning of the world, animals had just begun to work for man. A strange animal lived alone in the hot land and refused to do any work. If anyone asked him to help, he only answered with one word. He always said, Humph, when people spoke to him. The horse, the dog, and the other animal grew very angry because of this behavior. They had to do the extra share of the work. They also had to do their own daily tasks. Each animal felt tired while watching the strange animal rest all day long. A powerful person who was in charge of all hot lands heard their complaint. He went across the ground to speak with the animal about his bad attitude. However, the difficult animal only looked at him and said Humph once again. The powerful person became angry and decided to give the slow creature a bad response. He gave the animal a great back part, a huge load, right upon his back. This strange new shape was given so that he could work for three days without food.

Since that day, animals have carried their loads everywhere they go across the world. The load helps them survive in the land when times are hard and food is low. And that is how the slow animal finally received his proper load. If you look at the animal today, you will see the big load on his back. He has carried this load for many years. The other animals have accepted him because he now carries his share. Life in the world became much better for everyone after that day. The horse and the other animal can now do their own work without extra trouble. The dog is also happy to help man with daily tasks. Every animal knows what will happen if they do not work. The powerful person made sure that all rules were clear to everyone. Therefore, no one tries to avoid work in the present time. The strange animal learned his lesson and never refused to help again. He stands in the hot land and carries his load with care.

Many people visit the land to see the animal and his big load. They always remember the story of how he received his bad response. It is a famous story that people tell their children. If you travel to that hot land, you will see him there. He will probably look at you and make a strange sound. Perhaps he will say Humph just like he did in the old days. The world has changed a lot since the beginning of time. Many things have come and gone over the long years. However, some things remain exactly the same as they were before. The animal and his load are part of this history. We can still learn important lessons from these old stories today. Good work brings peace and a good feeling to every group of living things. If everyone does their part, the world will be a better place for all. That is the true meaning of the story from the beginning of the world.

### B2
At the very beginning of the world, animals had just begun to work for man. A lazy person lived alone in the middle of a desert and refused to do any work at all. Whenever anyone asked him to help, he only answered with a bad word, saying simply, No! He was a lazy worker who did nothing while other people labored. The horse, the dog, and the farm animal grew very angry because of this behavior. They had to do the lazy worker share of the work as well as their own daily tasks. Each animal felt tired and sad while watching the lazy person rest all day long. They felt that the situation was very unfair to them. Every single morning brought new trouble for the hardworking group. They had to run across the hot sand and carry heavy loads. Meanwhile, the lazy individual stayed in the same place without moving a muscle. He did not care about the pain of his friends. He just wanted to sleep and eat good food all day long. This bad behavior made everyone feel miserable and hopeless. They knew that something must change very soon. The working group decided to speak about the problem together. They hoped that a solution would appear before the end of the week. Life in the desert was already difficult enough without this extra trouble. Every animal wished for a peaceful and quiet environment. They wanted to share the burden equally among all members of the group. However, the lazy worker always refused to listen to their advice. He only laughed at their words and turned his back on them. This made the situation even worse than before. They felt completely powerless against such a strange creature. The sun shone brightly upon the yellow sand every single afternoon. The heat was terrible and made everyone feel weak and tired. Yet the lazy worker continued to sit down and do nothing at all. He did not care about the rising temperature or the lack of water. His heart was as cold as ice toward his fellow creatures. They could not understand how anyone could be so lazy. The horse spoke to the dog about the terrible situation. The dog agreed that the lazy worker was a major problem. They decided to bring the matter before someone in charge. They hoped that a powerful leader would fix the issue for them. The desert was a huge place where many strange things happened. It was a world of sand and wind where life was never easy. Animals had to work together if they wanted to survive the harsh conditions. Only through hard work could they find enough food to eat. Therefore, the presence of the lazy worker was a danger to everyone. He took food from the common supply without giving anything back. This unfairness could not be allowed to continue much longer. The time had come for a major change in the desert community. They looked around for someone who could help them. They wanted a strong leader to settle the dispute once and for all. The sun began to set behind the high mountains in the distance. The sky turned red and orange as the evening approached. It was a quiet moment in the vast and empty desert. Yet the problem of the lazy worker remained unsolved. The animals went to sleep with heavy hearts and worried minds. They knew that tomorrow would bring new challenges for them. They hoped that help would arrive before the next morning. The night was long and cold across the open land. Stars filled the dark sky above the sleeping desert. Nothing moved except the gentle wind blowing over the sand. The lazy worker slept deeply without any worries on his mind. He did not care about the trouble he had caused. He dreamed of a life where no one would ever ask him to work. It was a selfish dream that ignored the needs of others. Morning came quickly with the bright light of the sun. A powerful Djinn who was in charge of all deserts heard their complaint. He went across the sand to speak with the lazy worker about his bad attitude. However, the difficult animal only looked at him and said No once again. The Djinn became very angry and decided to give the lazy creature a fitting lesson. He magically gave the person a great word, a huge part, right upon his back. This strange new shape was given so that he could work for three days without eating any food. Since that day, workers have carried their big loads everywhere they go across the world. The heavy load helps them survive in the desert when times are hard and food is difficult to find. And that is how the lazy person finally received his proper load. Everyone in the desert learned an important lesson about hard work and responsibility. They understood that every member of the community must do his part. The horse, the dog, and the farm animal were very happy with the final result. They returned to their daily tasks with a new sense of hope and joy. The lazy worker now had to carry his own heavy load across the desert. He could no longer rest all day while others did his share of the work. He finally understood why fairness was so important for everyone. Life in the desert became much better after that day. The animals lived together in peace and mutual respect. They worked hard and shared the responsibilities of daily life. And so the great story of the desert came to a happy end. People still talk about this famous event today. It remains a wonderful example of justice and hard work for everyone to remember.

### C1
At the very dawn of creation, when the world was freshly minted and the ink of existence still wet upon the page, animals had only just begun to labor under the command of man. In the desolate heart of a howling wilderness, a creature of singular indolence dwelt entirely alone. While the rest of the nascent world strained their muscles against the yoke of responsibility, this stubborn dweller refused to perform even the smallest scrap of labor. Whenever anyone approached to solicit his assistance or merely to offer a polite greeting, he responded with nothing more than a single, defiant syllable. To every entreaty, he uttered only a lazy and absolute refusal, content to let the universe pass him by while he remained utterly inert upon the shifting dunes.

He was a profoundly idle worker who contributed nothing to the collective endeavor while all around him others toiled under the blistering sun. Before long, the horse, the dog, and the heavy-set ox grew incandescent with resentment at such flagrant dereliction of duty. They found themselves unjustly burdened, forced to shoulder not only their own daily quotas of toil but also the hidden share belonging to the desert sluggard. Each loyal beast felt a profound weariness settle into his bones while watching the lazy creature recline in the shade hour after tedious hour. It was a situation so glaringly inequitable that it poisoned the morning air before the dew had even evaporated from the sand.

Every single sunrise ushered in a fresh wave of hardship for the hardworking contingent, who were driven across scorching expanses with heavy burdens strapped to their backs. Meanwhile, the lazy individual remained entirely stationary, refusing to disturb so much as a single grain of sand beneath his sprawling limbs. He manifested a complete and callous indifference toward the physical suffering of his peers, caring for little beyond the simple luxuries of sleep and sustenance. This egregious disregard for communal harmony plunged the community into a state of deep despondency and silent despair. They recognized that unless a swift remedy could be found, the fragile structure of their cooperative existence would inevitably fracture under the strain.

Driven to the brink of endurance, the working group convened an informal assembly beneath the blinding glare of the afternoon sky to debate their grievances. They earnestly hoped that a viable solution might materialize before the week had drawn to its weary close, sparing them further degradation. The desert was already a formidable adversary, a merciless expanse where survival demanded absolute unity and relentless vigilance from every living soul. Every animal secretly yearned for a tranquil sanctuary where the burdens of existence were distributed with fairness and mutual respect. Yet whenever they attempted to reason with the offender, he merely laughed mockingly and turned his broad back upon their earnest entreaties.

Such insolence compounded their misery, leaving them feeling profoundly powerless in the face of such stubborn recalcitrance. The sun beat down with relentless ferocity upon the golden dunes, driving temperatures to heights that sapped the very will to live from the weary travelers. Yet the lazy worker remained utterly indifferent to the thermal onslaught, his heart seemingly as cold and unyielding as the bedrock deep beneath the sand. The sheer incomprehensibility of his unwavering apathy baffled those who understood the harsh biological realities of their arid domain. How any living creature could embrace such absolute stagnation in a world so dependent on cooperation remained an insoluble mystery to them.

Seeking counsel in their mutual distress, the horse drew close to the dog to analyze the mounting crisis. The dog readily concurred that the lazy resident constituted an existential threat to the peace and prosperity of the entire community. Together, they resolved to escalate the matter to a higher authority, placing their hopes in someone possessing the power to enforce justice. They knew the desert was vast and unpredictable, a theatre of ancient forces where extraordinary interventions were known to occur. Only through coordinated appeal to a higher power could they hope to restore equilibrium to their fractured world.

Survival in this unforgiving crucible required every resident to contribute their utmost energy to the common storehouse. Because the idle dweller consumed resources without ever replenishing them, his continued presence represented a parasitic danger to all. Such flagrant injustice could no longer be tolerated without inviting total ruin upon their cooperative society. The time had arrived for a decisive reckoning that would settle the dispute once and for all and restore order to the shifting sands. With heavy hearts and anxious minds, the animals watched the sun descend behind the distant jagged peaks, painting the horizon in bruised shades of crimson and amber.

As twilight settled over the silent wilderness, the cool breath of evening offered a brief respite from the daytime heat, yet brought no comfort to their troubled thoughts. They retired to their resting places beneath a canopy of brilliant stars, acutely aware that tomorrow would demand the same exhausting labor. The night stretched endlessly across the open plains, a quiet expanse where only the nocturnal winds whispered secrets to the dunes. While the hardworking beasts tossed and turned with worry, the lazy worker slumbered deeply, untroubled by conscience or remorse. He dreamed exclusively of a world engineered for perpetual rest, blissfully ignorant of the storm gathering on the horizon.

Dawn broke with sudden brilliance, banishing the shadows and flooding the desert with an intense, unfiltered glare. At that very moment, a powerful Djinn who held dominion over all arid lands strode across the sand to investigate the growing unrest. He approached the lazy creature with purposeful steps, intending to call him to account for his disruptive behavior and uncooperative spirit. Yet when challenged, the stubborn animal merely cast a dismissive glance upward and repeated his favorite word of defiance. Stunned by such audacious insolence, the Djinn felt a surge of righteous indignation and resolved to administer a lesson that would endure through the ages.

With a sweeping gesture of magical authority, the Djinn bestowed upon the sluggard a magnificent and unexpected addition directly upon his spine. This extraordinary anatomical modification materialized as a great, swelling mound designed to store nourishment for extended periods. Through this ingenious curse, the lazy creature was granted the biological capacity to labor for three consecutive days without requiring a single morsel of food. Ever since that fateful morning, creatures burdened with this specific adaptation have carried their great loads across the continents with stoic endurance. The wondrous hump provides the exact sustenance required to navigate the most unforgiving terrain when provisions are scarce and distances immense.

Thus the lazy worker finally received his proper load, permanently altered to reflect the responsibilities he had long sought to evade. A profound sense of vindication rippled through the desert community as they witnessed the brilliant resolution to their protracted ordeal. The horse, the dog, and the ox resumed their daily duties with renewed vigor, comforted by the restoration of cosmic fairness. No longer could the recalcitrant dweller lounge in idleness while others bore the brunt of existence; he now carried his own destiny upon his back. Harmony was restored to the wilderness, leaving behind a timeless legend that generations would recount whenever discussions of duty and equity arose.

## st-0156 — Rip Van Winkle
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Rip Van Winkle is a good man. He lives near high land. He does not like hard work. His family is very bad to him. Rip walks up the forest with no company. One day, Rip walks high into the place. He hears a man call his name. A little man carries a big thing. The man shows Rip a way. Rip follows the little man.

Other men play a game. The men give Rip water. Rip feels less well. Rip falls into a long sleep. He comes up from the sleep. His hand tool is old and bad. His face look is long and white. He is with no company now. Rip walks back down the place. He sees new houses.

People do not know him. His home is without people. Rip goes to the town center. An old woman looks at him. She knows his sound. Twenty years pass by. The people take his story. Rip lives a good life now. He is a love story person. People take his story. He is good with friends.

### A2
Rip Van Winkle was a good man. He lived near big high land. He did not like hard work. His wife often got very mad at him. Rip would walk into the green forest. He went with his dog. He loved the green trees. He wanted to rest. He walked every day. He did not want to stay home. His wife always spoke to him with high words. Rip just listened and walked away. The forest was a good place for him. He felt free there. He took his time. He sat under the trees. He looked at the sky. The green leaves made him happy.

One afternoon, Rip walked high into the land. He heard someone call his name. He saw a little man. The man carried a large drink. The person showed Rip a way. Rip followed the man to an opening. Some other people played a game. They were very still. They stopped and looked at Rip. They gave him some drink. Rip drank and felt very low on energy. He lay down and fell into rest. He slept for a long time. When he opened his eyes, the day was bright. His old tool was bad. His hair on his face was long and white. His dog was gone. Rip did not know why.

Rip stood up and walked down. He went back to his town. He did not know the houses. Children laughed at his old clothes. His home was open and clear of people. Rip tried to talk to people. No one knew him. An old woman looked at him. She heard his voice. A person said twenty years passed. The town people listened to his story. Rip felt strange. He looked around the town. Everything was different. The streets had new houses. The people had new clothes. No one remembered his name. He felt alone in the world.

Rip lived a happy life. He became a big part of the town. People came to hear his story. He loved his new life. He sat outside his old home. Children came to listen to him. He told them about the small men. He told them about the long sleep. Everyone liked his story. He was an old man now. He had many friends in the town. His days were full of talk. He never had to work hard again. He smiled every day. His life was good until the end.

### B1
Rip Van Winkle was a friendly man who lived near high mountains. He did not like hard work on his farm. His wife was often very angry about this. Rip would go into the forest with his dog and gun. He enjoyed walking through the green trees and looking at nature. He spent many hours there because he loved the quiet place. If he went into the woods, his dog always followed him closely. Rip had never liked to work hard on the family property. He preferred to look at the sky and the tall trees. His wife told him that he did nothing useful every single day. She would get very angry when he came home late. Rip always tried to find new ways to avoid her big voice.

One afternoon, Rip walked higher into the mountains. He heard someone call his name from deep among the rocks. He saw a strange little man carrying a heavy drink. The man made signs for Rip to follow him. Rip followed the stranger to a dark place. A group of odd people played a game there. They were very quiet and only made sound with heavy balls. When they saw Rip, they stopped and stared. They gave some drink and told Rip to taste it. Rip had taken several drinks because he felt very dry. Soon he felt tired and fell into a very deep sleep. When he woke up, the sun was looking bright. His gun was old, and his beard was long and white. His faithful dog was missing from the area. Rip had slept for a very long time indeed. He could not understand what had happened to his favorite gun. The little men had disappeared into the dark mountain places. Rip moved his hand over his eyes because he felt completely confused. He stood up and looked around the strange place.

Rip stood up slowly and walked down the mountain. He reached his village, but did not recognize any houses. Children laughed at his long beard and old clothes. He went to his house, but it was empty. Rip tried to talk to people, but they spoke differently. An old woman looked at him and recognized his voice. A neighbor told the crowd that twenty years had passed. The people listened closely to his history. Rip had missed two full decades of his normal life. His wife had died many years before he returned home. His grown daughter welcomed him into her own house. The local residents gathered around to hear his strange report. They could not believe that a man had slept for so long. Rip told them about the strange little men in the hills.

Rip spent the rest of his life as a famous speaker. Visitors came to listen to his story about the little men. He lived a calm life with his family and friends. People from all around the country visited his new home. They wanted to hear the amazing report about the mountains. Rip always answered their questions with a big smile. He had become a well known person in the local town. His young family members loved to sit near him every evening. If anyone asked about the mountains, Rip shared his memories. He never forgot the hidden game played by the odd men. His quiet days passed quickly among people who cared for him. He finally found happiness after all those years of sleep.

### B2
Rip Van Winkle was a very friendly man who lived in a small village near high mountains. However, he was quite lazy. He did not like hard work on his own farm. This made his wife very angry every single day. To escape her loud voice, Rip would often wander up into the forest with his dog and gun. He enjoyed spending time walking through the green trees and looking at the natural world. He would spend hours just walking and looking at the trees. Sometimes he sat down on a rock because he felt tired from walking so far. He wanted to rest his body. The sun was bright above him. He liked the quiet air. Nobody was there to talk to him. His dog stayed close to him. They walked for a long time. The trees were green and tall. Birds were singing in the branches. Rip felt happy when he was away from his home. His wife would usually shout at him about the farm work. He did not care about the farm at all. He only cared about the forest and the quiet nature around him.

One afternoon, Rip walked higher into the mountains than he had ever gone before. While he was resting there, he suddenly heard someone call his name through the quiet air. He looked up and saw a strange little man in old clothes. The man was carrying a heavy drink. The little man did not speak to him. Instead, he motioned for Rip to follow him up a narrow path. Rip followed the stranger until they reached a dark opening in the rock. A group of odd people were playing a game there in the shadows. These strange men were very quiet as they played their game. The only sound was the heavy roll of the balls across the ground. When they saw Rip, they stopped their game and stared at him. Then they poured some drink and made signs for Rip to taste it. Rip looked at the strange people. They did not look like anyone from his village. Their clothes were old and strange. They moved very slowly. The game they played made a strange sound in the mountains. Rip felt a bit nervous. He did not know what they wanted from him. They just pointed at the cup and smiled.

Being in need of water, Rip took several drinks from the cup. He soon began to feel very tired. He lay down under a big tree and fell into a deep sleep which lasted for a long time. When he finally woke up, the sun was shining brightly in the sky. Everything around him had changed completely while he was sleeping. His old gun was bad, his beard had grown very long and white, and his dog was nowhere to be found. He tried to stand up. His legs felt weak and old. He looked around the mountain area. The trees looked different. He could not find his faithful dog anywhere. He called the dog's name, but nobody answered. Only the wind made a sound through the trees. He touched his face and felt a long beard. He could not understand what had happened to him. He thought he had slept for only one night. The world around him felt completely new and strange. He picked up his bad gun and started to walk. He wanted to find his way back to his village. His heart beat fast because he was so confused.

Confused by these strange things, Rip stood up slowly and began walking back down the mountain toward his home. When he reached his village, he did not recognize any of the houses or the people. The local children laughed at his long beard and old clothes. Nobody seemed to know who he was or where he came from. He went to his own house, but it was old, empty, and abandoned. The door was open and broken. Windows were dark and dirty. No smoke came from the chimney. His wife was not there. He walked through the familiar streets, but everything looked different. New houses were built where empty land used to be. People looked at him with strange eyes. They whispered to each other as he passed by. He felt like a stranger in his own town. He wanted to find a friend who could tell him the truth. He went toward the center of the village. A crowd began to gather around him. They pointed at his long white beard. They laughed at his old clothes. Rip felt more confused than ever before.

Rip walked to the village center and tried to talk to some people. Everyone spoke about different things which he could not understand. Finally, an old woman looked closely at his face and recognized him. An older neighbor stepped forward and told the crowd that twenty years had passed since Rip had walked up the mountain. The people were amazed by this tale and listened closely to every word he said. Rip could not believe the news. Twenty years had gone by while he slept in the mountains. His wife had passed away long ago. His friends were all old or gone. The world had changed its government and its ways. People talked about big events which he knew nothing about. He stood in the middle of the crowd and felt very small. An old man in the crowd shook his hand. They invited him to stay with them. They gave him food and a warm place to rest. They wanted to hear more about his long sleep. Rip realized that his quiet life in the mountains had changed his entire existence forever. He was ready to accept his new life in the village. He knew he could never go back to the past. He looked at the friendly faces around him.

Rip spent the rest of his life happily as the village old man. Whenever visitors came to the town, they would sit with him for hours. They would listen to the story about the strange little men in the mountains. He lived a quiet life and was loved by all his friends until the end of his days. Children loved to hear his amazing tale. They sat around him on the ground and asked many questions. Rip always answered them with a gentle smile. He told them about the quiet forest and the men playing their game. Nobody ever doubted his wonderful story. He became a famous part of the village community. People brought him food and drinks every day. He never had to worry about hard work on a farm again. His lazy days were finally rewarded with peace and quiet. He enjoyed the sun and the company of his new friends. The mountains looked down on the village every day. Rip would often look up at them and remember his big adventure. He was glad that he had wandered up that high path so long ago. His long sleep was a mystery which nobody could ever solve. He lived out his final years in peace and happiness. His name became a legend in the town.

### C1
Rip Van Winkle possessed a disposition of remarkable geniality, yet he harbored an aversion to labor that bordered on the artistic. While his neighbors toiled relentlessly upon their rocky acres, Rip neglected his own farm until the fences fell and the weeds choked the corn. This chronic neglect served as the primary source of exasperation for his wife, whose sharp tongue pursued him from dawn until dusk. Seeking refuge from her perpetual reprimands, Rip would routinely gather his trusty fowling piece and summon his faithful canine companion, Wolf. Together they would ascend the densely wooded slopes of the Kaatskill Mountains, where the canopy offered a sanctuary of absolute tranquility.

Hours would dissolve as Rip wandered beneath the towering elms and ancient oaks, inhaling the crisp, resinous air of the wilderness. Occasionally, fatigue would overtake him, compelling him to pause upon a mossy boulder to watch the lazy drift of distant summer clouds. No human voice broke the solitude save for the sporadic melody of thrushes and the rustle of leaves stirred by an idle breeze. In these quiet moments, miles away from the domestic tyranny of his homestead, Rip experienced a profound and unclouded sense of contentment. He cared nothing for the demands of agriculture, choosing instead to lose himself in the timeless rhythms of the natural world.

One sultry afternoon, driven by an impulse to explore, Rip scaled heights he had never previously ventured to investigate. As he rested in a secluded ravine, a distant voice suddenly pierced the quiet air, calling his name with an eerie resonance. Startled, he glanced upward to discover a peculiar short figure burdened by a heavy flagon, beckoning him onward without uttering a word. Compelled by a strange curiosity, Rip followed the silent guide along a narrow gorge until they emerged into a natural amphitheatre of sheer rock. There, bathed in deep shadow, a company of solemn men engaged in a silent game of ninepins, their movements strangely deliberate and devoid of mirth.

The only sound echoing through the stony recess was the thunderous roll of the wooden balls, resembling distant muffled thunder. Upon perceiving Rip, the mysterious bowlers suspended their recreation, fixing him with a collective gaze of profound, unnerving scrutiny. Without offering a greeting, one among them drew a portion of the liquor from a large vessel and gestured for the intruder to partake. Apprehension fluttered in Rip's chest as he observed their archaic garments, their heavy beards, and their motionless, statue-like expressions. Hesitant yet overwhelmed by thirst, he accepted the flagon and drank deeply, finding the beverage surprisingly refreshing.

Scarcely had the last drop crossed his lips than an irresistible heaviness invaded his limbs, clouding his waking consciousness. Seeking relief from the sudden fatigue, he stretched out beneath the shelter of a spreading tree and sank into a profound, dreamless slumber. When at last he pried his heavy eyelids open, the sun stood high in the firmament, suggesting that the day had advanced significantly. Rubbing his eyes, Rip attempted to rise, but his joints felt stiff and uncooperative, aching with the unfamiliar burden of extreme age. To his utter bewilderment, his reliable old musket had rusted away to a red-brown stock, and Wolf had vanished utterly into the brush.

Calling out for his dog produced only the mocking echo of the wind through branches that somehow appeared denser and more ancient. He raised a trembling hand to his jaw and encountered a beard of silver hair that descended halfway down his chest. Panic began to quicken his pulse as he tried to reconcile this physical transformation with the memory of a single night's rest. Determined to resolve the mystery, he retrieved the ruined skeleton of his firearm and commenced the arduous descent toward his village. Every step down the familiar mountain paths revealed an unfamiliar landscape, populated by trees and rock formations he could not place.

Approaching the outskirts of his settlement, Rip experienced a growing sense of alienation as he surveyed the altered architecture of the town. The rustic hamlet had expanded significantly, yet none of the houses matched the weathered cottages he had left behind just hours prior. Groups of urchins jeered at his disheveled appearance and long white beard, pointing fingers at him with carefree adolescent cruelty. Reaching his own dwelling, he discovered a roofless ruin with broken windows, abandoned to the elements and devoid of any human presence. His wife was nowhere to be found, and the heavy silence of the empty rooms confirmed his worst, unspoken anxieties.

Wandering back into the bustling central square, he found himself surrounded by unfamiliar faces who stared at him with undisguised curiosity. The sign above the village inn bore the portrait of a strange man in a uniform instead of the familiar visage of King George. People muttered among themselves, gesturing toward his antiquated clothing and questioning his sanity as he attempted to converse with them. Desperate for an anchor in this surreal reality, Rip demanded to know the whereabouts of his old acquaintances and drinking companions. The crowd grew silent, exchanging perplexed glances until an elderly woman pushed through the gathering to study his weathered features closely.

Recognizing the familiar bone structure beneath the tangled beard, she cried out that Rip Van Winkle had miraculously returned after two decades. A gray-haired neighbor stepped forward to corroborate the unbelievable declaration, explaining that twenty years had slipped away since his disappearance. Murmurs of astonishment rippled through the assembled townspeople as they realized they were standing in the presence of a living historical ghost. Rip learned that his sharp-tongued wife had passed away long ago, and his former friends had either died or scattered to distant places. Overwhelmed by the magnitude of these revelations, he stood motionless in the square, feeling like an absolute stranger in his own homeland.

Fortunately, the villagers embraced the prodigal wanderer with kindness, offering him food, shelter, and a patient ear for his extraordinary tale. An elderly resident who remembered his youth invited Rip to live in his household, ensuring that his remaining years would pass in comfort. Relieved of agricultural duties and domestic strife, Rip settled easily into his new role as the respected patriarch of the community. Whenever travelers visited the local inn, they invariably sought out the old man to hear the bizarre account of the silent mountain bowlers. No one ever doubted the veracity of his memories, and the mystery of his slumber became an accepted legend within the valley.

Rip spent his remaining days basking in the gentle warmth of the afternoon sun, surrounded by affectionate children who loved his stories. He often gazed upward at the majestic peaks of the Kaatskill range, recalling the shadowy ravine and the fateful draught of liquor. Though twenty years of his existence had vanished into the ether, he felt no bitterness regarding the immense loss. His lazy disposition, once a curse to his household, was finally rewarded with a tranquil retirement characterized by endless peace. Thus Rip Van Winkle lived out his twilight years in absolute serenity, his name permanently woven into the folklore of the mountains.

## st-0157 — Puss in Boots
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One day, a man does die. He leaves an animal to a young person. The young person is very sad and not happy. The animal asks for two things now. He wants something for his lower body parts. He wants a small case for things. The man gives him the foot things. The animal puts them on his body parts.

He brings food to the big ruler. He says the food is from a friend. The big ruler goes near the water. The animal tells his master to go down. The animal puts the man things away. The big ruler sees them and helps. The big ruler gives him new things. The animal runs to the land quick.

The big ruler drives on the way. He tells people to speak well. The big ruler sees big land. The animal goes to a great house. A person lives in the great house. The person can change his form. He becomes a small little animal. The smart animal wins over him very quick. The smart animal wins the great house.

The big ruler comes to the great house. The smart animal sees the big ruler. The big ruler gives his child away. The young man loves the child. They love each other every day. The smart animal lives a very good life.

### A2
Once upon a time, a poor man died. He left his young son only one animal. The young man felt very bad. He thought the animal was a small thing. The good animal looked at his leader. He asked for two things. He wanted fine clothes and a food container. The leader gave him those items. The animal put on the clothes fast. He began bringing food items to the president. He spoke a big name to the court. He said the items came from the leader.

One day, the president traveled near the water. The good animal told his leader to go. The animal took the man clothes. The local car arrived at the water. The animal said that the leader was bad. The president sent people to save him. The president also provided new clothes. The president drove along the road. The animal ran ahead to manage a plan. He told local workers to speak to the president. They said the land was for the leader. The president felt very happy.

The animal entered a great house. The house was for a major person. The bad owner could change his shape. The good animal asked if he could become a small animal. The bad man turned into a small animal. The animal caught him fast. He won the house for his leader. The president arrived at the great house. The happy animal welcomed the people outside. The president offered his daughter to the young man. The young man married the girl.

He became a major person. The good animal lived happily. He never needed to catch animals again. The animal was always there for his friend. They lived in the great house together. The young man was very glad. The president visited them many times. Everyone remembered the clever animal. Life was good for both of them.

### B1
Once upon a time, a poor man died. He left his young son nothing except a fast cat. At first, the young son felt sad. He thought this family gift was very small. However, the smart animal looked at his master. He asked for only two things. He wanted a pair of fine black shoes and a bag to catch food. The master gave him those items. The cat put on the shoes at once. Using good plans, the animal began bringing food gifts to the king. He presented a nice animal and spoke a high name. He always told the court that the gift came from his master, the Marquis of Carabas. The king received the gift with joy. Many people saw the kind gifts arriving at the great house. The court officers wondered who the sender could be. The cat felt proud of his work. He planned to bring more food the next day. The king wanted to meet the sender of these fine presents. He sent a message to the court about the matter.

One day, the cat learned that the king would travel near the river with his daughter. The fast cat told his master to swim in the water. The cat hid the man clothes. When the king transport arrived, the cat shouted. He shouted that the rich man was down in the water. The king at once sent guards to save the young man. The king provided him with wonderful clothes. The young man looked very good in the new clothes. The king smiled at the marquis and asked him to join the ride.

While the king drove along the road, the cat ran ahead to manage a plan. He commanded local workers in the fields. He told them to tell the king that the land belonged to the marquis. Workers told the king that the fields belonged to the marquis. The king felt very interested because the supposed man owned such large properties. The king looked around the fields with great interest. He thanked the marquis for having such fine land. Meanwhile, the cat entered a great house. That house belonged to a powerful, rich strong man.

The dangerous owner could change his shape into any animal that he wanted. The smart cat acted to feel doubt. He asked if the strong man could become a tiny mouse. The strong man changed into a small mouse. The fast cat jumped on him. He caught the little animal right away and won the fine house for his master. The cat felt very happy about this success. He waited for the king to arrive at the big house.

When the king arrived at the great house, the happy cat welcomed the guests outside. Delighted by the lovely marquis and his vast lands, the king offered his daughter in marriage. The young man married the lady and became a rich leader. As for the fast animal, he lived happily. He never needed to follow mice again except for his own fun.

### B2
Once upon a time, a poor man died. He left his young son nothing except a smart cat. At first, the young man felt very sad. He thought this gift was small. However, the smart animal looked at his master. He asked for only two things. He wanted a pair of fine leather boots and a bag to catch food. The master gave him those items. The cat put on the boots immediately. Using smart tricks, the animal began bringing food gifts to the king. Whenever he presented a nice animal to the head leader, he spoke a grand name. He always told the royal court that the gift came from his master, the Marquis of Carabas. The king received every present with great joy. The young man became famous throughout the land because of these gifts. People talked about the rich man who sent such fine things to the royal house. The cat worked hard every single day to build a strong reputation for his master. He wanted everyone to believe that the marquis owned vast lands and many valuable houses. The king often asked his ministers about this unknown man. Nobody knew where the marquis lived, but everyone respected him deeply. The cat smiled whenever he heard people discussing his master. His clever plan was working better than he had hoped.

One day, the cat learned that the king would travel near the river with the young woman. The smart animal told his master to swim in the water. The cat hid the man clothes while the master swam. When the royal vehicle arrived, the cat shouted loudly. He said that the rich marquis was falling into water. The king immediately sent guards to save the young man. The king provided him with wonderful clothes. The king looked at the young man and smiled warmly. The royal guards pulled the master out of the river very quickly. The young man wore the fine new clothes that the king provided. He looked like a true man standing by the water. The king invited him to ride in the royal car for the rest of the journey. The master felt very happy about this turn of events. He sat beside the king and talked about many different subjects. The young woman looked at him and smiled several times. She thought that he was a very charming person.

While the king drove along the road, the cat ran ahead to manage a smart plan. He commanded local workers in the fields to tell the king that the land belonged to the marquis. The ruler felt very impressed because the supposed person owned such large properties. Meanwhile, the cat entered a great castle that belonged to a powerful, rich giant. The dangerous owner could change his shape into any animal that he wanted. The smart cat pretended to feel doubt. He asked if the giant could become a tiny mouse. As soon as the silly giant turned into a small mouse, the quick cat jumped on him. He caught the little creature immediately. He won the great castle for his master. The giant had lived in that building for many years. He controlled all the surrounding fields and forests. When he changed into the tiny mouse, he made a big mistake. The cat had waited for this exact moment. He moved faster than the mouse could run. The giant was gone forever. The castle was now free for a new owner. The cat waited inside the great hall for the royal car to arrive.

When the king arrived at the great castle, the happy cat welcomed the royal guests outside. Delighted by the charming marquis and his vast lands, the king offered his daughter in marriage. The young man married the princess and became a rich person. As for the smart animal, he lived happily. He never needed to chase mice again except for his own fun. The wedding celebration lasted for many days. People came from all over the country to see the happy couple. The king gave them many gifts and wished them a long life together. The former poor man now ruled the region as a true man. He never forgot how much he owed to his faithful cat. The cat spent his days resting by the warm fire in the great hall. He caught a mouse sometimes just for fun. He never had to worry about food again. Everyone in the country remembered the story of the smart cat and the Marquis of Carabas. It became one of the most famous tales in the whole world.

### C1
Upon the melancholy dissolution of an impoverished miller, his estate proved meager indeed, leaving his youngest son nothing more than a seemingly unremarkable cat. At first, the young man succumbed to a heavy sorrow, believing this solitary creature was a pitiful inheritance incapable of sustaining a livelihood. However, the feline surveyed his despondent master with a remarkably astute gaze that suggested hidden depths of intellect. Quietly, the animal requested only two provisions to alter their shared destiny: a sturdy pair of custom leather boots and a sack suited for gathering sustenance. Though perplexed by these unusual demands, the master provided the items, and the cat laced the footwear with deliberate care.

Donning the boots immediately, the clever animal transformed himself into a dedicated provider through a series of calculated maneuvers. He ventured into the dense surrounding woods, cunningly trapping plump game and presenting the offerings directly to the sovereign as tribute. Whenever the animal delivered these fine specimens to the royal court, he announced them with a grand and fabricated title. He insisted that every single offering originated from his wealthy and distinguished master, whom he named the Marquis of Carabas. The king, delighted by the unexpected generosity, received each present with profound gratitude while the young master remained entirely unaware of his rising fame.

Throughout the realm, whispers began to circulate concerning the mysterious and generous aristocrat who showered the royal house with such exquisite gifts. The cat labored tirelessly each day, meticulously constructing a powerful reputation for his master to ensure universal admiration. He planted stories among the townsfolk, cultivating the illusion that the marquis possessed vast estates, magnificent manors, and immense wealth. Curiosity plagued the king, who frequently questioned his ministers regarding the identity of this elusive nobleman whom nobody had ever directly encountered. Hearing these discussions, the cat smiled with quiet satisfaction, knowing his intricate plan was unfolding with absolute precision.

One afternoon, the clever animal learned that the royal carriage would soon travel along the winding path beside the local river. Acting swiftly, the cat instructed his bewildered master to enter the water and pretend he was drowning. While the young man splashed near the riverbank, the faithful feline gathered his master's worn garments and concealed them beneath a large rock. Just as the royal vehicle drew near, the cat began to cry out with feigned panic that the noble marquis was slipping beneath the surface. Recognizing the esteemed title from the countless previous gifts, the king immediately commanded his guards to rescue the drowning man.

The royal guards pulled the master from the water with great haste, offering him dry shelter and garments crafted from the finest silks. Draped in the magnificent attire provided by the sovereign, the young man suddenly possessed the distinguished appearance of a true aristocrat. The king looked upon him with genuine warmth and invited the newly rescued marquis to join him inside the royal carriage for the remainder of the journey. Overwhelmed by this sudden twist of fortune, the master accepted the invitation and sat comfortably beside the monarch. As they conversed about various subjects, the king's daughter glanced sideways at the handsome stranger, smiling and finding him remarkably charming.

While the carriage rumbled along the dusty road, the ambitious cat raced ahead of the royal procession to execute the next phase of his grand design. Encountering local peasants laboring furiously in the sun-drenched fields, the cat issued a stern warning concerning what would happen if they disobeyed. He commanded them to inform the king that every acre of the surrounding land belonged exclusively to the Marquis of Carabas. When the royal vehicle eventually passed, the king inquired of the workers whose property they tended, and they repeated the exact phrase the cat had taught them. Deeply impressed by the sheer expanse of these prosperous lands, the monarch congratulated his traveling companion on possessing such a magnificent domain.

Meanwhile, the clever animal approached a towering stone fortress that belonged to an immensely wealthy and terrifying giant. This formidable master of the castle possessed the rare and dark ability to transform his physical form into any creature he desired. The cat requested an audience, entering the great hall with a calm demeanor despite the intimidating presence of the magical owner. Feigning respectful doubt, the feline asked whether the giant could truly assume the shape of something as small as a common house mouse. Boasting proudly, the foolish giant instantly vanished and reappeared as a tiny rodent scurrying across the stone floor.

The cat had anticipated this exact moment with calculated patience, watching the transformation unfold with predatory focus. Moving with astonishing speed, the feline leaped forward before the tiny mouse could comprehend the trap closing around him. He caught the creature instantly, ending the reign of the magical tyrant and securing the fortress for his master in a single decisive movement. The giant vanished forever, leaving the magnificent castle entirely unoccupied and ready for a new lineage of nobility. The victorious cat settled inside the grand hall, waiting peacefully for the arrival of the royal guests.

When the royal carriage finally rolled through the heavy iron gates, the clever animal stood proudly on the stone steps to welcome them. Bowing deeply, he announced the arrival of the estate in the name of his illustrious master, the Marquis of Carabas. Completely enchanted by the sprawling lands, the magnificent fortress, and the charming nobleman, the king made a generous proposal. He offered the hand of his beloved daughter in marriage to the young man, securing a royal future for the former miller's son. The wedding celebration that followed lasted for many days, drawing cheerful crowds from every corner of the prosperous kingdom.

The newly wedded couple lived in absolute splendor, while the former poor man finally understood the full value of his unusual inheritance. As for the faithful cat, he retired from his scheming ways, lounging comfortably by the hearth in the great hall whenever he desired warmth. He occasionally chased a mouse across the stone floor, though purely for his own amusement rather than out of any desperate necessity. The king showered the household with generous gifts, ensuring that peace and prosperity reigned over the region for decades to come. Long after the celebrations faded into memory, the tale of the clever feline and his master lived on as the most celebrated story in the land.

## st-0158 — The Three Questions
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A big man has three questions. He wants to know the right time. He wants to know important people. He wants to know important things. No one gives a good answer. He decides to visit an old man. He walks to the little home.

The man takes off his good things. He puts on old things. He walks to the little place. The old man works in the land. The big man asks his three questions. The old man keeps working without speaking. The big man takes something to work. The big man works in the land.

Many hours pass while he works. The day goes down. A bad man comes from the trees. The person holds a bad hand. The big man cleans the bad place. He puts something on it. The man stays down in a room.

The big man sits on the dirt. Morning comes to the world. The person looks at the big man. Water is in his eyes. He asks for life back. He is a bad person. He comes to end the life. The big man keeps his life.

The big man is very good. They make no war right now. He gives land back. He asks the old man again. The old man gives the answers. The most important time is now. The most important person is here. The most important thing is good.

### A2
Once upon a time, a great leader wanted three important things. He wanted to know the right time for every action. He wanted to know the most important people. He wanted to know the most important task. No one could give him a good answer. He decided to visit an old person in the high land. The man put on simple clothes and walked alone to the garden. The old person worked hard and did not speak. The leader saw that the old man was low on power. The leader took the hand tool and began working in the garden. He wanted to show good will to the old man. The old man watched him work for a long time. The leader did not stop his work. He kept moving his hands and digging the earth.

Hours passed while the leader worked. Soon the light went down behind the high land. A person came out from the trees. The man held his arm. The leader went over and cleaned the problem. He put something on it and helped the man lie down inside. The leader fell down on the floor. When he opened his eyes, the man looked at him. Water was in his eyes. The man asked for help. He was against the leader and came to kill him. The leader saved his life during the dark night. The stranger felt surprise when the leader helped him. The leader gave him food and water to drink. The man felt very weak from his hurt arm.

The leader gave help and saved his life. The leader was very happy to make good terms. He promised to give back all lands. Before leaving, the leader asked his three questions one last time. The old person explained the answers. The most important time is now. The most important person is the one you are with. The most important thing is to do that person good. The leader went home with a new mind. He understood many things after this long day. The experience changed his whole life. He planned to rule his country with care. He wanted to help every person in need. The old man smiled as the leader walked away.

### B1
Once upon a time, a powerful king wanted to know three important things. He wanted to know the right time to begin each action. He wanted to know who the most important people were. He wanted to know what the most important task was. Because no one could give him a good answer, he decided to visit an old man. The old man lived in a garden near the forest. Many people came to see him for advice. The king dressed in plain clothes and walked alone until he reached the garden. When the king asked his questions, the old man kept working without speaking. Seeing that the old man was tired, the king took the tool and continued working. Hours passed while the king worked, and the sun began to go down. Suddenly, a bad stranger came out of the woods with a hurt arm. The man was losing blood fast. The king rushed over, cleaned the wound, and covered it carefully. Tired from the hard labor, the king fell into bed on the floor. When he woke up, the stranger looked at him with deep sadness. The man explained that he was an enemy who had come to kill the king. The king was glad to make peace and promised to give back all lands. Before leaving, the king asked the old man one final time for the answers. The old man explained that the most important time is now. The person you are with is the most important person. The good thing to do is to help that person.

The king thought about these words for a long time. He understood that life is short and very valuable. Every day brings new chances to do good things for others. If you help someone today, you will feel happy. The king had learned a great lesson from the old man. He walked back to his big house with a calm mind. His heart was full of love for all people. From that day on, the king tried to live a better life. He always made sure to help anyone in need. If a problem came up, he stayed calm and thought carefully. He never forgot the important lesson he learned in the garden. The country grew strong and calm under his rule. People from far away came to hear his wonderful stories. They wanted to know how he became such a good leader. The king always told them about the old man in the garden. He told them that the present moment is all we really have. We must use our time well and always be kind to others. This is the secret to a happy and good life.

Many years passed after that special day in the garden. The king ruled his land with great care and constant attention. If anyone had a serious problem, the king listened to them directly. He walked among the citizens to see their daily lives. Children played in the streets while adults worked in shops. No one suffered from hunger because the king shared food freely. The army stayed strong, but peace remained the main goal. Visitors from foreign countries arrived to study the system. They could not believe how well the community operated. The king always shared his personal story with the guests. He told every new generation about the wise old man. If you remember to live in the present, you will find peace. The future depends on what we do right now. Therefore, the king kept his heart open to every person. He ensured that kindness guided every single action he took. His name became a symbol of hope and proper leadership. Even when difficult challenges appeared, he remained completely steady. The country thrived and remained secure through all seasons. Every single citizen felt safe and deeply respected. The king smiled as he watched his people live well. He knew that helping others brought true satisfaction. His final years were filled with quiet strength and dignity. When the king finally passed away, the whole nation mourned. They remembered his fair laws and his gentle nature. Statues were built to honor his memory in the capital. Children learned about his wonderful rule in school. The lesson of the garden lived on through the years. People understood that the present moment is truly everything. If we treat each other well, the world improves. This simple truth remained the greatest guide for everyone.

### B2
Once upon a time, a powerful king became convinced that he would never fail if he knew three important things. He wanted to know the right time to begin each action. He wanted to know who the most important people were. He wanted to know what the most important task was. Because no one at court could give him a good answer, he decided to visit a wise old person. This person lived simply in the hills. The wise person was known by many people as a man who understood the world. The king dressed in plain clothes and left his horse behind. He walked alone until he reached the small garden where the wise man worked every single day. When the king asked his three questions, the old man listened carefully. The man kept digging his earth without speaking a single word to the ruler. Seeing that the old person was tired, the king took the tool from him. The king continued working in the garden for a long time. Hours passed while the king dug the earth under the warm sun. Soon the sun began to sink behind the hills in the late afternoon. Suddenly, a hurt and exhausted stranger ran out of the woods. The stranger was holding his injured arm with both hands while dark blood ran down. The king rushed over to the man without any delay. The king cleaned the wound and wrapped it carefully with clean material. Then the king helped the injured person lie down on a bed inside the small room. Exhausted from the hard labor and the long day, the king fell down on the floor. He slept deeply on the floor of the simple house until the morning came.

When he woke up the next morning, the stranger was looking at him with deep sorrow and tears in his eyes. The man asked for pardon, explaining everything to the ruler. He explained that he was a bitter enemy who had come to kill the king during the journey. However, the king had saved his life with great care and support. The king was very glad to make peace with his former enemy. The king promised to give back all his lands and properties. Before leaving the small room, the king asked the wise man one final time for the answers to his questions. The wise old man explained that the most important time is now. He stated that the most important person is the one you are with at that moment. Finally, the wise man told the king that the most important thing is to do that person good. The king listened to these words and understood their deep meaning. He knew that his journey had been successful because he had learned valuable lessons about life. The ruler returned to his big house with a calm mind and a clear heart. He spent the rest of his days helping people and doing good things for everyone in his country. Whenever a problem came up, the king remembered the wise man words. He always focused on the present moment and the person standing right in front of him. Peace and happiness stayed in the land for many long years. The story of the king and the wise man was told from generation to generation across the whole nation. People learned that doing good is always the right path to follow. The simple lessons changed the heart of the powerful ruler forever. He never forgot the three important things that he discovered in the hills. The king walked through the garden again and thought about the events of the past days. He felt that his mind was clear and his future was bright. Every person in the country began to respect the ruler much more than before. The king shared his story with his closest friends and family members. They agreed that helping others was the greatest purpose a person could ever have. Life in the country became peaceful and happy for all citizens. The king continued to rule with wisdom and fairness until the end of his days. No more enemies came to trouble his peaceful lands. The memory of the wise man remained in the heart of the king forever.

### C1
Once upon a time, a powerful king became entirely convinced that he would never fail if he knew three vital things. He wanted to know the exact right time to begin each action, and who the most important people truly were. Furthermore, he desperately wanted to discover what the most important task was in any given moment. Because no one at court could give him a satisfactory answer, he decided to visit a wise old person. This reclusive hermit lived simply in the hills, far away from the noise and flattery of the royal palace.

The wise person was widely known across the kingdom as a man who truly understood the fundamental nature of the world. Determined to remain unrecognized, the king dressed himself in plain clothes and left his royal horse far behind. He walked alone through winding mountain paths until he finally reached the small garden where the wise man worked every single day. When the monarch arrived and asked his three pressing questions, the old man listened with profound and patient attention. However, the hermit kept digging the dark earth without speaking a single word in response to the ruler.

Noticing that the old person was visibly frail and exhausted, the king decided to intervene out of genuine concern. He gently stepped forward and took the heavy iron tool from the trembling hands of the hermit. The king continued working in the garden for a long time, turning the stubborn soil under the increasingly warm sun. Hours passed slowly while the royal visitor dug the earth, sweat pouring down his forehead in the late afternoon heat. Soon the sun began to sink slowly behind the green hills as evening approached with a cool breeze.

Suddenly, a severely wounded and exhausted stranger burst out of the dense surrounding woods with a desperate cry. The fugitive was holding his heavily injured arm tightly with both hands while dark blood ran down his sleeve. Without a moment of hesitation, the king rushed over to the fallen man and knelt in the dirt beside him. The compassionate ruler quickly cleaned the jagged wound and wrapped it carefully with clean linen material from his own tunic. Then, with the help of the hermit, the king supported the injured person and led him inside the small room to rest.

Exhausted from the heavy physical labor and the emotional strain of the long day, the king collapsed on the wooden floor. He fell into a deep and dreamless sleep right there on the hard floor of the simple house until the morning light arrived. When he finally woke up the next morning, the stranger was staring straight at him with deep sorrow and tears shining in his eyes. The recovering man asked for immediate pardon, deciding to confess his true identity and dark intentions to the surprised ruler. He explained that he was a sworn enemy who had vowed to kill the king during this very journey across the mountains.

The assassin revealed that he had waited all day in the woods, expecting the king to return alone along the open path. When the hours passed and the king failed to appear, the desperate man had left his hiding place to search for him. Instead of finding an easy target, he had stumbled into the royal guards who recognized his face and wounded him severely. However, despite knowing nothing about his identity, the king had saved his life with incredible care and generous medical support. The astonished king was very glad to make complete peace with his former enemy, realizing the strange turn of fate.

Showing immense grace, the king promised to give back all the lands and properties that had been confiscated from the man's family. He sent his guards away and treated the former conspirator not as a prisoner, but as a valued and honored guest. Before leaving the small room to begin his journey home, the king asked the wise man one final time for the answers to his questions. The wise old man looked up calmly and explained that the most important time in the world is always the present moment, which is now. He stated clearly that the most important person is invariably the one you are actually with at that precise moment.

Finally, the wise hermit told the king that the most important task of all is simply to do that person good. The king listened carefully to these simple words and understood their profound and lasting meaning in his soul. He knew that his difficult journey had been completely successful because he had learned the most valuable lessons about human existence. The ruler returned to his grand house with a remarkably calm mind and a clear, unburdened heart. He spent the rest of his remaining days helping vulnerable people and doing good things for everyone across his entire country.

Whenever a difficult problem or political conflict came up, the king remembered the wise man words and paused to reflect. He always focused intently on the present moment and the specific person standing right in front of him. Peace and genuine happiness stayed in the land for many long and prosperous years under his thoughtful guidance. The inspiring story of the king and the wise man was told from generation to generation across the whole nation with great admiration. Simple citizens quickly learned that doing good for others is always the single right path to follow in life.

These simple lessons changed the heart of the powerful ruler forever, erasing his old arrogance and thirst for endless conquest. He never forgot the three important things that he had discovered through hard experience away from his palace. The king often walked through the palace gardens again, remembering the events of those transformative days in the hills. He felt that his mind was remarkably clear and that his kingdom's future was brighter than it had ever been before. Every single person in the country began to respect the wise ruler much more deeply than they had in the past.

The king shared his incredible story with his closest friends and family members during quiet evening gatherings. They all agreed unanimously that helping others selflessly was the greatest purpose a human being could ever hope to have. Life in the country became wonderfully peaceful and happy for all citizens, rich and poor alike. The king continued to rule with extraordinary wisdom and complete fairness until the very end of his days on earth. No more enemies ever came to trouble his peaceful lands, because kindness had built a wall stronger than any stone fortress.
