## st-0159 — The Oak and the Reeds
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One big plant grows near water. The plant is very strong. Many small plants live by it. The big plant looks down. It looks down on them. The small plants are very low. They are little and small. They live near the water. The big plant has a body. The body is very strong. It is a big plant. It stands by the water. The small plants stay close. They look at the big plant. The big plant feels very good. It stands up every day. The water gives it life.

One day a strong wind comes. The big plant stands up. It tries to stay up. The little plants do not try. They do not stand up. The wind comes from far away. It moves with much power. The wind moves the little plants. They go low to the land. They move easily with everything. The big plant stays up. It stands strong. It has a strong body. It does not go down. The big plant is very much. It thinks it is the best. It does not move at all.

The wind grows more. It is very strong now. The big plant cannot hold. It cannot hold the land. A sound comes from it. The big plant breaks. It falls down on the land. The wind stops now. The little plants stand up. They stand up again. They are very good. They go down every time. They do not break. They live on the land. The big plant is gone now. The little plants remain there. They live by the water.

### A2
Once upon a time, a big tree grew near a large water. The tree was very strong and full of its size. Many small plants lived along the water close to the large tree. While the small plants were little, the tree looked down on them. The tree felt very good about itself every single day. It did not care about the little plants at all. The light came down, and the big tree grew more and more. It thought nothing could ever bring it down to the ground. The other plants stayed still and lived their own simple lives day by day. They knew the world was large and full of different things. They did not try to grow as high as the big tree. They were happy to be small and live near the water.

One day, a very strong air came across the land. The big tree stood firm and tried to fight the force. However, the little plants did not fight the air at all. They moved easily with the air and avoided any damage. When the bad wind hit the plants, they went low. They went low down to the ground. The big tree did not go down and faced the wind alone. It thought its big size was able to protect itself. As the wind grew, the pressure on the tree increased fast. The poor tree could no longer hold its position at all. Suddenly, a big sound was heard all over the area. The huge tree broke from its place and fell down.

When the wind stopped, the little plants stood up again. They were well because they learned how to go low. Going low in a bad wind is better than fighting against force. This true story was shown by the fall of the big tree. The other plants saw what happened to the strong tree. They knew they made the right choice by moving with the air. Life went back to normal near the large water after that day. The light came out, and everything was fine once more. The little plants continued to grow in their own simple way. They would always remember the day the big tree fell down. It was a matter for every plant in the land. They would never forget to stay low when the wind moved hard.

### B1
Once upon a time, a huge tree grew near a large river. The tree was very strong and happy with its size. Many small plants lived along the water right near the massive tree. While the small plants were thin and low, the big tree looked down on them with high pleasure. Many people have seen such trees. They always stand high above everything else. The small plants have always lived quietly near the water. They have never made any trouble for anyone. Every single day passed in the same way for them. The sun came down, and the water flowed past their roots. They were simple plants in a big world. Nobody paid much attention to them.

One day, a very strong wind came across the land. The heavy air moved with great power against everything in its path. The happy tree stood firm and tried to keep the force of the storm away. However, the little plants did not fight the wind at all. They have never fought the wind during bad weather. If the wind comes, they move easily with the air. This helps them stay safe from any serious damage. The storm was very bad and covered the whole area in dark water drops. Animals ran to find a safe place to hide. Birds flew away from the big trees. Everything felt the power of the natural world.

When the great storm hit, the small plants moved low down to the ground. They moved easily with the air and avoided any serious damage. The huge tree, which refused to move, faced the full power of the wind alone. It trusted its big size and strong body to protect itself from the bad weather. Many trees have fallen because they did not move. If you stand too firm, bad things might happen to you. The big tree did not know this simple truth. It thought its body was too heavy to fall. It did not move when the wind blew hard against its branches. The other plants watched from the ground below.

As the wind grew more strong, the pressure on the tree increased. The poor tree could no longer hold its position against such force. Suddenly, a big sound was heard throughout the area. The massive tree broke from its roots and fell down into the earth. Many people have heard such heavy sounds during a storm. If a storm is strong enough, big trees will always fall. The tree had no chance against the power of the wind. Leaves flew everywhere in the dark air. Branches broke into many small pieces upon the ground. The great tree was gone in a single moment.

When the storm finally stopped, the little plants stood up straight again near the water. They were safe because they had learned how to move instead of fighting. Moving in a storm is often better than fighting against strong forces. This simple truth was shown clearly by the end of the happy tree. They have survived because they know what to do. If you are smart, you will also learn from this story. Life is sometimes hard, but you can find a way to live. The water continued to flow past the green banks. New days would come for those who could move and grow. Everything was quiet once again in the calm place.

### B2
Once upon a time, a huge tree grew near a large river. The tree was very strong and proud of its size. Many small plants lived along the water right beside the giant tree. While the small plants were thin and low, the big tree looked down on them with deep pride. The big tree felt that it was better than everything else around the water. It believed that its great size would protect it from any bad weather. Every single day, the tree stood proudly in the warm sun. It did not care about the small plants that lived near its roots. The small plants were very quiet and calm. They knew that the world was full of different forces. Sometimes, the weather could change very quickly. People who live near the river always watch the sky for signs of trouble. The giant tree just laughed at the thought of any danger. It thought that nothing could ever hurt its massive form. The strong plant stood completely still day after day. It never learned how to move with the air. Instead, it trusted only its own power. This pride would soon become a major problem for the large plant. A storm was coming across the country. The air would soon move with great power against everything in its path. The tree grew tall and felt very safe in its position. It had stood there for many years without any trouble. Birds often built nests in its wide branches. Children played beneath its shade during the hot summer months. The tree thought that its life would always remain the same. It never imagined that a day of trial could arrive. The small plants watched the giant with quiet attention. They understood the nature of the river and the wind. They knew that flexibility was important for survival. The big tree ignored all such wisdom. It relied only on its thick trunk and heavy roots. The sky above began to darken as the afternoon progressed. Dark clouds gathered quickly across the horizon. Animals ran back to their homes to hide from the coming rain. The river water rose slightly against the banks. A strange silence fell over the entire area before the wind started. Then the first strong gusts of air hit the region. The battle between the trees and the wind was about to begin. Everyone who lived nearby stayed indoors to watch the event. The air pushed harder against the landscape. Nothing could stop the force of the growing storm. The giant tree prepared to fight the incoming pressure with all its might. It did not know that its strength would be tested to the absolute limit.

One day, a very strong wind came across the land. The heavy air moved with great power against everything in its path. The proud tree stood firmly and tried to resist the force of the storm. However, the little plants did not fight the wind at all. When the bad storm hit, the plants bent low down to the ground. They moved easily with the air and avoided any serious damage. The giant tree, which refused to bend, faced the full power of the wind alone. It trusted its big size and strong body to protect itself from the bad weather. As the wind grew more violent, the pressure on the tree increased. The poor tree could no longer hold its position against such force. Suddenly, a loud sound was heard throughout the area. The massive tree broke from its roots and fell down into the ground. When the storm finally stopped, the little plants stood up straight again near the water. They were safe because they had learned how to bend instead of resisting. Bending in a storm is often better than fighting against strong forces. This simple truth was shown clearly by the end of the proud tree. Many people came to see what had happened to the giant plant. They looked at the broken pieces on the ground with great surprise. The little plants were still standing safely by the river. They did not suffer any damage during the terrible weather. Everyone who saw the scene understood the important lesson. If the big tree had been more flexible, it would have survived the storm. Instead, it chose to fight the wind and lost everything. The story of the tree and the plants remains famous today. People often talk about this event when they face difficult problems in life. They remember that being too proud can lead to a bad end. It is always better to be flexible when life brings different changes. We should learn from the small plants and the giant tree. Success often comes to those who know how to adapt. The river continues to flow past the same spot where the plants live. Everything is calm again after the great storm has passed. The birds returned to the area after the weather improved. They looked for new places to build their homes. The small plants continued to grow tall and green in the sunlight. They whispered stories to each other about the great wind. The fallen giant lay on the ground like a bridge across the mud. Time passed, and nature slowly covered the remains of the tree. New grass grew over the spot where the roots used to be. Visitors still walked along the river bank to look at the site. Guides told the story of the proud plant to every listener. The lesson was passed down from generation to generation. Children learned that stubbornness often brings ruin. Adults remembered the tale when they faced hard times in their own careers. The river kept moving forward day after day. Nothing could stop the flow of the water. The world changed around the riverbank, but the basic truth remained. Flexibility became the main rule for anyone who wanted to survive harsh conditions. The small plants lived happily for many more years by the stream. Their quiet existence proved that softness can conquer strength. And so the river flows on forever past the green banks.

### C1
Once upon a time, beside the winding current of a broad river, there stood an oak tree of immense proportions. Its massive trunk and sprawling canopy commanded the landscape, filling the ancient plant with a deep and unshakeable pride. Clustered along the damp riverbanks right beneath the giant, numerous slender reeds lived their quiet lives. While these small plants were thin and low to the ground, the great tree looked down upon them with absolute disdain. It was thoroughly convinced that its superior size made it far better than anything else sharing the shoreline. Day after day, the majestic oak basked in the warm sunlight, entirely unconcerned with the modest vegetation rooted near its base. The reeds remained remarkably calm, acutely aware that the world was governed by volatile and unpredictable forces. They understood that the weather could shift in a single afternoon, turning a peaceful valley into a chaotic landscape. Locals living near the water constantly watched the distant sky for the first signs of atmospheric trouble. By contrast, the giant tree simply laughed at the mere thought of facing any genuine danger. It firmly believed that nothing in nature could ever damage its thick bark or disturb its deep roots. The sturdy plant stood completely rigid through every season, never once bothering to learn how to sway with the passing breeze. Instead of cultivating adaptability, it trusted solely in its own formidable mass and rigid structure. Little did the proud giant know, this stubborn confidence would soon transform into a catastrophic vulnerability. A massive storm was gathering far across the country, destined to test every living thing in its path. The oak grew ever taller, reveling in the absolute security of its long-established position by the water. Having stood undisturbed for decades, it assumed its peaceful existence would continue unchanged forever. Colorful birds frequently nested among its expansive branches, while local children sought refuge beneath its shade during scorching summer months. Never once did the tree imagine that a day of severe trial could possibly arrive to challenge its supremacy. The reeds watched the giant with quiet vigilance, possessing a profound understanding of the river and the wind.

They knew intuitively that ultimate survival depended entirely upon flexibility rather than brute resistance. The magnificent tree, however, ignored all such silent wisdom, relying exclusively on its heavy trunk and stubborn roots. As the afternoon progressed, the sky overhead darkened dramatically as thick, slate-grey clouds accumulated along the horizon. Forest animals retreated swiftly to their subterranean homes, seeking immediate shelter from the impending tempest. The river water rose slightly against the muddy banks, reacting to the sudden drop in atmospheric pressure. An eerie, breathless silence descended over the entire valley just moments before the first gale arrived. Then, violent gusts of wind swept across the region, signaling the commencement of a relentless aerial battle. Everyone living nearby prudently remained indoors, peering through glass windows to witness the unfolding drama. The rushing air pushed with relentless fury against the landscape, sweeping away loose debris in its path. No earthly obstacle seemed capable of halting the raw momentum of the gathering tempest. The giant oak braced itself to absorb the incoming pressure with every ounce of its considerable might. It remained entirely oblivious to the fact that its legendary strength was about to be stretched to the breaking point. Before long, a ferocious gale struck the land, slamming heavy air against every stationary object in the vicinity. The proud tree stood its ground resolutely, attempting to overpower the unstoppable force of the storm through sheer stubbornness. The fragile reeds, by contrast, offered no resistance whatsoever to the howling wind. When the severe squall finally hit, those small plants bowed gracefully all the way down to the wet earth. They moved in effortless harmony with the moving air, thereby avoiding any structural damage whatsoever. Meanwhile, the towering oak refused to bend an inch, facing the full kinetic fury of the storm entirely alone. It placed its ultimate faith in its massive girth and unyielding wood to shield it from the elements. As the wind intensified minute by minute, the mechanical pressure bearing down upon the tree multiplied exponentially.

The unfortunate giant could no longer maintain its footing against such an overwhelming lateral force. Suddenly, a deafening crack echoed across the river valley, shattering the roar of the gale. The massive tree was violently ripped from its subterranean anchor point and crashed heavily into the mud. When the storm finally subsided and the clouds parted, the little reeds stood up straight again near the water. They remained completely unharmed simply because they had mastered the art of bending rather than fighting. This simple yet profound truth was demonstrated vividly by the tragic fate of the fallen monarch. Curious onlookers soon gathered along the riverbank to inspect the ruined giant with unconcealed astonishment. The modest reeds still stood beside the flowing water, entirely free from any structural blemishes or broken stems. Everyone who observed the striking scene instantly grasped the essential lesson embedded in the wreckage. If the magnificent oak had possessed the wisdom to be flexible, it would have survived. Instead, it chose to wage a futile war against the wind and lost everything in the encounter. The famous tale of the stubborn tree and the adaptable reeds has been retold across many generations. People frequently invoke this dramatic event when confronting difficult obstacles in their personal or professional lives. They recall that excessive pride invariably leads to ruinous outcomes when rigid systems collide with change. Embracing flexibility during turbulent transitions remains the wisest course of action for any human being. We would all do well to learn the enduring lesson taught by the reeds and the fallen oak. True success often favors those who possess the intelligence to adapt when circumstances shift around them. The river continues its steady flow past the exact spot where the resilient plants make their home. Complete calm has returned to the landscape now that the great weather disturbance has completely passed. Birds have reappeared in the recovering territory, searching for fresh locations to establish their nests.

The small plants continue to thrive, growing tall and vibrant under the warm afternoon sun. They appear to whisper ancient secrets to one another whenever a gentle breeze ripples the water's surface. The ruined giant lies flat across the muddy bank, resembling a makeshift bridge over the shallows. As time marches forward, nature is slowly reclaiming the remains of the tree with moss and ivy. Fresh blades of green grass have already begun to sprout directly above the cavernous hole left by the roots. Curious visitors still walk along the traditional footpath to inspect the historic site of the collapse. Local guides eagerly narrate the cautionary tale of the proud plant to every attentive listener who stops. This vital lesson has been faithfully transmitted downward through an unbroken chain of local families. Children learn early in life that stubbornness usually invites disaster rather than safeguarding stability. Adults frequently reflect upon the narrative when navigating harsh economic or personal hardships. Meanwhile, the river maintains its relentless forward motion day after day, indifferent to human affairs. Nothing in the physical world can halt the eternal journey of the flowing water toward the sea. The surrounding world transforms continually, yet this fundamental ecological truth remains eternally constant and reliable. Developing adaptability has become the primary rule for anyone hoping to endure harsh environmental conditions. The modest reeds will likely live happily for many more years beside the peaceful stream. Their quiet and unassuming existence demonstrates conclusively that softness can ultimately outlast rigid strength. And so the river flows onward forever past the green banks, carrying its timeless story to the distance.

## st-0160 — The Frogs Who Wished for a King
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Little small animals live in a water place. They have everything they need. They do not feel good. They want a leader. They want a boss. They ask high powers for help. The powers put a big wood down. The wood makes a big sound. All small animals feel very bad.

Small animals stay under the water. One small animal goes close. The wood does not move. The small animal calls friends back. Small animals see the boss is still. Little small animals play on the wood. They think the boss is bad. They ask for another leader.

A water animal comes down. The animal takes small animals every day. Small animals are in great trouble. They know good care is good. They want a change now. They stay in the water. The animal comes again and again. They feel very unhappy every day. They look up for help. No help comes to them.

### A2
Long ago, little people lived together in a good water place. They had everything. They were not happy. These people wanted a leader to rule over them. They felt life without someone was too simple. They wanted a change. So, the people sent a message to the high powers. They asked for someone to become their leader. The powers heard them. They dropped big tree into the water. The big tree fell. It made a huge sound. At first, all the people were very worried. They stayed under the water from the big object. After some time, one good person moved closer. He saw the tree did not move at all. Then he called his friends to come back. Soon, the people realized their new leader was boring. Some little people went on top of the tree. They felt such a bad ruler was not right. They sent another message to change their leader. This time, a fast water animal was sent down. The animal was very fast. It began to catch people. Now the poor people were in great trouble. They finally understood good life was much better.

The people learned a big thing. Life without a leader was good. They did not need any more changes. They were going to stay together. They were going to be happy. The water place was good again. Every person was right. They did not want another leader. They were going to play every day. They were going to enjoy their time. The fast animal was gone. The big tree was still there. Nobody went near the tree. The people knew better now. They were going to live in quiet. The high powers did not send anyone else. The community was very strong. They had a good system. They did not want any trouble. Life was simple and nice. They were going to keep it that way. The story of the leader came to an end. Everyone was happy. They were going to remember this day. The water place was their true home.

### B1
Long ago, a group of little animals lived together in a quiet water place. They had everything that they could ever need. However, they were not happy. These little animals wanted to have a king. They wanted a king who would rule over them. They wanted someone who could give them order. They felt that life without a leader was too simple. Life was also too plain for them. So, the group decided to send a message. They sent it to the high powers of the world. They asked for someone who could become their king. They also asked for someone who could bring great change. The powers heard the request from the little group. They decided to drop a big piece of wood. They dropped it right into the water. When the heavy wood fell, it made a huge sound. This sound made every single animal feel afraid. At first, all the little animals were very afraid. They were afraid of this strange new object. They hid under the water. They watched the quiet wood from a safe distance. After some time, one strong animal swam closer. He wanted to check the situation. When he saw that the wood did not move. It did not move at all. He called his friends to come back.

Soon, the animals realized something important. They realized that their new king was completely quiet. They also saw that he was boring. Some little animals even jumped on top of the wood. They played games without any fear. They felt that such a weak ruler was not right. He was not right for their great community. Therefore, they sent another request. They wanted to change their leader for a better one. This time, a fast water animal was sent down. He was sent down to be their new king. The new king was very active. He began to catch the little animals day after day. Now the poor animals were in great danger. They had no place to hide. They finally understood the truth. They understood that quiet freedom was much better. It was better than a dangerous master. Every single member learned a hard lesson. They learned about leadership and peace.

### B2
Long ago, a group of little residents lived together in a quiet water place. They had everything that they could ever need, but they were not happy. These residents wanted to have a king who would rule over them and give them order. They thought that life without a leader was too simple and plain. They thought about this situation every single day. They desired someone who might change their world completely. A leader could bring great power to the water place. Many individuals believed that their society lacked a proper direction. Therefore, they decided to act upon this serious matter. They prepared a formal request for the high powers of the world.

So, the residents sent a message to the high powers of the world. They asked for someone who could become their king and bring great change to the water place. The powers heard the request and decided to drop a big piece of wood right into the water. When the heavy wood fell, it made a huge sound that scared every single animal. At first, all the residents were very afraid of this strange new object. They hid under the water and watched the quiet wood from a safe distance. After some time, one strong individual swam closer to check the situation. When he saw that the wood did not move at all, he called his friends to come back. They discussed the strange event among themselves. Nobody understood why the king remained completely still.

Soon, the residents realized that their new king was completely quiet and boring. Some little residents even jumped on top of the wood and played games without any fear. They thought that such a weak ruler was not right for their great community. Therefore, they sent another request to change their leader for a better one. They hoped that the high powers would listen to their voice again. A community needs a strong figure who can make important decisions. This wooden object could not provide any real help or protection. Children and adults grew tired of the boring ruler. They wanted someone who could move and speak. The situation required a different approach immediately. Many members thought that the wood was just a terrible mistake. They spoke about the issue during every single meeting. No one could find a good reason to keep the wooden object. They wanted the high powers to know their true minds.

This time, a fast water animal was sent down to be their new king. The animal was very active and began to catch the residents day after day. Now the poor residents were in great danger and had no place to hide. They finally understood that quiet freedom was much better than a dangerous master. Every single member of the community thought great regret. They realized that their previous life was actually wonderful. The fast hunter moved quickly through the water. Many friends were lost because of this terrible choice. They tried to find a way to escape the danger. However, there was no safe place left in the world. They wished that they had never asked for a new ruler. The fast animal was too strong for the little residents. They had to hide every single minute of the day. Life in the water place became a very dark dream for everyone involved.

### C1
Long ago, an extensive community of frogs inhabited a tranquil, reed-fringed marshland where the water mirrored the vast expanse of the open sky. They possessed everything required for a comfortable existence, yet a profound and restless dissatisfaction gnawed persistently at their collective consciousness. These amphibians convinced themselves that they desperately needed a sovereign ruler to impose order and govern their daily affairs. To their minds, an unstructured life without an authoritative figure appeared altogether too simple and plain. Day after day, they obsessed over this perceived lack of leadership, convinced that a king would completely transform their humble world.

Many influential voices within the society argued that their culture suffered profoundly from a complete absence of proper direction. They truly believed that external power and majestic pomp could elevate their simple pond to a position of great significance. Driven by this ambition, they decided to act decisively upon a matter that had consumed their thoughts for months. A formal petition was carefully drafted, expressing their eager desire for a monarch who could command respect across the entire marsh. With high hopes, they prepared to dispatch their earnest request upward to the governing powers of the universe.

Having finalized their petition, the residents sent their urgent message outward into the wider cosmos, pleading for a royal guardian. They asked for someone powerful enough to alter the quiet rhythm of the water place and bring grand purpose to their lives. The governing powers heard the appeal and decided to grant the foolish request in a rather dramatic and unexpected fashion. Without warning, a massive, heavy log was dropped from above, plunging directly into the center of the peaceful pond. When the gigantic timber struck the water, it generated a tremendous shockwave and a deafening roar that terrified every living creature.

At first, all the residents panicked, fleeing wildly from the strange, looming object that had invaded their sanctuary. They dived deep beneath the surface, hiding among the tangled roots and watching the silent wooden intruder from a cautious distance. As hours passed and nothing catastrophic occurred, a particularly brave frog finally swam a bit closer to investigate the situation. Observing that the large piece of wood remained entirely motionless, he felt a surge of courage and beckoned his trembling friends to return. They gathered around the mysterious object, whispering among themselves and trying to comprehend why their new king refused to move.

Soon, the harsh reality settled over the community: their magnificent new ruler was completely quiet, unresponsive, and profoundly boring. Before long, the younger frogs lost all their initial fear and began treating the floating timber as nothing more than a convenient playground. They hopped boldly onto its rough surface, staging diving contests and playing noisy games without a shred of lingering respect. Such a weak and passive ruler, they decided, was entirely inappropriate for a community with such grand aspirations. Convinced of this failure, they resolved to send yet another petition upward, demanding a monarch who possessed actual vitality.

They earnestly hoped that the high powers would listen to their grievances once more and rectify an embarrassing mistake. After all, a proper society required a commanding figure capable of making decisive choices rather than a silent piece of timber. The wooden object provided no protection whatsoever against the daily trials of marshland life, frustrating both adults and children alike. Weary of a ruler who could neither speak nor act, the community felt that the situation demanded an immediate and drastic remedy. During every evening assembly, members voiced their deep dissatisfaction, arguing that the first choice had been a terrible blunder.

No one could find a single valid reason to tolerate the passive log any longer, and they were determined to make their true feelings known. They implored the governing powers to send someone dynamic, demanding a ruler who could truly command the domain with authority. This time, the powers granted their wish by dispatching a swift, agile water snake to serve as their royal master. Unlike the wooden log, the new king was intensely active from the very moment he arrived in the murky depths. Instead of offering wise guidance, however, the sleek predator began hunting and consuming the residents day after day without mercy.

Panic swept through the reedy shallows as the poor frogs realized they were trapped in a nightmare of relentless danger. Every corner of the marsh offered no sanctuary, and they found themselves with nowhere safe to hide from the snapping jaws. Too late, they finally understood that their previous quiet freedom was infinitely preferable to a powerful and ruthless master. A bitter wave of regret washed over every single member of the community as they contemplated the paradise they had carelessly thrown away. They looked back on their simple, peaceful past and recognized that it had been wonderful beyond measure.

The fast hunter darted through the water with terrifying speed, turning the once-joyful pond into a zone of constant terror. Countless friends and neighbors were lost to the sharp teeth of the tyrant because of one reckless and foolish demand. Desperate to survive, the remaining frogs scrambled frantically through the reeds, searching for any possible escape route from the slaughter. To their horror, they discovered that there was no safe place left anywhere within their familiar world. They wished with all their hearts that they had never dared to ask for a ruler to change their destiny.

The agile predator proved simply too formidable for the fragile residents, dominating the ecosystem with absolute cruelty. Every minute of the day now required agonizing vigilance and desperate hiding just to draw another breath. Life in the water place had transformed into a grim fable where peaceful existence was traded for a cruel illusion of grandeur. As the shadows lengthened across the troubled water, the survivors could only mourn the quiet independence they had once taken for granted. Their ambition had betrayed them, leaving them at the mercy of a king they had begged the universe to provide.

## st-0161 — Belling the Cat
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago, little animals live in a house. They have one big problem. A big black animal lives there. The black animal always wants the little animals. When little animals leave home, the big animal gets them. Life is hard. The little animals hold a meeting. They need a plan for home. Many little animals talk during the evening. Some suggest staying close. A young animal has an idea. Put a little ring on the black animal. Then the little animals hear her coming. The ring makes a sound. Every little animal can run away. All little animals are very well. The plan seems good. An old animal asks a question. Who puts the ring on the black animal?

No sound comes in the room. No little animal takes a try. Going to the big black animal means no life. Talking is easy. Doing it is hard. The little animals still have the big problem. They stay in the room and think. No one moves. Everyone waits. The black animal walks near. The little animals look at each other. Time passes by. They need another plan. No one knows what to do. The big black animal waits outside. The little animals look around. They want a good answer. They want to be free. They want a right place. The little animals sit and wait. They talk more about the problem. Each animal thinks about the animal. The animal is always near the house. The small animals feel very bad. They need help now. Someone must find a way. They look at the old animal. The old animal moves his head. No one has another good idea. The big problem is still there. The little animals stay together. They hope for a new day.

### A2
Long ago, a group of small animals lived in a large old house. They had one major problem every single day. A big black animal lived in the same building. She was always following them. When the small animals left home for food, she would catch them. Life became difficult. The small animals decided to hold a meeting. They needed a plan against this big risk. Many small animals stood up to discuss the situation. Some suggested staying deeper inside the walls, but that did not work.

Finally, a young animal stepped forward with an idea. He talked about a plan to put a small ring around the big black animal. Then they would hear her coming. When the animal came near, the ring would make a sound. Every small animal could run away quickly. All the other small animals were very happy with this proposal. They were happy because the plan seemed simple.

However, an older animal asked an important question. He wanted to know who would place the ring on the big black animal. Quiet fell across the room. Not a single small animal wanted to take such a risk. They knew that getting close to the big animal meant death. Talking about a good solution was much easier than doing it.

The small animals stayed in the house and tried to think of more ways. They talked for hours during the night. No one could find a better answer. The big black animal was still there. They had to be very close watchers every day. Life went on like this for a long time. They always watched the door. They never felt fine in the large house.

### B1
Long ago, a group of mice lived in a large old house. They had one major problem. This problem caused them constant fear every single day. A big black cat lived in the same building. She was always hunting them. At any time the mice left their home to find food, the cat would catch them. Life became extremely difficult. The mice decided to hold a meeting. They needed a plan to protect themselves from this terrible danger. Many mice stood up to discuss the situation during the long evening. Some suggested hiding deeper inside the walls. That did not solve anything. Finally, a young mouse stepped forward with an interesting idea. He explained that if they put a small metal object around the cat, they would hear her. If the animal came near, the object would make a sound. Then every mouse could run away quickly before the danger arrived. All the other mice were very happy when they heard this wonderful proposal. They felt very glad because the plan seemed so simple and effective. However, an older mouse stood up and asked a very important question. He wanted to know which person would actually place the object on the cat. Silence fell across the room immediately as everyone thought about the problem. Not a single mouse wanted to take such a huge risk. They all knew that approaching the big cat meant certain death. In the end, talking about a good solution was much easier than doing it. Every mouse went back to hiding in the dark walls. The big cat continued to live in the house without any trouble. The mice learned that having a great idea is only the first step. Doing the actual work is much harder than speaking about it. They still must be careful every single day.

Many days passed since that meeting. The mice have tried to find other ways to stay safe. They have looked into every corner of the dark walls. They have talked about the cat again and again. Yet nobody has found a better answer. The big cat has remained in the same old building. She has continued to hunt them without any pity. The little mice have understood that fear is a heavy load. They have realized that words are easy to share. Actions are much harder to perform. Every single mouse has learned a hard lesson. They have accepted their difficult life in the house. They have stayed quiet when the sun goes down. They have watched the doors and windows with great care. The danger has never gone away. The black cat has stayed close to their home. The mice have kept themselves hidden in the dark. They have lived with constant worry in their hearts. They have known that only real action could save them. Since nobody has taken the risk, the problem has remained. The story has shown us a simple truth about life. Good plans are wonderful to make. People have often spoken about great ideas. However, strong people are truly hard to find. The mice have accepted their end in the old house.

Time has continued to move forward in the house. The mice have grown older and better. They have shared stories with the young mice. They have told them about the famous meeting. The young mice have listened with wide eyes. They have understood the danger of the black cat. They have also learned about the metal object. They have known that the plan was good. They have realized why nobody placed it on the cat. Fear has kept them all inside the walls. The house has remained a place of constant worry. Life has gone on day after day. The cats have come and gone over the years. New cats have replaced the old ones. The danger has always been present for the little animals. They have always needed to watch their steps. They have never forgotten the hard lesson. Talking has never solved a real problem. Working together has required true strength. Until someone takes the risk, nothing will change. The mice have lived by these words. They have stayed safe inside their dark home. They have faced their fears with quiet strength. The big cat has still lived up. The story has ended right where it began.

### B2
Long ago, a group of mice lived in a large old house. They had one major problem that caused them constant fear every single day. A big black cat lived in the same building, and she was always hunting them. Whenever the mice left their home to find food, the cat would catch them. Life became extremely difficult, so the mice decided to hold a meeting. They needed a plan to protect themselves from this terrible danger. Many mice stood up to discuss the situation during the long evening. Some suggested hiding deeper inside the walls, but that did not solve anything. Finally, a smart young mouse stepped forward with an interesting idea. He explained that if they put a small bell around the cat, they would hear her coming. Whenever the animal came near, the bell would ring loudly. Then every mouse could run away quickly before the danger arrived.

All the other mice were very happy when they heard this wonderful proposal. They shouted loudly because the plan seemed so simple and effective. However, an older mouse stood up and asked a very important question. He wanted to know which person would actually place the bell on the cat. Silence fell across the room immediately as everyone thought about the problem. Not a single mouse wanted to take such a huge risk. They all knew that approaching the big cat meant certain death. In the end, talking about a good solution was much easier than doing it. The mice continued to live in fear because no one could solve the difficulty. They realized that a plan without someone to complete it had no real value. Every single day brought the same trouble, and the black cat remained nearby. The little animals stayed inside their safe holes and waited for hours.

Sometimes they looked out through small gaps in the old wooden walls. They saw the large animal walking slowly across the cold floor. Whenever she appeared, the mice felt their hearts beat fast with bad pain. They understood that their lives depended entirely on staying hidden away. No matter how much they wished for freedom, the dangerous situation did not change. The smart young mouse sat quietly in the corner and thought about the failure. He knew that having a good idea was only the first small step. Turning that idea into reality required a level of strength they lacked. The other members of the group looked at him with sad expressions. They agreed that talking about protection was very different from achieving it. Weeks passed by, and the old house remained a place of constant anxiety.

The black cat continued her daily hunt without any break at all. Mice learned to live with the bad reality of their daily existence. They never found a way to place that small metal bell around the animal. Thus, the famous problem remained open for all the following months and years. Every generation of mice heard the story of the bell from their parents. They understood the lesson well and avoided taking bad risks in the dark. Good knowledge told them that impossible goals only led to certain destruction. Therefore, they chose the path of care and remained safely out of sight. The big cat lived out her days in peace while the mice stayed hidden. Nothing changed between them, and the old house kept its ancient secret. That story became a well-known warning for all young mice in the building. Whenever danger approached, they remembered the lesson and stayed very quiet. Action was indeed much harder than words, exactly as the old mice had taught.

### C1
In the decaying belly of a grand old house that time had long forgotten, a fragile community of mice struggled to survive. Generations had nested behind the rotting wainscoting, carving out a meager existence from crumbs dropped by careless humans. Yet, beneath the fragile comfort of their hidden tunnels lay a constant, suffocating dread that poisoned every waking hour. A sleek, midnight-black cat had claimed the sprawling building as her personal hunting ground, ruling every corridor with silent tyranny. Whenever the desperate creatures ventured beyond their tiny thresholds in search of sustenance, the feline stalked them from the shadows.

Life had devolved into a harrowing gauntlet where a single misstep meant a swift and terrifying end. Starvation nipped at their heels, forcing them to weigh the agony of hunger against the lethal certainty of the open floorboards. Realizing that their current state of disorganized terror would soon lead to total annihilation, the elders called for a general assembly. From every dusty nook and cranny behind the walls, the frightened rodents crept out to gather in a hollow beneath the grand staircase. The evening air hung heavy with anxiety as scores of anxious whispers echoed through the gloom, searching frantically for a way out.

Many voices rose during that protracted council, each offering desperate suggestions born of panic rather than strategy. Some argued that burrowing deeper into the stone foundations would keep them safe from the claws above. Others suggested retreating to the upper attic rafters, completely ignoring the fact that no food could be found in those barren heights. None of these desperate measures offered a genuine solution to the relentless predator patrolling their hallways. As frustration threatened to dissolve the gathering into hopeless chaos, a young mouse stepped boldly into the center of the ring.

He was known among his peers for a quick mind and a restless curiosity that often bordered on the reckless. With a steady gaze that commanded the attention of the murmuring crowd, he presented an ingenious proposal. He argued that their primary disadvantage was not a lack of hiding places, but rather their inability to detect the cat's approach. If they could somehow fasten a small, resonant brass bell around the neck of the merciless beast, everything would change. Whenever the shadow crept near, the clear chime would ring out as an undeniable warning of impending doom.

That acoustic shield would grant them precious seconds to scurry back into the safety of their narrow tunnels. No longer would the black cat be able to ambush them in the silent dark without warning. As the sheer brilliance of the concept washed over the assembly, a wave of collective euphoria swept through the crowd. Cheers erupted from the dark corners, and tiny paws clapped in joyous anticipation of a liberated future. The simple elegance of the bell seemed to promise an immediate end to their long tyranny of fear.

Not a single soul doubted the efficacy of the mechanism; the logic was as pristine as it was comforting. Then, from the back of the chamber, an aged mouse with scarred flanks and clouded eyes stood up slowly. He raised a single paw to quiet the exuberant celebration, casting a heavy, sobering shadow over the excited gathering. With the quiet authority of years, he posed a single, devastating question that pierced the heart of their optimism. He asked softly who among them possessed the courage to approach the beast and fasten the bell around her neck.

An instantaneous and suffocating silence descended upon the hollow, banishing the warmth of hope in a fraction of a second. Every mouse looked down at the dusty floor, suddenly intensely aware of the fragile beat of their own terrified hearts. The realization dawned with crushing weight that inventing a brilliant defense was entirely different from executing it. Approaching the sleeping predator meant walking willingly into the jaws of certain death with no hope of escape. Not one volunteer stepped forward from the stunned crowd to claim the task of heroism.

The bitter truth settled over the assembly like a shroud, revealing the hollow nature of their clever plan. To conceive a solution required only imagination, but to enact it demanded a sacrifice far beyond their collective courage. Slowly, the assembly dissolved into quiet resignation as the mice slunk back to their respective hiding places in the walls. The smart young mouse sat alone in the fading light, acutely aware of the distance between theory and reality. He understood now that a brilliant design without an executioner was nothing more than an empty dream.

Days bled into weeks, and the old house resumed its familiar, agonizing rhythm beneath the heavy footsteps of the cat. The black hunter continued her ruthless patrol of the wooden corridors, undisturbed by the ambitions of her prey. Mice learned to endure their precarious existence, accepting that survival required eternal vigilance rather than miraculous inventions. They never found a way to attach that small metal bell, leaving the famous problem entirely unresolved. Generations yet unborn would inherit the story, passing down the moral warning from parent to child in the dark.

Whenever the floorboards creaked overhead, young mice were taught to freeze instantly and listen with absolute concentration. They learned that wishful thinking and grand strategies were useless without the strength and means to carry them out. Prudence dictated that they remain hidden behind the wainscoting rather than chase impossible ideals. The big cat lived out her natural days in peaceful supremacy over the domain she claimed. Nothing changed between the hunters and the hunted, preserving the ancient balance of the old house.

Thus, the legendary tale of the bell became a timeless cautionary fable whispered in the damp shadows. Action remained infinitely harder than speech, just as the weathered elders had always maintained. The mice stayed safe within their narrow confines, content to let the world outside belong to the terrifying beast. Wisdom, they discovered, lay not in devising grand schemes that could never be fulfilled. True wisdom lay in recognizing one's limitations and surviving in a world full of insurmountable dangers.

## st-0162 — The Fox and the Stork
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, a big dog lives near water. The dog is big. He is not kind. He wants food. He wants to play. He comes to the bird. He makes food. The food is on a low plate. The dog eat the food. The bird has a long head. She cannot eat the food. The dog look and play. The dog is not good.

The bird say thank you. The bird leave the house. Days pass. The bird want to play. The bird make food. The dog come to her house. The dog want food. The bird put food in a high room. The room has a small door. The bird eat the food easily. The dog cannot eat anything. The dog cannot reach the food.

The dog feel bad. The dog is not happy. The dog know the rule. The dog learn a good thing. He will be kind. He will not play bad games. The days pass by. The bird and the big dog live now. They know each other well. The world is big. They find water every day. The day is high. They walk around the water. The bird look at the dog. The dog look at the bird. They do not play bad now. They want to be good friends. The bird make a little sound. The dog stay with her. He feels very happy. He stop bad games. The house is still. The room is fine. They stay in the day. Life is good for both.

### A2
Once upon a time, a boy animal lived near a green forest. The animal lived near clear water. A friend also lived there. The animal was very clever, but he was sometimes bad. One fine morning, the animal decided to ask the friend. He wanted to share food at his home. He wanted to play a little game on his long friend. The friend was very happy to receive the nice message. She arrived at the house right on time. The animal served good food on an open plate. The animal could easily eat every drop of food. However, the poor friend could only touch the end. She touched the end of her long face. The animal laughed while the poor friend tried to eat. The nice friend did not show bad feeling. She said thank you to the animal for the food. She left her home before dark. She walked away from the house with an easy mind. The animal stayed behind and felt very happy. He felt happy about his little game.

A few days later, the friend decided to ask the animal. She asked the animal to her home by the water. The animal gladly accepted the message to eat. He was going to eat a great lunch. When the animal arrived, food was already ready. The food smelled very good. The friend served the good food inside a long jar. The container had a very small top. Because her face was long, the friend reached down. She reached down easily into the container. The animal could only touch the outside. He touched the outside of the jar. The animal realized that he had lost the game. He felt very small and said sorry. He said sorry for his past behavior. He understood that people should treat others well. They should treat others the way they want. He will always remember this day. The friend and the animal became good friends. They became good friends after that time. They play together by the water every single day. They share food and help each other. They help each other when they need support.

### B1
Once upon a time, a fast animal and a long bird lived in a large green forest. They lived near a clear blue river. The fast animal was known to be very good. Sometimes he could be quite bad to other animals. One fine morning, the fast animal decided that he would ask the long bird. He wanted her to share a meal at his home. He wanted to play a little game on his friend. He thought it might be funny. The long bird was very happy to receive such a nice message. She arrived at the home right on time. When they sat down at the table, the fast animal served a nice food. He put the food in a flat plate. Because the plate was completely flat, the fast animal could easily eat every drop. He ate all of his food. However, the poor long bird could only touch the end of her long mouth. She touched the surface of the plate. The fast animal laughed quietly. He watched the little long bird struggle with her meal. The good long bird did not show her mind. She thanked the fast animal before she left. A few days later, the long bird decided that she would ask the fast animal. She asked him to dinner by the river. The fast animal happily accepted the message. He was expecting to eat a wonderful meal and laugh again. When the fast animal arrived, dinner was already prepared. It smelled absolutely wonderful. The long bird served the nice meal inside a tall glass bottle. The bottle had a very narrow neck. Because her mouth was long and thin, the long bird could easily reach down. She reached down into the glass bottle. Meanwhile, the fast animal could only touch the outside of the glass. He was unable to get any food. The fast animal realized then that he had lost at his own game. The long bird won the game. He felt very bad and said sorry for his earlier behavior. He understood that people should treat others the way they want to be treated.

The forest was large and full of green trees and cool water. Animals lived together near the clear blue river every single day. The sun was bright upon the tall trees and the green ground. Sometimes the animals played games together in the afternoon. They shared food and sat by the water when the sun went down. Life in the forest was usually quiet. Every animal had a special place to sleep and rest. The river provided cool water for everyone who lived nearby. Birds flew across the sky while fish swam in the deep water. The fast animal and the long bird were part of this big community. They had known each other for a long time. Although they sometimes played games, they usually got along well. The forest remained a safe home for all the animals. Nothing could change the beauty of the green trees and the blue river. Every evening brought a calm silence to the woods. The animals always looked forward to the next morning. They knew that tomorrow would bring new stories and fun times. So they slept quietly under the stars until the sun rose again. Animals would always remember the lessons they learned by the river. Good friends would share meals and respect each other in the woods. Peace and quiet returned to the green trees after the big game. The river kept flowing toward the sea under the bright blue sky.

### B2
Once upon a time, a smart animal and a gentle bird lived in a large green forest near a clear blue river. The animal was known to be very brilliant, but sometimes he could be quite bad to other creatures. One fine morning, the animal decided that he would invite the bird to share a meal at his house. He wanted to play a little game on his long friend because he thought it might be funny. The bird was very happy to receive such a nice invitation, and she arrived at the home right on time. When they sat down at the table, the host served a wonderful meal in a flat plate. Because the plate was completely flat, the host could easily eat every drop of his food. However, the poor bird could only touch the end of her long mouth against the glass surface without getting anything inside. The host laughed quietly while he watched the wanting bird struggle with her dinner, pretending that nothing was wrong. The calm bird did not show her anger, but she thanked the host for the food before she left the house. A few days later, the bird decided that she would invite the animal to dinner at her own home by the river. The animal gladly accepted the invitation, expecting to eat a wonderful meal and perhaps laugh again. When the animal arrived at the house, dinner was already prepared and smelled absolutely wonderful. The bird served the food inside a tall glass container which had a very narrow neck. Because her mouth was long and thin, the bird could easily reach down into the container and eat everything she wanted. Meanwhile, the animal could only touch the outside of the glass, unable to get a single piece of food. The animal realized then that he had been defeated at his own game by the smart bird. He felt very silly and said sorry for his earlier behavior. He understood that people should treat others the way they want to be treated.

Long ago, animals in the forest lived together and learned many important lessons about friendship and respect. Every single creature in that region knew how important it was to treat neighbors with care. If someone played a trick, the result would usually return to them in a surprising way. This particular story became famous across the entire community because it taught a valuable truth. People and people too often remember that actions speak much louder than simple words. When the host tried to trick his friend, he did not expect that the same trick would happen to him later. Life in the green forest continued for many years after this funny event took place. The bird and the animal remained good friends and never tried to play bad games again. They understood that true friendship requires truth, respect, and proper care for everyone involved. Whenever new visitors arrived near the clear river, they heard this wonderful story about the flat plate and the tall container. Everyone agreed that the brilliant bird handled the situation in a brilliant manner. The forest remained a quiet place where all creatures could live happily without fear of bad tricks. Even today, parents tell this tale to their children to teach them how to be good friends. Sharing meals together became a symbol of peace and trust throughout the entire green forest. No one ever forgot the day when the proud host learned his lesson from the gentle bird. Such stories help us understand why treating others properly is always the best choice in life. Animals and birds lived in this forest for a very long period of time. Each day brought new adventures and new challenges for everyone who lived near the water. The river provided fresh water for all creatures, and the green trees offered shade during hot summer days. Sometimes the weather was cold, but the animals always found ways to keep warm inside their homes. They shared food and stories when the winter months arrived, ensuring that no one went without help. The community was built upon trust and mutual respect, which kept everyone safe from danger. Whenever a problem arose, the animals gathered together to discuss the situation and find a good solution. They knew that cooperation was essential for survival in the wild forest. No single creature could manage everything alone, so teamwork became a natural part of their daily routine. The wise leaders of the forest guided the younger generation with patience and understanding. They taught them how to listen carefully and how to speak with respect during meetings. These traditions were passed down from generation to generation without interruption. As the years went by, the forest grew even more beautiful and full of life. Flowers bloomed in every color during the spring, and birds sang lovely songs from the high branches. The river flowed smoothly over the smooth stones, making a relaxing sound that calmed everyone. Visitors from distant places often traveled to see this wonderful forest and learn from its residents. They were always welcomed with open arms and treated to delicious meals by the riverbank. The host who had once played a selfish trick now welcomed every guest with genuine warmth. He never forgot the lesson he learned on the day of the flat plate and the tall container. His friendship with the bird became an example for all the younger animals in the region. Whenever arguments happened, the animals looked back at this famous story to find guidance. They realized that playing tricks on friends only leads to trouble and regret in the end. Instead, working together and showing respect brings lasting happiness and true peace. The green forest remained a safe haven where friendship and honesty ruled above all else. Parents continued to tell the tale every evening before putting their little children to bed. The children listened with wide eyes, absorbing the deep meaning behind the adventures of the bird and the animal. They promised themselves that they would always be good and fair to their own friends. And so the legacy of the smart bird and the humbled host lived on through the ages. No one in the forest ever doubted the power of kindness and respect among neighbors. The clear blue river kept flowing toward the horizon, mirroring the endless passage of time. Every living thing in the forest flourished under the protection of this shared philosophy. Peace and harmony defined the daily existence of every creature in that magical woodland. Even when difficulties appeared, the community remained strong and united against any threat. The memory of the past mistakes served as a constant reminder to stay humble and honest. Thus, life in the great green forest continued in a wonderful and harmonious way forever.

### C1
Long ago, within the dense emerald shadows of a sprawling forest, a brilliant fox and a gentle stork lived near the winding bends of a crystalline river. The fox possessed a sharp mind and a silver tongue, yet he harbored a penchant for mischievous pranks that often tested the patience of his neighbors. Though he was outwardly charming, his cleverness occasionally crossed the boundary into callousness, leaving smaller creatures bewildered and hurt. The stork, by contrast, moved through the world with quiet grace and unwavering dignity, paying little heed to the petty amusements of others. Not a single resident of the woodland failed to notice the stark contrast between the arrogant red beast and the serene, long-beaked bird.

One particularly luminous morning, the fox hatched a plan that he found immensely amusing, driven by a sudden urge to test the stork's legendary composure. With a disarming smile, he trotted over to the riverbank where the stork was preening her feathers and offered a polite invitation to share a midday meal at his den. He suggested they dine together as companions, masking his malicious intent beneath layers of elaborate hospitality and warm compliments. Touched by what she believed was a genuine gesture of fellowship, the stork accepted the invitation with gracious appreciation. She arrived at the fox's underground home precisely on time, her heart light with the expectation of an enjoyable culinary exchange.

When they gathered at the low wooden table, the host served a wonderful, fragrant broth, ladling it generously onto a completely flat stone plate. Because the surface was entirely level, the fox could easily lap up every single drop of the savory liquid with his quick tongue. He consumed his portion with apparent delight, making appreciative noises while casting sideways glances at his expectant guest. However, the poor stork could only tap the very tip of her long, slender beak against the cold stone without retrieving so much as a single morsel. Try as she might to angle her bill, the shallow design of the vessel rendered the meal utterly inaccessible to her specialized anatomy.

The fox chuckled softly behind his paw, watching the struggling bird with thinly veiled amusement while pretending that everything was proceeding normally. He offered polite hollow remarks about her lack of appetite, feigning a gentle concern that only served to sharpen the cruelty of the joke. Not a single word of anger escaped the stork's beak, nor did she allow her composure to fracture under the weight of such deliberate humiliation. With quiet dignity, she thanked her host for his hospitality, complimenting the aroma of the broth before taking her leave. She walked away from the den with measured steps, her mind already turning over the mechanics of the interaction with cool detachment.

Several days passed before the stork decided the time had arrived to return the invitation and offer her own form of hospitality by the river. She sent a formal message to the fox, asking him to join her for a midday meal at her shaded residence near the water. The fox accepted the invitation with eager anticipation, expecting another round of effortless entertainment at the expense of his long-necked companion. He arrived at the stork's home with a jaunty stride, his stomach rumbling pleasantly as rich, inviting aromas drifted toward him on the breeze. Dinner was already prepared and waiting, housed within a tall, narrow glass container that rested steadily upon a mossy stump.

The stork welcomed her guest with refined courtesy, gesturing toward the vessel where the magnificent meal had been carefully deposited. Because her beak was long and thin, she could easily reach deep down into the narrow neck of the container to feed at her leisure. She extracted choice portions of the feast with practiced ease, savoring every flavor while the fox sat watching in stunned silence. Meanwhile, the fox could only scratch at the exterior of the thick glass with his paws, completely unable to breach the barrier. No matter how he tilted his head or stretched his snout, the food remained frustratingly out of reach behind the impenetrable walls.

Realization struck the fox with the force of a falling branch as he understood he had been thoroughly outmaneuvered at his own game. The clever bird had used his own methods against him, exposing the profound thoughtlessness behind his earlier practical joke. Heat rose in his cheeks as a deep sense of foolishness washed over him, displacing his usual arrogance with genuine embarrassment. Looking up at the serene bird, he offered a sincere apology for his past behavior and acknowledged the justice of the outcome. He finally understood that mutual respect requires treating others precisely as one wishes to be treated in return.

News of the encounter spread rapidly across the green forest, transforming the event into a legendary tale told by every riverbank. Animals of all kinds gathered in the evenings to discuss the lesson, agreeing that the brilliant bird had handled the situation with masterclass precision. The fox never attempted another cruel prank, choosing instead to cultivate genuine friendships built upon honesty and shared consideration. Whenever new visitors arrived in the woodland, older residents recounted the story of the flat plate and the tall glass container. Parents passed the tale down to their children, ensuring that the next generation understood the heavy cost of selfish amusement.

Life by the clear river continued in a harmonious rhythm, sustained by the mutual trust that now bound the community together. The forest flourished through changing seasons, offering cool shade in the summer and warm shelter when winter winds swept through the trees. Whenever disputes arose among the younger creatures, the elders reminded them of the stork's calm wisdom and the fox's humbling lesson. Cooperation became the foundation of daily survival, proving that no single animal could thrive without the support of its neighbors. Peace and quiet reigned supreme throughout the woodland, silencing the petty grievances that had once threatened to fracture the community.

Even today, the memory of that fateful meal remains a cornerstone of the forest's unwritten laws regarding community behavior. The clear blue river continues to mirror the endless passage of time, flowing past the very spots where the famous lesson took place. Young animals listen with wide eyes as the story is recounted, promising themselves that they will always remain fair to their companions. Kindness and respect proved far more powerful than any clever trick, shaping the destiny of every creature who called the green forest home. Thus, the legacy of the smart bird and the humbled host lives on, echoing softly through the ancient trees for all eternity.

## st-0163 — Hansel and Gretel
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1

The woman is a bad person. She puts Hansel in a little room. She makes Gretel work hard. Gretel is not bad. The woman asks Gretel. The woman wants Gretel to look. Gretel looks at the light. Gretel puts the woman in the light. The woman is gone. The children find much money.

### A2
Once upon a time, Hansel and Gretel lived near a large forest. The family had very little food every single day. Times were difficult. The parents worried about the coming year. The next time, Hansel used pieces of food instead of small rocks. Small animals ate the food. Hansel and Gretel were lost in the dark trees. They felt cold and bad while they walked. She welcomed them inside with a promise of food and a bed. That old woman was a bad person. She put Hansel inside a small box. She made Gretel work hard every day. The bad woman planned to make the boy in a hot place. She wanted to cook him for dinner.

The woman asked Gretel to check the hot place. The smart girl stated that she did not understand. As the woman moved inside, Gretel pushed her fast.
