### B1
Once upon a time, two young children named Hansel and Gretel lived near a large forest. The family had very little food, so times were extremely difficult. The parents worried constantly about how they will survive the coming winter. They had no money and almost nothing to eat. Every single day, the father and mother tried to find a solution. However, the problem remained very big and serious. The mother decided that the children must be left deep inside the woods. She believed this choice was the only way to save the family. The father felt sad, but he agreed with her plan. They talked about the idea late into the night. The children listened quietly from their small bed in the corner. They felt very afraid of what might happen next. Hansel thought about a way to keep them safe. He knew the forest well because he played there often. When the sun went down, he gathered small white stones along the path. He put the stones into his pocket and waited for morning. The next day, the parents took Hansel and Gretel deep into the woods. As they walked, Hansel dropped the white stones one by one behind him. The parents left the children alone and walked away quickly. Hansel and Gretel sat down on the ground and waited for hours. When the night sky grew dark, the white stones appeared clear and bright. Those stones helped them find their way back home safely. Their parents looked surprised when the children returned to the house. For a short time, the family had peace again. Unfortunately, the food ran out completely after a few days. The mother made the same harsh plan once more. She told the father that they must leave the children again. This time, Hansel could not gather stones because the door was locked. The next morning, the family walked far into the forest. Hansel used small pieces of food instead of stones. He dropped tiny parts of food along the dark path. Unluckily, small birds flew down and ate every single piece. The birds left Hansel and Gretel completely lost in the deep woods. The brother and sister walked for a long time. They felt tired, cold, and extremely afraid. The wind blew through the trees, making strange noises all around them. They held hands tightly as they moved forward. Suddenly, the brother and sister discovered a strange house. The house was made of sweet food like sugar, bread, and chocolate. They felt very happy because they were extremely empty inside. As they began to eat the walls, an old woman opened the front door. She invited them inside with a big smile. She promised them a wonderful meal and a warm bed for the night. The children entered the room without any fear. They sat at a large table and ate as much as they wanted. After that, the old woman gave them comfortable places to sleep. However, that old woman was actually a bad person who liked to catch children. She wanted to cook them and eat them for dinner. The next morning, she locked Hansel inside a small cage. She made Gretel work hard every single day. Gretel had to clean the floors and bring water from the well. The bad woman planned to cook the boy as soon as he grew fat. Gretel cried every night because she missed her home and parents. She hoped that someone would come and save them. Despite the danger, the young girl remained strong and smart. She watched the bad woman carefully every single day. One morning, the woman told Gretel to check the hot oven. She wanted to bake bread and needed the fire to be very hot. The smart girl pretended that she did not understand. She asked the woman to show her how to check the heat. The bad woman grew angry and leaned inside the hot oven. As soon as the woman was inside, Gretel pushed her quickly. Then she closed the heavy door and locked it tight. The bad woman was completely defeated inside the fire. Gretel ran fast to the small cage and opened the door for Hansel. The brother and sister hugged each other with great joy. They explored the strange house and found a box full of gold coins. Taking the gold with them, Hansel and Gretel ran happily out of the forest. They reached a wide water and saw a friendly bird waiting. The bird carried them across the water safely. Soon, they saw their small house in the distance. Their parents were deeply sorry for what they had done. The family hugged the children and cried tears of joy. With the gold they brought home, they bought plenty of food. The parents never left their children again. Hansel and Gretel grew up healthy and strong. The family lived together in peace for the rest of their lives.

### B2
Once upon a time, two young children named Hansel and Gretel lived near a large forest with their family. Because the family had very little food, times were extremely difficult. The parents worried constantly about how they would survive the coming winter. They did not know what they should do to feed the children. Every single day, the situation became worse than it was before. The mother and father talked about the problem for hours. They knew that something must be done immediately. Otherwise, everyone would die because of the lack of food. The family had very few options left to consider. Therefore, the parents made a hard decision. They decided that the children must be left deep inside the woods. If the children were left alone, the parents might find a way to manage. This plan was terrible, but the parents felt they had no other choice. Hansel heard everything that his parents discussed. He was a smart boy who always planned ahead. Before they went into the forest, he gathered small white stones along the path. He put the stones into his pocket quietly. His parents did not notice what he was doing. They walked for a long time until they reached a very dark part of the woods. There, the parents told the children to wait while they gathered wood. Soon, night came, and the children were completely alone.

When the moon shone brightly, those white stones helped them find their way back home safely. Their parents were surprised when the children returned. However, food ran out again very quickly. The family faced the same terrible problem within days. The parents decided to leave the children in the woods once more. This time, they walked even deeper into the forest. Hansel wanted to gather more stones, but the door was locked. He could not go outside to find any stones. Instead, he used pieces of bread which he kept from breakfast. As they walked, he dropped small pieces of bread on the ground behind them. He thought that the bread would show them the way home again. Sadly, many birds flew down from the trees. Those birds ate the bread pieces completely. When evening arrived, Hansel and Gretel looked for the bread. Nothing was left on the ground. They were completely lost in the dark trees. While they walked deeper into the forest, they felt tired and cold. Both the brother and sister were very afraid. They did not know where they should go. The wind blew through the trees, making strange noises. They held each other tightly as they walked through the dark. They hoped that someone might help them find a safe place.

Suddenly, the brother and sister discovered a strange house. This house was made entirely of sugar, bread, and sweet chocolate. They were very empty inside, so they began to eat parts of the walls. While they ate the sweet walls, an old woman opened the front door. She looked at them and smiled in a friendly way. She invited them inside her strange home. She promised them a wonderful meal and a warm bed for the night. The children were happy because they were so tired. They went inside without any fear. The old woman gave them good food to eat. After they finished eating, she showed them comfortable beds. Hansel and Gretel went to sleep quickly. They believed that they were finally safe. However, that old woman was actually an evil person who liked to catch children. She wanted to harm them badly. The next morning, she showed her true character. She caught Hansel and locked him inside a small room with iron bars. Then, she made Gretel work hard every single day. Gretel had to clean the floors and carry heavy water. The evil woman planned to cook the boy and eat him for dinner. She built a big fire under a hot metal container. Gretel cried every day because she missed her home and her parents.

Despite all the trouble, Gretel remained smart and strong. She knew she had to find a way to save her brother. One day, the evil woman asked Gretel to check the hot metal container. The woman wanted to see if the fire was ready. The smart girl pretended that she did not understand the instructions. She told the woman that the opening was too small. The evil woman became angry at the girl. She told Gretel that the opening was big enough. To show the girl how to do it, the evil woman leaned inside the container. As soon as the woman was inside, Gretel pushed her quickly. Then, Gretel closed the heavy door and locked it securely. The evil woman was thus defeated inside her own house. Gretel ran quickly to open the small room where her brother was kept. Hansel came out, and both children hugged each other with joy. They looked around the strange house and found a box full of gold. Taking the gold with them, Hansel and Gretel ran happily out of the forest. They walked for hours until they reached a wide river. They could not cross the water by themselves. Suddenly, a friendly bird appeared near the water. The bird let the children sit on its back. It carried them safely across the wide water.

After they crossed the river, they finally reached their home. Their parents saw them coming down the road. The parents were deeply sorry for what they had done. They cried tears of joy when they hugged their children. The family promised that they would never separate again. With the gold they brought from the strange house, they bought all the food they needed. They never had to worry about winter or hunger again. The family lived together in peace from that day forward. Hansel and Gretel grew up healthy and strong. They often remembered their adventures in the dark forest. They learned that they could solve any problem if they stayed together. Their lives changed completely for the better. The parents treated them with love and care every single day. Nothing bad ever happened to them again. The house was always full of laughter and happiness. People in the village heard the story and visited them often. The children told everyone about the house made of sugar and chocolate. No one forgot the lessons they learned during those difficult months. They lived a long and peaceful life until the very end.

### C1
At the edge of a sprawling, ancient forest, a meager cottage sheltered a destitute family of four. A relentless famine had settled over the land, turning each passing day into a desperate struggle against the encroaching winter. Inside their damp home, the parents whispered late into the night, wrestling with an unbearable choice. The mother argued that only by sacrificing the children to the wilderness could the adults hope to survive. With a heavy heart and a spirit broken by hunger, the father finally yielded to her devastating plan.

Little did the adults know, young Hansel lay awake in the dark, absorbing every harsh word of their conspiracy. Possessed of a sharp and pragmatic mind, the boy began plotting his defense long before the dawn broke. As the first pale light filtered through the trees, he slipped into the frosty yard and filled his pockets with luminous white pebbles. The quartz stones clinked softly against his cotton tunic, reassuring him as the family set out toward the densest part of the woods. They walked for hours until the canopy overhead choked out the sun, plunging them into a gloomy twilight.

Upon a false pretext of gathering firewood, the parents abandoned the children to the deepening shadows. Hours bled into the night, leaving Hansel and Gretel shivering and entirely alone in the terrifying stillness. Yet when the full moon finally climbed above the canopy, its silvery light caught the polished surfaces of Hansel's scattered stones. Guided by the gleaming trail, the children retraced their steps and arrived home by morning much to their parents' astonishment. Alas, the meager store of flour vanished almost immediately, plunging the household back into the abyss of starvation.

Once more, the parents resolved to march the children into the uncharted depths of the forest, locking the heavy timber doors to prevent Hansel from collecting stones. Left with no alternative, the boy saved his morning crust of bread, crumbling the dry heel into tiny fragments as they walked. With every step deeper into the maze of trunks, he dropped a crumb, trusting the trail to guide them back. He failed to account for the watchful crows perched high above the branches. Flocks of hungry birds descended in a silent flurry, devouring every morsel before the shadows could even lengthen.

When evening descended with a freezing wind, the children searched in vain for the reassuring white crumbs. Lost amidst towering pines that seemed to whisper dark warnings, they huddled together against the biting chill. Hours blurred into a blur of exhaustion as they stumbled blindly through the underbrush. Just as despair threatened to consume them, a sudden scent of warm honey and spun sugar drifted through the damp air. Drawn by the intoxicating aroma, they emerged into a small clearing and stared in absolute wonder.

Rising before them stood a bizarre architectural marvel constructed entirely of gingerbread, frosted glass windows, and rich chocolate tiles. Overwhelmed by gnawing hunger, the children rushed forward and began to break off pieces of the sweet sugary wall. Suddenly, a creaking hinge broke the silence as an elderly woman peered out from the heavy oak door. Wreathed in wrinkles and leaning heavily on a cane, she smiled with practiced warmth and invited them inside. Promises of pancakes, sugar, apples, and soft feather beds lured the exhausted children across the threshold without a single shred of suspicion.

The gracious hostess fed them a lavish feast before tucking them into crisp, inviting beds where they fell into a profound slumber. They believed their trials were finally over, little knowing that their sanctuary was a trap spun by a wicked sorceress. With the first rays of morning, the old woman shed her benevolent facade and revealed her cruel nature. With surprising strength, she seized Hansel and dragged him to a tiny, iron-barred stable in the yard. Then she turned her attention to weeping Gretel, forcing the terrified girl into endless drudgery of scrubbing floors and carrying heavy buckets of water.

The witch intended to fatten the boy and roast him for a grand feast, stoking a massive fire beneath a great iron cauldron every single morning. Gretel wept bitter tears for her brother and her distant home, yet her native cleverness never deserted her. One afternoon, the impatient hag ordered the girl to check if the roaring oven fire was hot enough to bake bread. Feigning total ignorance, the smart girl looked up with wide, innocent eyes and declared the opening far too small for her frame. Exasperated by the delay, the foolish woman snapped that the mouth of the oven was wide enough for anyone.

To demonstrate, the witch thrust her head and shoulders into the roaring heat of the iron chamber. Seizing the miraculous chance, Gretel lunged forward with all her might and slammed the heavy iron door shut, dropping the heavy latch into place. The wicked woman shrieked in fury as the heavy metal locked her securely inside her own fiery trap. Without wasting a single second, Gretel raced across the courtyard and flung open the barred door of the stable. Hansel burst out into the fresh air, and the siblings fell into each other's arms, weeping tears of pure relief and vindication.

Exploring the strange confectioner's cottage, they discovered heavy chests filled with glittering pearls and heavy gold coins. Stuffing their pockets with the gleaming treasure, they fled the forest and ran until they reached the banks of a wide, rushing river. No bridge spanned the churning waters, leaving them stranded until a helpful white duck offered to carry them across one by one. Safely on the opposite bank, they recognized the familiar rolling hills leading toward their humble village. As they burst through the cottage door, their repentant parents wept openly, begging for forgiveness and pledging never to abandon them again.

With the boundless wealth they had brought from the forest, poverty became a distant memory and hunger vanished from their lives forever. The family dwelt together in unbroken harmony, bound by a profound trust that no hardship could ever fracture again. Hansel and Gretel grew into wise and resilient adults, often gazing toward the distant treeline and remembering the lessons of the dark woods. No shadow ever touched their threshold again, and their home remained a haven of warmth, laughter, and enduring peace until the end of their days.

### A1

Her father asks what is not right. The man says words must stay true.

The child lets him in the house. They share a room. They eat food. The small animal wants to sleep. She gets hard and bad. She moves him. The small animal changes. He becomes a man. ### B1
Once upon a time, a young girl lived in a big house near a dark forest. She loved to play outside with her favorite gold ball. On a warm summer day, she walked near the water and threw the ball. Peace and quiet returned to the house. They always walked near the clear water. The sun shone down upon the green forest every single morning. ### B2
Once upon a time, a young girl lived in a great castle near a deep, dark forest. She loved to play outside with her favorite golden ball. On a warm summer day, she walked near the water and threw the ball. Suddenly, the ball fell deep into the cold water and disappeared. The poor child began to cry because she could not reach her toy. A little green animal jumped out from the water and asked why she was sad. She told him about the golden ball, and the creature offered to help her. However, the animal wanted something in return for his service. The creature told her that he must live in the castle and eat from her golden plate. Every morning, the sun shone brightly over the tall castle towers. The birds sang sweet songs in the deep forest while the wind blew softly through the green trees. Their friendship grew stronger with each passing year, bringing light and happiness into the entire household. The servants and workers in the castle respected the couple deeply for their good heart.

Even when difficult situations came up, they always found a way to solve them together. The story of the golden ball became a famous story told by parents to their children across the land. And so the years passed quietly, leaving wonderful memories in the hearts of everyone who lived in the great castle. The young man and the young woman enjoyed their days together. The castle servants were happy to work for such a kind couple. Every room was filled with laughter and music. No one ever felt sad or apart in that wonderful place. If someone had a problem, the couple was always ready to help. They gave food to the poor and supported people who needed money. Everyone in the town knew about the famous story of the golden ball. Children played games near the water and hoped to see something special. The young man and the young woman always told the children to keep their promises. They explained that trust was the most important thing in the world. Years went by, but their love never changed. They grew old together, sitting by the fire during long winter nights. Their story was written down in old books so that future generations would remember it.

People from distant lands came just to hear the wonderful tale. The great castle remained a symbol of hope and truth for all. And so they lived out their days in complete happiness, knowing that they had done right. The memory of the little green animal stayed with them forever as a reminder of their beginning. Whenever the wind blew through the trees, it sounded like the soft songs of birds. The world outside the castle walls was large, but their home was their entire universe. ### C1
Once upon a time, a young princess lived within the limestone walls of a magnificent castle situated near the edge of a deep, ancient forest. Her days were frequently spent wandering the royal grounds, her attention entirely captivated by her most treasured possession, a perfectly spherical golden ball. On a particularly warm summer afternoon, she sought the coolness of the deep royal fountain, tossing her prized toy high into the air and catching it as it fell. In a moment of catastrophic miscalculation, the glittering sphere slipped past her outstretched fingers, tumbling deep into the dark, churning water until it vanished completely. The inconsolable child sank to the mossy stones, weeping bitterly over the sudden and seemingly permanent loss of her favorite companion.

While her lamentations echoed through the trees, a peculiar amphibian with slick green skin emerged from the murky depths of the water. He hoisted himself onto the stone rim, inquiring gently as to why her delicate features were so thoroughly marred by grief. Through tearful breaths, she confessed the fate of her golden ball, prompting the creature to offer an immediate and unconditional rescue of the toy. However, the amphibian demanded a heavy toll for his labor, insisting that he be permitted to enter the castle, dwell in its halls, and dine from her golden plate. Desperate to recover her lost treasure without delay, the princess readily agreed to every demand the strange creature set before her.

True to his word, the little green animal plunged back into the dark water and swiftly returned with the gleaming golden sphere. No sooner had the toy been placed upon the grass than the ungrateful girl snatched it up and bolted toward the castle without a backward glance. She slammed the heavy oak door behind her, instantly erasing any memory of the promise she had made mere moments before. Later that evening, as the royal family gathered for supper, a soft, deliberate tapping sounded against the massive entrance doors. When the king's daughter pulled the door open, she recoiled at the sight of the green amphibian waiting patiently upon the threshold.

She attempted to bar the door against him, but her observant father noticed her distress and demanded an explanation for her bizarre behavior. Upon learning of the bargain struck at the fountain, the stern monarch declared that a royal promise must never be broken under any circumstances. Reluctantly, the miserable girl admitted the creature into the grand hall and permitted him to share her private dining table. When they had finished their meal, the amphibian requested to accompany her upstairs and sleep upon her embroidered pillow in the quiet bedroom. Overwhelmed by a sudden surge of blind fury and utter exhaustion, she seized the creature by his slippery limbs and hurled him forcefully against the stone wall.

Before he struck the hard masonry, a blinding flash of light enveloped the little animal, transforming him instantly into a remarkably handsome young man. He explained with calm dignity that a wicked enchantress had trapped him in the form of a lowly water creature for many long years. Only the steadfast commitment of another person, demonstrated through the honoring of a difficult vow, could ever hope to break the heavy spell. Realizing the depth of her error and the surprising grace of her companion, the princess felt her heart soften toward him profoundly. They forged a deep bond through countless hours of conversation, eventually realizing that their lives were forever intertwined within the safety of the castle walls.

Every morning thereafter, the sun illuminated the tall castle towers while songbirds filled the ancient forest with sweet, harmonious melodies. Life within the royal household grew remarkably peaceful, characterized by genuine joy and mutual respect between the newly formed couple. Citizens from the surrounding town frequently traveled up the winding road to catch a glimpse of the king and his remarkably content family. The young man never truly forgot his time spent as a cold, green amphibian at the bottom of the royal fountain. Whenever he gazed out over the dark water, he reflected on the vital importance of honesty and integrity in all human affairs.

The princess herself remained utterly faithful to her word, ensuring that no broken vows or dishonesty ever threatened their quiet happiness. They shared every burden equally, supporting one another through unexpected challenges with patience and remarkable understanding. Their friendship deepened with each passing year, radiating warmth and stability throughout every corner of the vast stone fortress. Servants and kitchen workers alike held the couple in high regard, deeply respecting their humility and unfailing kindness toward everyone. Whenever difficult situations arose in the kingdom, the pair invariably found a wise and compassionate solution together.

In time, the tale of the golden ball evolved into a beloved legend recounted by parents to their children across the entire land. Everyone learned that true companions must cultivate absolute trust and maintain strict adherence to their spoken commitments. The years slipped by quietly, leaving behind a wealth of cherished memories embedded firmly in the hearts of the castle inhabitants. The young man and his wife spent their afternoons strolling through the green forest, discussing philosophy, nature, and their shared future. Sometimes they sat quietly by the fountain edge, watching small fish dart beneath the shimmering surface of the water.

The aging king frequently observed the couple from his high window, smiling deeply as he witnessed their enduring devotion. Every chamber of the castle echoed with laughter and music, ensuring that sorrow found no permanent home within those walls. If a neighboring family fell upon hard times, the generous couple was always first to offer substantial aid and provisions. Local children often played games near the water, secretly hoping to catch a glimpse of something magical beneath the ripples. Whenever the young man and woman encountered these children, they gently reminded them that keeping one's promise is the most vital duty of all.

Decades passed smoothly, yet the affection between the couple never wavered even slightly as they grew gray together. During long winter nights, they sat side by side before a roaring fireplace, recounting the strange adventures of their youth. Their remarkable story was eventually inscribed into ancient leather-bound volumes so that future generations would never forget the lesson. Travelers from distant realms arrived at the gates merely to hear the tale of how a broken vow led to an enduring union. The grand castle stood proudly as a timeless symbol of truth, resilience, and the quiet power of kept promises.

They lived out their remaining years in complete tranquility, knowing they had navigated life with honor and genuine care. The lingering memory of the little green animal remained with them forever, serving as a humble foundation for their happiness. When the wind swept through the tall forest trees, its rustling sound mirrored the sweet songs of birds at dawn. Though the world beyond the fortress gates was vast and unpredictable, their home remained their entire universe. Requiring nothing more than each other's presence, they rested securely in a peace that endured for all time.

## st-0165 — The Fisherman and His Wife
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
An old man lives near water. A woman lives with him now. They stay in a small house. The man goes out every day. He wants to get some food. He tries to take something from water.

One day, he takes a little animal. The animal is in the water. It talks to the kind man. It asks him to let it free. The man lets it go. He goes home to the woman. He tells her everything he sees. The woman is not good with this. She wants a new house. The man goes back to the water. He calls the little animal. The animal comes out of water. It gives them a good house.

The woman is still not well. She wants more power now. She asks to become a leader. The man goes back to the water. The little animal makes her a leader. The woman wants control of the world. She asks to become like a god. The animal is very bad about this. The animal does not like her hope.

The man goes back to the water. The day turns bad. The water is not clear. It is a big problem. The animal takes the new house away. The man and woman are back home. They stay in the small house.

### A2
Once upon a time, an old man lived near the sea. His wife lived there too. The man went out every day to catch fish. One afternoon, he caught a very unusual fish. The fish could speak like a human. The fish asked him to set it free. The kind man did not want to cause trouble. He let it go back into the water. He returned home and told his wife about the special fish. His wife became very upset. She asked why he did not take anything. She wanted a better house. Their old house was small. The man went back to the sea. He called the fish.

The fish gave them a nice new house immediately. However, the woman was still not happy. She wanted more power. She asked her husband to make her a ruler. The man went back to the water again. The special animal made her a ruler. Then the woman wanted control over the whole world. She asked to become like a god. That made the fish very upset. The husband returned to the sea for this final wish. The air turned bad. The water became dark and strong. The fish took away all their new things. They were back in their small, poor house.

### B1
Once upon a time, an old man and his wife lived near the sea. The man went out every day to catch fish in the water. One afternoon, he caught a very strange fish that could speak. The fish asked the man to set it free back into the water. The kind man did not want to hurt the animal, so he let it go. When he returned home, he told his wife about the wonderful fish. She listened to the story with great interest and surprise. The old man sat down and smiled at his dear wife. He had never seen anything like that strange animal before. The wife thought about the fish for a long time. She believed that they might get something good from the sea. The sun began to set behind the dark blue water. They went to sleep and dreamed about a better life. Morning came quickly, and the sun rose high in the sky. The air felt warm and nice as another day began.

His wife became very angry and asked why he had not taken anything. She wanted a better house because their old one was small and poor. The man went back to the sea and called for the fish. The fish gave them a nice new house immediately. However, the woman was still not happy after a few days passed. She wanted more power and asked her husband to make her a big leader. She wanted control over the town and all the people. The husband did not like this idea, but he had to go. He walked down to the water and called out to the fish again. The special animal made her a big leader, but she wanted even more control. She always wanted something new and never felt happy. The man walked home and saw his wife in her new place. She wore fine clothes and sat on a high chair. Many people came to see her and show respect. Yet her heart remained cold and full of dark thoughts. She looked at the people and told them what to do.

She finally asked to become like a god, which made the fish very angry. When the husband returned to the sea, the weather turned bad. The water became dark and wild as the fish took away all their things. The man and his wife were left back in their small, poor house. The sky turned black, and heavy rain fell down upon the land. Strong wind blew across the water and made huge waves. The man ran back home as fast as he could run. All their nice things were gone, and the new house was missing. They stood outside in the cold rain and looked around. The wife cried because she had lost everything they got. She realized that she had brought them bad luck. The old man put his arm around her and said nothing. They walked inside the small room and sat down together. The room felt empty and very cold without any fire. They knew that they had made a terrible mistake.

They have learned a hard lesson from this strange event today. If the woman wants more, she will lose everything again. The old man hopes that they will stay happy in their small home. Life near the water is quiet now, and they live in peace. The fish swims far away and never comes back to the land. Every afternoon, the man looks at the water and remembers the day. He thinks about the wonderful fish and what happened then. The wife never asks for anything else from the sea. They eat simple food and share quiet moments every evening. Sometimes they talk about the past and the big change. The sun goes down, and light falls on the water. They feel safe inside their little house by the sea. The world around them is quiet and full of rest. They know that simple life is better than too much power. Hope stays with them as they live through their remaining years.

### B2
Once upon a time, an old man and his wife lived in a small house near the sea. The man went out every day to catch fish in the water. One afternoon, he caught a very strange fish that could speak like a human being. The fish looked at the man and asked him to set it free back into the sea. The kind man did not want to hurt the animal, so he let it go without any trouble. When he returned home, he told his wife about the wonderful fish he had seen. His wife became very angry and asked why he had not taken anything from the special creature. She wanted a better house because their old one was small and poor. The man went back to the sea and called for the fish, which appeared above the water.

The fish listened to the man and gave them a nice new house immediately. However, the woman was still not happy after a few days passed. She wanted more power and asked her husband to make her a ruler. The man went back to the water again and asked the fish for another big change. The special animal made her a ruler, but the woman wanted even more control over the whole world. She finally asked to become like a god, which made the fish very angry. When the husband returned to the sea to ask for this final wish, the weather turned bad. The water became dark and wild as the fish took away all their new things. The man and his wife were left back in their small, poor house once again.

Many years passed while the couple lived in that small house near the water. Sometimes they thought about the special fish and the strange events that had happened. The old man always remembered how the fish had spoken to him in the afternoon. His wife often wished that she had been more patient with their simple life. If she had kept her happiness, they would have stayed in the nice new house forever. Instead, her great desire for power led to complete ruin and sadness. The local people who heard the story often talked about the very difficult woman. They agreed that people should always be glad for what they already possess. Extreme desire only brings trouble, while a calm heart brings true joy.

Life by the sea went on quietly for the old man and his faithful wife. Every morning, the man walked down to the shore to look at the blue waves. He never saw the special fish again, but he often thought about that afternoon. His wife learned a hard lesson about wanting too much power and control. She spent her days working around the small house and helping her husband. They did not need any more big changes or fancy things to be happy. Their simple home was enough for them as they lived out their remaining years. Whenever the wind blew hard over the water, they remembered the storm. That terrible day stayed in their minds as a warning against boundless desire.

The man and woman stayed in their home and worked every single day. They ate simple food and drank clean water from the nearby well. The sun rose and set over the water while the seasons changed slowly. They talked about the past and remembered the lessons they had learned. The woman understood that her strong drive had destroyed their bright future. She smiled at her husband because she finally appreciated his company. They shared stories with people who visited the small local town. Everyone agreed that simple lives were better than lives built on extreme demands. The old couple found peace in each other during their final years together.

### C1
Once upon a time, an old man and his faithful wife inhabited a modest dwelling situated right near the restless sea. Every single day, the man walked down the rocky shore to cast his net into the deep water in search of fish. The salt air filled his lungs while the endless horizon offered a quiet comfort to his aging mind. He had lived this simple existence for decades, accepting whatever fortune the tide decided to bring him. Little did he know that one particular afternoon would shatter the quiet routine of their unremarkable lives forever.

During that fateful afternoon, the man felt a heavy tug upon his net and pulled with all his remaining strength. To his utter astonishment, he hauled up a very strange fish that possessed the uncanny ability to speak like a human being. The creature looked directly into the old man's weathered eyes and begged to be set free back into the safety of the sea. Being a gentle soul, the kind man had no desire to harm any living creature, especially one so miraculous. Without hesitation, he released the captive fish back into the water, watching it vanish beneath the dark waves without any trouble whatsoever.

When the fisherman finally returned home to his cramped cottage, he eagerly told his wife about the wonderful fish he had encountered. Instead of sharing his wonder, his wife became fiercely angry and demanded to know why he had not asked the creature for a reward. She pointed out their miserable, decaying walls and argued that they deserved a far better house than this wretched hut. Though he felt reluctant to ask for favors, the man yielded to her persistent nagging and walked back down to the shore. He called out to the enchanted fish, which promptly appeared above the surface of the water to hear his request.

The obligating fish listened carefully to the man's embarrassed plea and granted them a charming new house immediately. Upon his return, the old man found his wife standing before a sturdy cottage complete with a lovely garden and a warm hearth. For a brief period, contentment seemed to settle over the couple as they enjoyed their upgraded surroundings and comfortable furniture. However, after only a few days had passed, the woman's ambition began to fester like an untreated wound. She realized that a mere house was not enough to satisfy her growing hunger, so she decided to aim much higher.

Driven by an insatiable thirst for power, the woman commanded her husband to return to the shore and demand that the fish make her a ruler. Reluctantly, the weary man walked back to the water, where the waves had turned a sullen gray to match his heavy heart. He whispered his wife's grand demand to the receptive creature, and the magical animal fulfilled the wish without uttering a single word of complaint. When the husband returned, he discovered his wife seated upon a magnificent throne while local officials bowed before her commands. Yet this elevation only fueled her arrogance, for control over a single region could never appease her boundless appetite.

Not content with ruling a kingdom, the ambitious woman decided she must control the entire world and everything within it. She sent her husband back to the shore once more, instructing the reluctant messenger to demand that she be granted the status of a supreme deity. As the old man delivered this final, outrageous demand, the previously calm ocean swelled with sudden and violent fury. The water grew pitch dark and wild as the majestic fish listened to the arrogant request with profound displeasure. Without granting the wish, the creature vanished into the depths, leaving the terrified man standing alone in the howling wind.

When the husband hurried back to find his home, the magnificent palace and the royal throne had completely vanished into thin air. Instead of luxury, he found his wife waiting outside the exact same small, poor house where their journey had originally begun. The harsh weather mirrored their ruined fortunes, and the cold reality of their greed settled heavily upon their shoulders. Many long years passed while the humbled couple continued to live in that very same modest dwelling near the water. Sometimes they looked out at the churning waves and reflected upon the strange events that had disrupted their quiet lives.

The old man frequently remembered how the miraculous fish had spoken to him with such human sorrow on that afternoon. His wife often wept as she wished she had possessed the wisdom to appreciate their simple life of quiet contentment. Had she maintained her initial happiness, they certainly would have retained the comfortable new house for the rest of their days. Instead, her relentless drive for absolute control had led them directly to complete ruin and profound emotional sadness. The local villagers who heard whispers of the strange tale often shook their heads when discussing the exceptionally difficult woman.

Everyone in the surrounding area agreed that human beings should always remain grateful for whatever blessings they already possess. They understood that extreme desire inevitably invites trouble, whereas a calm and accepting heart brings genuine joy. Life by the sea eventually resumed its quiet rhythm for the old man and his now faithful, reflective wife. Every morning, the man walked down to the shore just to observe the endless rolling of the blue waves. Although he never encountered the special fish again, the memory of that encounter remained vivid in his mind.

His wife learned a painful lesson about the dangerous consequences of wanting too much power over the world. She spent her remaining days sweeping the floors of the small house and helping her husband with his daily chores. They realized they did not need grand titles or expensive possessions to find peace within their hearts. Their simple home proved entirely sufficient as they navigated the final chapters of their shared existence. Whenever a strong gale blew across the water, they paused to remember the terrifying storm that had cured their greed.

Working side by side every single day, the couple found a deep, enduring comfort in their mutual companionship. They ate plain food from their pantry and drank fresh water drawn directly from the stone well outside. The sun continued its predictable journey across the sky, rising and setting while the seasons shifted with gentle grace. By sharing stories with occasional visitors, the old woman openly admitted that her own ambition had destroyed their bright future. She would smile warmly at her husband, finally recognizing that true wealth is found only in love and quiet acceptance.

## st-0166 — Mother Holle
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, a mother lives with two girls. One girl is good and hard. The other girl is not hard. The mother loves that bad girl. The mother does not love the good girl. The good girl sits by a well. She lets her thing go down. The mother tells her to move. She moves into the water. She stays in the water.

She sees a place in the morning. She sees an old food box. The food asks for help. She takes the food out. She walks to a tree. The tree wants help. She moves the tree. The food things fall down. She comes to Mother Holle house. Mother Holle makes her work. She works day after day.

She shakes the old room things. White water falls on the land. She wants to go home now. Mother Holle brings her to a door. Money falls on her face. She returns to her mother. She has money with her. The bad girl moves in too. She sees the place. She does not help the food. She does not help the tree.

Mother Holle sends her away. Black color covers her all over. She returns home with black color. The good girl lives a good life. She is very happy now. The family stays in the house. The good girl helps every day. The bad girl stays away. Everyone looks at the money. The story comes to an end. The good girl helps the home. The family is very happy today.

### A2
Once upon a time, a mother lived with two girls. One girl did hard work every single day. The other girl did not want to do any work. The mother always loved the second girl more. The first girl had to do all the hard work.

Every day, the poor girl sat by a well. She made clothes there by hand. One day, her little thing fell down into the dark water. She ran back to her mother. She felt deep bad feeling inside her mind. The mother told her to move into the well.

She went into the water and lost her mind. When she came back, she found a fine green place. She walked along and came across an old room full of food. The food asked for help from the good girl. The good girl took the food out with great care.

Then she walked further until she saw a big plant. This plant was full of good food. The plant asked her to move its top part. She moved the plant until all food fell down. Soon, she reached a small house where a mother lived. This mother told the girl to stay and make her bed.

When she moved the bed things, cold water fell down upon them. After some time, she wanted to go home again. The mother brought her to a large door. A huge amount of money fell down upon her. She returned to her mother, covered in money.

The second girl saw the money and wanted to get large money too. She went into the well on purpose. She came back in the same place. She did not help the food and the food plant. She arrived at the house and did not work hard.

The mother finally sent her away from the house. A huge amount of black stuff covered her completely. She returned home with the black stuff upon her. Her mother saw her and felt very bad about it. The girls learned a big rule about work and life.

### B1
Once upon a time, a mother lived with two young girls. One girl was very busy and sweet. The other girl was lazy and proud. The mother loved the lazy girl much more than the good one. The busy girl had to do all the heavy work every day. She worked hard from early morning until late evening. She had no rest during the day. She cleaned the house and cooked food for them. She did everything because her mother asked her to do so.

The poor girl sat by a water well and made long lines for hours. Her hands were hurt and red from the constant activity. One day, her metal tool fell down into the dark water. She ran back to her mother, crying with great sadness. Her mother told her to jump into the well to find the tool. She jumped into the cold water without any fear. The water was very deep and dark all around her. She fell down and lost her mind for some time. When she woke up, she found herself in a wonderful green place full of green land.

The warm sun was high above her head in the clear sky. She walked along a small path. She came across an old cooking box full of hot food. The food inside cried out for help. The good girl took the food out of the box very carefully. Then she walked further until she saw a tall tree full of green fruit. The tree asked her to shake its branches with her hands. She shook the tree until all the fruit fell down to the ground. She picked everything up and put it in a nice place. She wanted to be helpful to every single thing.

Soon, she reached a small house where an old woman lived. The old woman told the girl to stay with her and make her bed. If the girl shook the cover properly every morning, snow fell down upon the earth. This made the girl very happy and calm. She stayed there and did her job every single day. Mother Holle was very kind to her and gave her nice food to eat. The girl always smiled and thanked the woman for her support.

After some time, she wanted to go home because she missed her family. Mother Holle brought her to a large door. As she passed through the door, a huge amount of gold fell down upon her. She returned to her mother, covered in gold, and told her everything. Her mother was very surprised to see all the bright gold. The family became rich because of the good girl's hard work.

When the lazy girl saw the gold, she wanted to get rich too. She jumped into the well on purpose to find the same good luck. She woke up in the same wonderful place with green land. However, she refused to help the food and the fruit tree. When she arrived at the old woman's house, she was lazy and refused to work. She slept all day and did not care about the bed. Mother Holle finally sent her away because of her bad behavior.

As she passed through the big door, black earth covered her completely. She returned home with the dark ground stuck to her clothes. The lazy girl never forgot that terrible day. She realized that people must work hard to get good things in life. The busy girl lived a happy life with her family for many years. They had enough money and no more serious problems.

### B2
Once upon a time, a mother lived with two girls. One girl was very good and sweet, but the other girl was lazy and proud. The mother always loved the lazy girl more, while the good girl had to do all the heavy work every single day. Every single day, the poor good girl sat by a well and made thread until her hands hurt very much. One day, the tool fell down into the dark water. She ran back to her mother, crying, and the mother told her to jump into the well to get it back. Without thinking, she jumped into the water and fell down. When she woke up, she found herself in a wonderful green field with a lovely sun shining above. The kind girl walked along and came across an old kitchen machine full of bread, which cried out for help. The good girl took the bread out of the machine carefully. Then she walked further until she saw a large tree full of fruit. The tree asked her to shake its branches so that the food could fall down. She shook the tree until all the fruit was on the ground, and then she continued her journey.

Soon, she reached a small house where an old woman lived. The old woman, who was Mother Holle, told the girl to stay with her and make her bed. Whenever she shook the bed properly, snow fell down upon the earth, which made the girl happy. After spending some time there, she wanted to go home because she missed her family. Mother Holle brought her to a large door, and as she passed through, a huge amount of gold fell down upon her. She returned to her mother, covered in gold, and told them everything that had happened. When the lazy girl saw the gold, she wanted to get rich too, so she jumped into the well on purpose. She woke up in the same wonderful field, but she refused to help the bread and the tree. When she arrived at Mother Holle's house, she was very lazy and refused to work hard.

Mother Holle finally sent her away. As she passed through the big door, a huge amount of dark material covered her completely. She returned home with the material stuck to her, which served as a lesson for her bad behavior. The lazy girl never forgot that day, and the good girl lived a happy life. Many people in the town talked about this strange story for a long time. They always remembered how the kind girl received a great reward for her wonderful service. The mother finally understood that being good and doing work brings good things into life. Even though the lazy girl tried to change her mind later, it was too late. Every child in the neighborhood learned a very important lesson from the two girls. They knew that truth and effort would always lead to success in the end. The village remained a quiet place where everyone helped each other through difficult times. Mother Holle continued to watch over the young people from her secret home above the clouds. The sun kept shining upon the green field, and the special bread remained warm in the kitchen machine. Life went on smoothly, and people always smiled when they thought about the special journey. The community grew strong because the children learned how to be kind. Every single person remembered the story of the good girl and her reward. The mother changed her mind and began to treat both children with care. No one ever forgot the important lessons of that wonderful summer. People talked about the magical well for many years. Children played near the water and listened to stories about Mother Holle. The village became a symbol of peace and hard work. Every family shared food and helped each other during difficult months. The sun shone brightly every morning, bringing light and hope to the entire area. Life in the small town was peaceful and full of joy. Everyone knew that being good would always bring a happy future.

### C1
Once upon a time, within the confines of a modest cottage, a mother shared her roof with two young girls. One of them was the biological child of the widow, a maiden whose heart was filled with vanity and whose hands remained permanently idle. The other girl, though treated as a servant in her own home, possessed a nature of profound gentleness and an untiring work ethic. Notwithstanding this stark disparity in character, the mother harbored an inexplicable preference for her own lazy daughter, lavishing her with affection. Meanwhile, the diligent stepdaughter was subjected to the most exhausting domestic labor, enduring her daily burdens without a single word of bitter complaint.

Every single day, the poor girl was forced to sit by the edge of an ancient well from dawn until dusk. There she spun thread with such relentless concentration that her fingers bled and her joints ached from the unyielding strain. One afternoon, as she worked through her blinding exhaustion, the wooden tool slipped from her numb fingers and plunged into the dark abyss. The heavy splash echoed ominously against the stone walls, signaling the absolute loss of the essential instrument. Panic gripping her chest, she ran back to her stepmother to confess the misfortune, terrified of the punishment that surely awaited her.

Instead of showing any mercy, the merciless woman scolded the weeping girl and commanded her to jump into the well. She was ordered to retrieve the fallen tool or never dare to show her face in the cottage again. Without a moment of hesitation, driven by pure despair rather than logic, the heartbroken maiden leaped over the rocky ledge. She plunged into the cold water, expecting the worst, but instead lost consciousness as she fell through the damp gloom. When her eyes finally fluttered open, she found herself not at the bottom of a watery grave, but in a breathtakingly green field kissed by warm sunlight.

Not knowing where her strange journey had brought her, the bewildered girl began to walk along a narrow, winding path. Soon she came across a peculiar kitchen appliance, an old oven packed tightly with loaves of freshly baked bread. The bread cried out in a desperate voice, begging her to take them out before they burned to a crisp. With gentle hands and practiced care, the kind maiden removed every single loaf from the hot metal chamber. She stacked them neatly on the grass nearby before continuing her solitary walk through the radiant landscape.

A little further along the path, she encountered a magnificent apple tree whose branches groaned under the immense weight of ripe fruit. The tree whispered a humble plea, asking her to shake its limbs so that the heavy harvest could finally fall. Without a second thought, the obliging girl grasped the thick trunk and shook it with all her remaining strength. Golden apples rained down upon the earth like autumn leaves until not a single piece of fruit remained on the boughs. Only then did she gather her composure and resume her journey toward the horizon.

Eventually, her wandering brought her to the doorstep of a quaint, rustic cottage nestled at the edge of a rolling meadow. An eccentric old woman with piercing eyes peered out from the doorway, introducing herself as Mother Holle. Sensing the innate goodness in the weary traveler, the old woman invited her inside and offered her permanent shelter. In exchange for room and board, the girl only had to perform one specific daily chore: she had to make Mother Holle's feather bed with absolute care. The eccentric matron gave strict instructions that the feathers must be shaken until the snow flew through the air.

Whenever the dutiful maiden fluffed the mattress with vigor, white flakes of snow drifted down from the sky to blanket the mortal world below. This magical duty brought a quiet, strange joy to her heart, and she performed her tasks with immense diligence. She lived in comfort for a long while, yet a persistent homesickness eventually crept into her thoughts. Despite the kindness of Mother Holle and the peace of the valley, the girl confessed that she deeply missed her family. Understanding her genuine sorrow, the old woman led her to a towering golden door and bade her farewell.

As the maiden stepped through the threshold to begin her journey home, a heavy shower of glittering gold coins fell upon her. The precious metal adhered to her clothes and skin, transforming her into a glowing figure of wealth and radiance. When she finally returned to the cottage, her stepmother and stepsister stared in utter astonishment at her astonishing transformation. She sat by the hearth and recounted every single detail of her miraculous adventure in the realm above the clouds. The greedy stepdaughter listened intently, her eyes fixed not on the story, but on the glittering riches her stepsister had brought home.

Consumed by fierce jealousy, the lazy girl decided she too must jump into the well to secure her own fortune. She threw herself down the watery shaft on purpose, caring little for the dangers that lay beneath. Just like her stepsister, she woke up in the sunlit green field, but her attitude remained entirely unchanged. When she passed the complaining oven, she ignored its frantic cries and walked right past without lifting a finger. Moments later, she encountered the laden apple tree, yet she refused to shake its heavy branches, deeming the labor entirely beneath her.

Upon arriving at Mother Holle's cottage, the arrogant girl expected to be rewarded instantly without having to prove her worth. Instead of working hard, she slept late, neglected the daily chores, and treated the old woman with shocking disrespect. Mother Holle tolerated the insolence for only a brief period before abruptly dismissing her from the household. As the ungrateful maiden passed through the grand doorway, expecting a shower of gold, a thick bucket of black pitch emptied over her head. The dark, sticky substance covered her completely, refusing to wash off no matter how hard she scrubbed.

She returned to her mother's cottage covered in the shameful mark of her laziness, a permanent reminder of her failure. The dark material clung to her skin for the rest of her days, serving as a bitter lesson to everyone in the village. The good girl lived a long and peaceful life, sharing her fortune and kindness with those who needed it most. Mother Holle continued to watch over the mortal world from her hidden home, ensuring that the seasons changed in their proper time. And so the village remained a place of quiet harmony, where every child learned that genuine effort and a pure heart always find their true reward.

## st-0167 — Snow White and the Seven Dwarfs
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Snow White lives in a big house. She has a mother. The mother looks at a thing. The thing is a special object. The object says Snow White is good. The mother is very bad now. She asks a man. The man takes Snow White away.

The good man does not stop her. He tells her to run far away. She walks into the night. She finds a small house. Seven small men live there. They let her stay with them. Snow White helps them every day. She does work for the men. They like her very much.

The mother knows Snow White is well. She wants to play a game. The mother looks like an old woman. She gives Snow White food. The food is bad. Snow White eats it. She falls down on the ground. She does not move at all. She seems gone now.

The seven men come home. They see their friend on the ground. They are very sad. They put her in a clear case. They leave the case on a high place. Forest people come to look at her.

A young man comes through the night. He sees the girl in the case. He loves her right now. He asks to put his hand. He puts it on her face. He moves her hand. The bad food part falls out. Snow White opens her eyes. Everyone is very happy today.

### A2
Long ago, a very beautiful girl named Snow White lived in a big house. She lived there with a woman who had a special glass that showed truth. The special glass was able to speak to her every day. It always gave a clear answer about who was the most beautiful person. One day, the glass told the woman that Snow White was more beautiful. Because of this, the woman wanted to send the girl away. She asked a man to take Snow White deep into the forest.

However, the kind man could not kill Snow White at all. He told her to run far away from the house. The poor girl walked through the dark trees. She found a small house owned by seven friendly little men. The seven little men welcomed her inside the room. They let her stay with them in their nice home. Snow White helped them clean the rooms every day. She also prepared food for them.

During this time, the woman learned from her special glass that Snow White was still breathing. The woman decided to play a game with the girl. She dressed up as an old person. She went to the little house and gave Snow White a bad food item to eat. As soon as Snow White bit the item, she fell down to the floor. She seemed completely dead.

When the seven little men returned home that evening, they were very unhappy. They found their good friend resting silent on the floor. They built a special box for her because she looked so lovely. The little men placed the box on a green land. They could watch over her all the time. Animals of the forest also came around to visit the sleeping girl.

After some time, a nice man traveled through the forest. He saw the beautiful young girl inside the box. He fell in love with her immediately. He asked the little men if he could touch her face. When the nice man moved her with care, the bad piece of food fell out from her open lips. Snow White opened her eyes and stood up at last. This made everyone very happy.

### B1
Long ago, a very nice girl named Snow White lived in a big house with an angry woman. The woman had a special device. The device always told her the truth about who was the most beautiful person. One day, the device told the woman that Snow White was much more lovely than herself. Because of this, the bad woman wanted to get away from the young girl. She asked a man to take her into the deep forest. She wanted to be sure that the girl would never return to the house again. The man agreed to help her because she was very powerful and dangerous. They walked for many hours until they reached a very dark place. Snow White felt afraid and did not know what would happen to her. The man looked at her and felt sorry because she was so young and sweet. He knew that he could not kill someone so pure.

He told her to run far away from the house. The poor girl walked through the dark woods until she found a small building. This building belonged to seven friendly men. The seven men welcomed her inside and let her stay with them in their warm home. Snow White helped them clean the rooms and cooked food for them every single day. They enjoyed her company and gave her a clean bed to sleep in every night. She felt happy and safe with her new friends in the deep forest. The men went out to work every morning and told her to stay safe inside. Snow White always locked the door and kept herself busy with house work. She washed the dishes and cleared the floors until everything looked wonderful and clean. The animals of the woods also came to visit her and brought her small gifts. She sang songs with the birds and played games with the little dogs.

Meanwhile, the woman learned from her special device that Snow White was still alive. She knew the girl was living in the forest. The angry person decided to play with the girl herself. She dressed up as an old person to fool the young child. She went to the little house and gave Snow White bad food to eat. As soon as Snow White bit the fruit, she fell down to the floor. She seemed completely dead. The bad woman smiled and walked away quickly back to her large house. She thought that she had finally won and that no one would ever know the truth. The device was quiet and did not say anything else about beauty that day. The woman felt very proud of her bad plan and sat down to rest. She hoped that the forest animals would take care of the body so she could forget everything.

When the seven men returned home that evening, they were extremely sad. They found their dear friend down on the floor. They built a special glass box for her. She looked so lovely even in death. The men placed the glass box on a green hill. They could watch over her all the time from their small home. Animals of the forest also came around to visit the sleeping girl and share their feeling. The little birds sang sad songs every morning near the glass box. The animals stayed close to the hill and protected her day and night. None of the men could eat or sleep well because they missed her so much. They kept fresh flowers around the box to show how much they loved their friend. Weeks passed by and her face still looked as beautiful as the day she fell down.

After some time, a nice man traveled through the forest. He saw the beautiful lady inside the glass box. He fell in love with her immediately and asked the men if he could touch her face. The friendly men allowed him to come close to the special box. When the man moved her, the piece of bad food fell out from her mouth. Snow White opened her eyes and woke up at last. This made everyone wonderfully happy. The men jumped and shouted with happiness because their dear friend was alive again. The nice man smiled and asked her to travel with him to his wonderful home. Snow White agreed and thanked the seven men for saving her life. They all held each other and celebrated this amazing event together. The bad woman never bothered them again, and they lived happily ever after.

### B2
Long ago, a very beautiful girl named Snow White lived in a grand castle. She lived with a woman who felt bad about herself. The woman possessed a magic mirror which always told the truth about who was the fairest person in the world. One day, the magic mirror told the woman that Snow White was much more beautiful than herself. Because of this, the angry woman wanted to get rid of the young girl. She asked a hunter to take her into the deep forest. She wanted him to make sure that the girl did not return to the castle ever again. The woman gave him strict orders because she was so jealous of the child. Every single morning, the queen would look into the glass and ask the same question. The mirror would answer with the same truth, and the queen would become even more angry. She could not stand the fact that someone else might be more lovely than her own reflection. Therefore, the cruel queen made up her mind to destroy the innocent girl. She believed that her life would be much better if Snow White was completely gone from the world. The hunter had to obey the queen because she held all the power in the royal house. He took the young girl away from the grand halls and led her deep into the green woods.

However, the kind hunter could not kill Snow White. He looked at her sweet face and felt a lot of pity for her. He told her to run away far from the castle. The poor girl walked through the dark woods until she found a small house belonging to seven friendly small men. The seven small men welcomed her inside. They let her stay with them in their sweet home. Snow White helped them clean the rooms every single day. She cooked food for them as well. The small men were very glad that she came to live with them in the forest. They worked in the mountains during the day and returned home in the evening. Snow White would prepare a warm meal and ensure the house was clean before they arrived back. They shared many stories together and lived in peace for a long time. The little men told her that she should never open the door to strangers while they were away. Snow White promised that she would listen to their good advice. She felt safe with her new friends in the cozy little cottage among the trees.

Meanwhile, the woman learned from her magic mirror that Snow White was still alive. The mirror showed her that the girl was living in the forest with the seven small men. The bad woman decided to trick the girl herself. She dressed up as an old seller. She went to the little house while the men were out working. She gave Snow White a bad fruit to eat. As soon as Snow White bit the fruit, she fell down to the floor. She seemed completely dead after that moment. The wicked queen smiled because she thought her plan had finally worked. She left the little house quickly and returned to her grand castle before anyone could see her. The forest became very quiet as the sun went down behind the high hills. The birds stopped singing, and the wind blew softly through the leaves of the tall trees. Everything was still in the woods while the poor girl lay motionless on the wooden floor. The dark night covered the little house like a heavy blanket. No one knew what had happened inside the small room during those quiet hours.

When the seven small men returned home that evening, they were extremely sad. They found their dear friend down on the floor and not moving at all. They cried and held her hands, but she did not wake up. They built a special glass box for her. She looked so lovely even in death. The small men placed the glass box on a green hill. They could watch over her all the time. Animals of the forest also came around to visit the sleeping girl. They came to share their sadness with each other. The little birds sang quiet songs of grief near the glass box every morning. The gentle deer stood by the hill and looked at Snow White with sad eyes. No one wanted to leave the beautiful girl alone on the green grass. The small men stayed close to the box day and night to protect her from any danger. They hoped that some miracle might happen to bring their dear friend back to life. Her skin remained as white as snow, and her lips stayed red like a rose.

After some time, a nice young man traveled through the forest. He saw the beautiful young lady inside the glass box. He fell in love with her immediately. He asked the small men if he could touch her face. When the young man moved her gently, the piece of bad fruit fell out from her mouth. Snow White opened her eyes and woke up at last. This made everyone wonderfully happy in the end. The prince smiled down at her and thanked the small men for keeping her safe. They celebrated together on the green hill where the glass box had rested for months. The animals of the forest danced around them in pure joy. Snow White got up and hugged her tiny friends with all her heart. She climbed onto the horse with the handsome prince and waved goodbye to the little men. They rode away toward the bright castle where a new life waited for them. The wicked queen never bothered anyone ever again, and peace returned to the land. Snow White and the prince lived happily together for the rest of their days.

### C1
Long ago, within the austere stone walls of a grand castle, lived a remarkably beautiful young girl whose name was Snow White. She shared her opulent yet cold existence with a woman whose heart was consumed by bitterness and a fragile sense of self-worth. At the center of the queen's troubled mind lay an ornate magic mirror, an artifact that never offered false comfort. Every single morning, the vain queen would approach the glass and pose the very same question regarding who held the greatest beauty in the land. So long as the mirror answered that she alone possessed the finest countenance, the queen remained content within her gilded cage.

One fateful morning, however, the silver surface rippled and delivered a verdict that shattered the queen's fragile world. The mirror declared that Snow White, young and innocent, had surpassed the queen in grace and fairness. A dark, corrosive jealousy took root within the royal chambers, poisoning the queen's thoughts day and night. She could no longer bear the existence of someone whose mere reflection mocked her own fading youth. Therefore, the cruel sovereign resolved to eliminate the girl, believing that only absolute erasure could restore her peace of mind.

Summoning a trusted hunter from the royal guard, the queen issued strict and merciless orders. He was commanded to lead the young girl deep into the untamed forest and ensure she never returned to the castle. Bound by the absolute power the queen held over the household, the troubled hunter had no choice but to obey. He approached Snow White with feigned casualness, inviting her to walk with him beneath the ancient canopy of green trees. The unsuspecting girl went along gladly, unaware of the grim purpose that had drawn them away from the safety of the halls.

Yet, as they penetrated deeper into the shadowy woods, the hunter found his resolve crumbling before the girl's gentle innocence. He looked upon her sweet face and felt a profound wave of pity wash over his troubled conscience. Dropping his gaze, he urged her to flee as far away from the castle as her feet could carry her. The frightened girl turned and ran blindly through the thick undergrowth, driven by a sudden, desperate instinct for survival. She traversed the dark and tangled woods for hours until the sprawling branches finally parted to reveal a modest, welcoming dwelling.

This secluded cottage belonged to seven industrious small men who mined the surrounding mountains for precious stones. Upon returning from their daily labor, the friendly inhabitants discovered the exhausted girl resting near their threshold. Moved by her plight, they welcomed her inside with open arms and offered their humble home as a safe sanctuary. Snow White quickly adapted to her new life, finding profound comfort in the simple routines of domesticity. Every day, she swept the wooden floors, dusted the corners, and prepared hearty meals for the tired workers.

The seven small men were immensely grateful for her warm presence, and the cottage soon echoed with laughter and shared stories. Peace settled over the little woodland home, shielding them all from the bitter winds of the outside world. Before leaving for the mountains each morning, however, the cautious men imparted a vital piece of advice. They warned her never to open the heavy wooden door to any stranger while they were away laboring in the deep mines. Snow White promised to heed their prudent words, feeling entirely secure within the embrace of her new friends.

Meanwhile, back in the grand castle, the queen's curiosity drove her once more to consult the polished surface of her magic glass. To her fury, the mirror revealed that Snow White was not only alive but thriving deep within the forest. Realizing the hunter had betrayed her, the wicked woman decided to take matters into her own hands. She disguised herself as an elderly peddler, concealing her royal identity beneath a frayed cloak and a wrinkled mask. Carrying a basket laden with deceptive goods, she journeyed through the winding forest paths until she reached the isolated cottage.

Finding the girl alone inside, the cunning visitor offered her a vibrant, sweet-smelling fruit with a deceptively alluring shine. Snow White hesitated briefly, but the deceptive charm of the old woman lulled her lingering doubts into silence. The moment the unsuspecting girl bit into the forbidden fruit, a dark numbness seized her limbs. She collapsed instantly onto the wooden floor, her eyes closing as the spark of life seemingly left her frail body. A triumphant smile crossed the wicked queen's face as she slipped away into the thickening shadows before anyone could return.

Evening descended upon the forest, wrapping the silent cottage in a heavy blanket of stillness and profound sorrow. When the seven small men returned from the mountains, they found their beloved friend motionless upon the floor. They wept bitterly, holding her cold hands and calling her name, but no breath of life answered their desperate pleas. Unable to lower her into the dark earth, they constructed an exquisite box of clear glass to preserve her radiant beauty. They carried the glass coffin to a verdant hillside, where they could watch over her vigilantly day and night.

The sorrow of her passing touched even the wild creatures of the forest, who gathered daily near the resting place. Little birds sang mournful melodies each morning, while gentle deer stood nearby with soft, sorrowful eyes. Her skin retained its snowy paleness, and her lips remained as vibrant as a red rose, defying the usual cruelties of death. Months passed in this quiet vigil until a wandering young prince happened to ride through the shaded woods. Catching sight of the breathtaking lady within the glass enclosure, he was instantly struck by a profound and overwhelming love.

He begged the mourning companions for permission to touch her face, marveling at the unnatural grace of her form. As the young man gently shifted her position, the lodged piece of poisoned fruit slipped from her mouth. Color rushed back to her cheeks as Snow White opened her eyes, drawing a deep breath after her long slumber. Joy erupted on the green hill as the small men cheered and embraced their miraculously revived friend. The prince thanked the loyal guardians, placed Snow White upon his majestic horse, and rode away toward a future of enduring peace.
