## st-0168 — Little Red Riding Hood
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A little girl live with her mother. People call her Little Red Riding Hood. She has a color thing on. Her mother give her food. She must bring food to her old friend. Old friend live down in the big area. Mother tell her to stay on one way. Mother say do not talk to people.

Little Red Riding Hood walk through big wood. A big wolf appear on way. He asks where she go. She do not know he be bad. She tells him about house. The big wolf tell her to get color thing. The wolf say get color thing now.

She get color thing in the forest. The wolf run to the house. He comes into the small room. He put old friend in a small room. He put on her things. He wait in bed.

Little Red Riding Hood come to house. She sees old friend in bed. Something look very different. She asks about big eye and face. The bad wolf move up fast. He says face is for eating her.

The little girl cry and run. The wolf run after her. A good man hear the sound. He comes to the house. He helps the little girl. The big wolf run away.

She let old friend out of small room. They thank the good man. She learns an important thing. She stays on the right way.

### A2
Once upon a time, a little girl lived near a big forest. She wore a red dress. People called her Little Red Riding Hood. One morning, her mother gave her food for her old family member. That family member lived deep in the area. Her mother told her to stay on the track. Her mother told her not to talk to people.

Little Red Riding Hood walked through the trees. She enjoyed the morning light. Soon, a big animal appeared on the track. He asked where she was going. She did not know the animal was bad. Then she told him about the house. The animal suggested that she look at lovely plants.

The little girl picked plants in the forest. The animal ran to the house. He hit the door and entered the room. He put the poor old woman in a small room. Then he put on her clothes. He waited patiently in the bed.

After some time, Little Red Riding Hood arrived at the house. She hit the door. She walked inside and saw her family member in bed. Her face seemed very strange. She asked why her family member had big eyes, side parts of the head, and face parts. The bad animal moved quickly out of the bed. He said those face parts were for eating her.

The girl felt worry and ran around the room. The animal ran after her. A strong man heard the high sound. He came to the house and saw the issue. He helped the little girl and saved her. The animal ran away into the dark forest. He never returned to trouble anyone again.

Little Red Riding Hood opened the small room. She let her happy family member out. They thanked the strong man for his help. The little girl learned an important study point. She learned about listening to her mother and staying safe. From that day, she always followed the right track.

### B1
Once upon a time, a little girl lived near a big forest with her mother. She always wore a red dress. People called her Little Red Riding Hood. One morning, her mother gave her some food. She had to bring food to her old parent. This parent lived deep in the woods. Before leaving, the mother told the child to stay on the path. She told her to avoid talking to people she did not know. Little Red Riding Hood walked happily through the trees. She enjoyed the bright morning sun. Soon, a big animal appeared on the path. He asked where she was going. Because she did not know that the animal was dangerous, she spoke to him. She told him about her family house. The smart animal suggested that she look at the lovely flowers growing nearby. While the little girl picked flowers in the forest, the fast animal ran directly to the house. He knocked on the door and entered the small room without any trouble. The animal quickly locked the poor old woman in a small room. He did this so that she would not make any noise. Then he put on her clothes and waited calmly in the bed. After some time, Little Red Riding Hood arrived at the house. She knocked on the hard door. She walked inside and saw her family in bed. Something about the face seemed very strange. She asked why her family had such big eyes. She asked why her family had such big ears. She asked why her family had such big mouths. The bad animal jumped out of the bed. He said that those parts were for eating her.

The scared girl cried and ran around the room. The animal ran after her. A good hunter who was working nearby heard the strong noise. He came to the house quickly. He saw the danger. He helped the little girl. He saved her from the bad animal. The animal ran away into the dark forest. He never returned to trouble anyone again. Little Red Riding Hood opened the small room and let her happy family out. They thanked the good hunter for his wonderful help and support. The little girl learned an important lesson about listening to her mother. She learned about staying safe. From that day forward, she always followed the right path through the woods. If she walks carefully, she will never meet another bad animal again. She has understood that safety is important. Her mother has been very proud of her ever since that day. They have lived quietly in their home near the woods.

### B2
Once upon a time, a little girl lived near a big forest with her mother. She always wore a red dress, which made people call her Little Red Riding Hood. One morning, her mother gave her some food to bring to her old grandmother who lived deep in the woods. Before leaving, the mother told the child to stay on the path and to avoid talking to strangers. Little Red Riding Hood walked happily through the trees, enjoying the bright morning sun. Soon, a big animal appeared on the path and asked where she was going. Because she did not know that the creature was dangerous, she told him about her grandmother house. The smart animal suggested that she look at the lovely flowers growing nearby. While the little girl picked flowers in the forest, the fast animal ran directly to the grandmother house. He knocked on the door and entered the small room without any trouble. The animal quickly locked the poor old woman in a room so that she would not make any noise. Then he put on her clothes and waited patiently in the bed.

After some time, Little Red Riding Hood arrived at the house and knocked on the wooden door. She walked inside and saw her grandmother in bed, but something about the face seemed very strange. She asked why her grandmother had such big eyes, big ears, and big teeth. The bad animal jumped out of the bed and said that those teeth were for eating her. The frightened girl screamed and ran around the room while the animal chased her. A good hunter who was working nearby heard the loud noise and came to the house. He saw the danger, quickly helped the little girl, and saved her from the bad animal. The animal ran away into the dark forest and never returned to trouble anyone again. Little Red Riding Hood opened the room and let her happy grandmother out. They thanked the good hunter for his wonderful help and support. The little girl learned an important lesson about listening to her mother and staying safe. From that day forward, she always followed the right path through the woods.

The sun was shining brightly as the child walked home with her family member. They spoke about the strange events of the morning while they moved along the quiet road. The mother was waiting near the garden when she saw them coming back. She held her daughter tightly because she was so glad that everything was fine. The grandmother made a nice meal for everyone to share in the evening. They sat together around the table and enjoyed the good food. The little girl promised that she would never speak to strangers again. Her mother smiled and told her that listening was the best way to stay safe. The hunter went back to his work in the deep woods, feeling happy about his good work. The bad animal never came back to bother the village or the people living there. Years passed, but the story was remembered by everyone in the community. Children were told the tale so that they would always follow the right path. Life continued well near the big forest, and everyone lived without any further fear. The red dress remained the favorite clothing of the young girl as she grew older. She always remembered the lesson she learned on that important day. Whenever she walked through the trees, she looked around carefully to ensure everything was secure. The birds were singing lovely songs high above the green branches. Nature seemed quiet and welcoming after the strange adventure. The wind blew softly through the leaves, making a quiet and pleasant sound. Little Red Riding Hood smiled to herself and thought about her wonderful grandmother. She knew that care was an important part of growing up. The memory of the event stayed with her throughout her entire life. She never forgot the help provided by the friendly hunter in her time of need. Every time she visited the old woman, they shared stories and drank warm tea together. The bond between them became even stronger after that morning. Nothing could ever disturb their quiet days in the little house near the woods. And so, the story became a famous lesson that was passed down through generations. People everywhere learned to value safety and listen to the advice of their parents. The path through the forest remained open for anyone who wished to travel safely. Little Red Riding Hood grew into a wise person who always made good choices.

### C1
Nestled on the rugged periphery of an ancient, brooding forest, a modest cottage sheltered a young girl and her devoted mother. Because she invariably wore a vibrant cloak of scarlet wool complete with an attached hood, the villagers universally referred to her as Little Red Riding Hood. One crisp morning, her mother arranged a small basket laden with freshly baked bread and a jar of sweet butter. She instructed her daughter to carry these provisions to her ailing grandmother, whose dwelling lay deep within the tangled labyrinth of the woods. Before the child departed, her mother offered a stern caution, reminding her to remain strictly upon the established path and to ignore the temptations of strangers.

Stepping out into the crisp morning air, the girl felt the warm sunlight filtering through the dense canopy of emerald leaves. She hummed a cheerful melody, her spirits light as she navigated the winding dirt track that cut through the towering timber. Seldom had she ventured this far alone, and the sheer vitality of the forest intoxicated her youthful senses. Without warning, a large silhouette materialized upon the narrow path, blocking her advance with an air of deceptive casualness. It was a cunning wolf, though the naive child perceived no menace in his gleaming eyes or polished posture.

With a voice as smooth as polished stone, the creature inquired where her hurried journey might be taking her. Oblivious to the peril lurking beneath his polite exterior, the trusting girl divulged the exact destination of her elderly grandmother. Scarcely had she spoken when the calculating animal suggested that she pause to admire the radiant wildflowers blooming just off the path. He painted a vivid picture of the sunlit clearing nearby, urging her to gather a fragrant bouquet for the ailing woman. Captivated by the notion, the girl immediately abandoned the safety of the trail and plunged into the underbrush in search of blossoms.

Seizing the opportunity, the swift predator sprinted through the dense undergrowth with ruthless efficiency. He bypassed the winding trail entirely, racing directly toward the secluded cottage of the unsuspecting grandmother. Upon reaching the rustic wooden door, he rapped his sharp claws against the timber and slipped inside without encountering the slightest resistance. The terrified elderly woman barely had time to react before the intruder overpowered her, confining her securely within a small adjoining pantry. Intent upon his deception, the cunning animal donned her nightcap and spectacles, settling into the feather bed to await his primary target.

Not long afterward, Little Red Riding Hood emerged from the trees and approached the familiar threshold of her grandmother's cottage. Finding the front door standing slightly ajar, she stepped cautiously into the dim interior, calling out a gentle greeting. Receiving no clear response, she drew closer to the bed, struck instantly by a profound and unsettling dissonance. Her grandmother appeared remarkably altered, possessing features far coarser and more exaggerated than the child remembered. Trembling slightly, the girl questioned why her relative possessed such unusually large eyes, such remarkably long ears, and such terrifyingly sharp teeth.

With a sudden burst of violent motion, the impostor cast off the bedclothes and sprang toward the terrified child. He declared in a booming, mocking tone that those formidable teeth were intended specifically to consume her whole. Panic overriding her senses, the young girl shrieked in terror, darting wildly around the cramped quarters as the predator pursued her. Desperation fueled her flight, yet the agile beast closed the distance with terrifying speed, threatening to seal her fate. Just as the situation reached its absolute zenith of despair, the heavy oak door creaked open to reveal a local woodcutter.

The vigilant hunter had been working nearby when the sharp sounds of distress penetrated the heavy timber walls. Recognizing the unmistakable aura of danger, he had rushed to the cottage with his heavy axe held firmly in his grasp. Taking in the chaotic scene at a single glance, he intervened decisively, driving the aggressive beast away from the trembling child. Realizing that he was severely outmatched, the cowardly predator abandoned his prize and bolted through the open doorway. He vanished into the shadowy depths of the forest, retreating hastily from the wrath of the armed protector.

Once the immediate peril had subsided, the brave girl hurried to unlock the pantry door and release her trembling grandmother. The two embraced fiercely, shedding tears of relief as the profound magnitude of their narrow escape settled over them. Turning to their rescuer, they expressed their deepest gratitude, thanking him profusely for his timely and courageous intervention. The woodcutter offered a reassuring smile before departing quietly back to his labor among the towering trees. Standing amidst the wreckage of the cottage, the young girl absorbed the bitter weight of the lesson she had just learned.

As the afternoon sun began its slow descent, the child walked homeward in the company of her cherished relative. They conversed in hushed tones about the harrowing events that had transformed their quiet morning into a waking nightmare. Waiting anxiously by the garden gate, the mother caught sight of them approaching and rushed forward with open arms. She enveloped her daughter in a crushing embrace, weeping with sheer thankfulness that the ordeal had ended in safety. That very evening, the grandmother prepared a hearty meal, and the family gathered around the wooden table to restore their shattered equilibrium.

The young girl offered a solemn vow that she would never again converse with strangers or stray from the designated route. Her mother nodded approvingly, emphasizing that obedience and vigilance formed the twin pillars of personal safety. Years drifted by, yet the cautionary tale remained indelibly etched into the collective memory of the entire village. Whenever the growing maiden walked beneath the leafy canopy, she maintained a vigilant watch over her surroundings. The vibrant red cloak remained her favorite garment, serving as a permanent reminder of the day she nearly lost everything.

No longer naive, she understood that the world beyond the village boundary required both respect and caution. The forest retained its breathtaking beauty, but it was now viewed through a lens of profound maturity and awareness. She frequently visited her grandmother, sharing warm tea and endless conversations that deepened their familial bond. Peace returned permanently to the cottage near the woods, undisturbed by the shadow of the departed beast. Thus, the legendary event became a timeless heritage, passed down through generations to ensure that children would always heed the wisdom of their parents.

## st-0169 — Rapunzel
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Long ago. A young girl live in a high place. She has no door. She stays in the room all day. An old woman visit her. The woman move up her long head part. She do this every day. The girl look out of the place. She wait for the day to end. The room is small. She has no friend there. She looks at the land. She stays quiet. The old woman bring food. The girl take the food. She eats and drink. She has no other person to see. The sun go down. She goes to sleep.

One day, a man walk near. He hear the girl give sound. He wants to meet her. He watch the woman get in. The woman leave the place. The man call out to the girl. The girl bring her head part down. The man move up into the room. They become friend. They decide to leave together. They talk a lot. They want a new life. The man hold her hand. The girl smile at him. They make a plan. They want to go home. The old woman find out. She sends the girl far away. The room is empty now. The man be sad.

The man return to the place. The old woman play a bad game. He fall from the high place. After a long time, he find the girl. She moves around in the land. They be very glad now. They finally go home together. The man see her face. He be very glad. The girl look at him. They hold each other. They never leave again. They live a good life. The old woman be not there. They stay home all day. They look at the sun. They feel very well. The story come to an end.

### A2
Long ago, a young girl lived in a high house. She had long hair. There was no door. She stayed inside all day. An old woman visited her. The woman moved up the hair. She went up every day. The girl lived alone in the room. She looked out through the window. She saw the green land. She sang a song each morning. Her voice was very nice. People could hear her sing. She hoped to see the world. She waited for a change. She wanted to be free. She did not have any friends. She only had the old woman. The old woman came often. She brought food and water. She always called the girl. The girl dropped her long hair down. The woman used the hair to get in. She was going to keep visiting her for many years. The girl stayed in the room and looked at the green land. She always waited for the old woman to bring food. The house was very high and had no door at all.

One day, a young man walked near the house. He heard the girl sing a song. He wanted to meet her. He watched the old woman. He saw how the woman got inside. When the woman left, the man called the girl. The girl dropped her long hair down. The man moved up into the room. They saw each other and became friends. They talked for a long time. They liked each other very much. They decided to leave together. They made a plan for the future. However, the old woman found out. She was very difficult. She sent the girl far away. The girl had to go to a different place. She felt very bad. The man was going to find her soon. They wanted to live together in a new place. The old woman did not want them to be happy. She made a plan to stop the young man.

The young man returned to the house. The old woman played a game with him. She made him fall. He fell down from the high house. He hit his eyes. He could not see anything. After a long time, he found the girl. She was walking in the green land. She heard a voice and turned around. She saw the man. They were very happy to see each other. They put arms around each other and spoke loud. They finally went home together. They lived happily after that day. They never went back to the high house. They were going to stay together forever. The man and woman were very glad to meet again. No one was going to make them sad anymore.

### B1
Long ago, a young girl with long hair lived in a high stone place. She had no door, so she stayed inside all day long. An old woman visited her by climbing up her hair. This happened every single week without fail. The girl lived without anyone else and had no friends around. She often looked out from the high stone place. She watched the world down below during the day. The old woman brought food and water to her. The girl always waited for the old woman to return. She never went outside during those quiet years. Her life was very safe and calm. She spent her time singing songs to the empty room. Her voice was very sweet and clear. She loved to sing about the green trees and blue sky. She has sung those sweet songs for many years.

One day, a young man walked near the high place. He heard the girl sing a lovely song. He wanted to meet her very much. He watched how the old woman got inside the room. The woman called out to the girl from the ground. The girl let her long hair fall down. The woman climbed up the hair into the room. The man understood the plan now. When the woman left, the man called out to the girl. The girl dropped her long hair down again. The man climbed up into the room. They saw each other and became friends. They talked for a long time. They decided to leave together soon. If they stay, they will never be free. The girl has hoped for a friend for a long time. They have made a good plan to escape the high place.

However, the old woman found out about the plan. She was very angry with them. She sent the girl far away to a far place. When the man returned, the old woman made a bad surprise for him. He fell from the high place and hurt himself badly. He could not see anything after that terrible fall. He spent many months searching for the girl. He walked through the woods every single day. He hoped to find her again. After a long time, he heard a familiar voice. He found the girl walking in the woods. They were very happy to see each other again. They finally went home together. They have lived happily ever after. They have never forgotten that hard time.

### B2

This process happened many times during each week. The girl often looked outside from her high window. She wanted to see the world beyond her room. He saw how the woman got inside the room. When the old woman left the tower, the young man walked closer. The young man called out to the girl in a soft voice. When he reached the top, he found the angry woman waiting. The old woman pushed him away from the window. He fell from the high tower down to the ground. After a long time, the young man found the girl again. She was wandering alone in the dark woods. They were extremely happy to see each other once more. They held each other close and cried tears of joy. They finally went home together and lived happily. Their long trial was finally at an end. They never returned to that terrible tower again. They shared a wonderful life with their family. Everyone in the country was glad that they were safe. The story of the tower became famous among the people.

### C1
Long ago, within the silent heart of an ancient forest, stood a solitary tower that possessed neither door nor stairway. Reaching high into the grey canopy, it kept its prisoner completely secluded from the world below. Inside this stone enclosure lived a young girl blessed with hair as radiant as spun gold and as long as a royal cloak. Because no architectural means existed to enter or exit, she remained confined within her elevated chamber day after day. Her sole visitor was an elderly woman who maintained access through a singular, bizarre method.

This process occurred multiple times throughout each week, defying the ordinary laws of nature and architecture. Whenever the woman approached the silent clearing, she would call out from the ground below with a sharp command. Hearing the familiar voice, the girl would release her heavy braids from the high window, letting them tumble down the stone wall. The old woman would then grasp the golden strands and haul herself upward, hand over hand, until she reached the sill. For several years, this unchanging routine defined the entire existence of the girl inside the tower.

Left alone for countless hours, the prisoner frequently gazed out from her high window at the vast, untouchable landscape. She desperately wanted to see the world beyond her room, yearning to walk among the trees and feel the earth beneath her feet. However, she could not leave because there was simply no way down from her perilous height. To pass the slow hours and soothe her aching spirit, she spent much of her time singing melodies that drifted softly through the leaves. Her voice possessed a rare sweetness, carrying a haunting clarity that could be heard from a considerable distance.

Travelers and peasants who walked near the edge of the deep forest often stopped abruptly to listen to the unseen singer. They wondered in hushed tones who could possibly live in such a strange and inaccessible place. The tower stood entirely alone, shrouded in mystery, and no local resident knew how to enter it properly. Only the old woman possessed the secret method that bridged the vast distance between the forest floor and the hidden window. The girl continued her quiet life of song and reflection, unaware that her solitude was about to be broken forever.

One afternoon, a young man happened to walk near the tower while exploring the dense and unfamiliar woods. He heard the girl singing her beautiful song, and the sheer grace of her voice instantly captured his heart. Determined to meet the mysterious singer, he searched for a way inside but found only cold, impenetrable stone. He hid discreetly behind some thick trees and waited patiently to see if anyone would approach the forbidden structure. Before long, he watched in astonishment as the old woman arrived and used the secret method to gain entry.

He observed the precise way the woman called out and how the golden hair cascaded down the sheer face of the tower. When the old woman finally descended and departed from the clearing, the young man stepped out from his hiding place. He walked closer to the base of the stone monument, summoning his courage before speaking in a soft, tentative voice. Hearing the unfamiliar tone, the girl instinctively dropped her long hair down from the high window as she always did. Seizing the golden strands, the young man hauled himself upward and climbed steadily into the forbidden room.

When they finally saw each other face to face, initial hesitation quickly melted away, replaced by profound relief and happiness. They became close companions almost instantly, sitting together for hours to share their thoughts, dreams, and quiet fears. During these extended conversations, they decided that they simply had to leave the tower together and escape their present reality. The girl desperately wanted to see new places with her new friend, longing to experience the freedom she had only imagined. They formulated a careful plan for their departure, hoping to slip away before the old woman returned.

However, through an unfortunate turn of events, the old woman eventually discovered their secret scheme. Filled with sudden rage and deep disappointment, she confronted the girl and made her pay a heavy price for her defiance. The angry woman sent the poor girl far away to an isolated wilderness, stripping her of the familiar surroundings she had known. The young girl had to leave everything behind, walking into exile with nothing but her courage and her memories. Later that very day, the unsuspecting young man returned to the tower, eager to reunite with his beloved companion.

Unaware of the tragedy that had transpired, the young man called out and was met by the silent lowering of the golden hair. The old woman had devised a bitter trick, waiting at the top of the stone wall with cold determination in her eyes. When the young man reached the summit and hoisted himself over the sill, he found the furious woman waiting for him. Before he could utter a single word, the old woman pushed him backward away from the safety of the window. He lost his footing and fell from the dizzying height of the tower all the way down to the hard ground below.

After a long and agonizing passage of time, during which he searched tirelessly across the region, the young man found the girl again. She was wandering alone and desolate through the dark, sprawling woods, bearing the scars of her harsh exile. When their eyes met across the dim clearing, they were overcome with an immense wave of joy and disbelief. They held each other close against the chill of the forest, crying tears of profound relief after years of separation. Their long trial was finally at an end, and the dark chapter of their lives closed for good.

They journeyed home together at last, settling down to build a peaceful life filled with quiet contentment. They never returned to that terrible tower, letting the stone monument crumble slowly into the forgotten past. Surrounded by family, they shared a wonderful and enduring life that rewarded their steadfast patience. Everyone in the country rejoiced to see them safe and thriving after such extraordinary hardships. The strange story of the forest tower eventually became a famous legend told among the people for generations.

## st-0170 — The Star Money
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
Once, a little girl have no mother. Her father also die. She has only one small room. She has no money. She be very kind. She gives food to other people. She helps people all day. She gives food again and again. She wants to help every person. She has a good heart.

One day, she walk in a place. An old man ask for food. She gives him her last food. She gives him all she have. She be happy to help him. She walks more on the land. She looks around every place. She wants to see more things. She thinks about the old man. She smile at the open sky.

Soon, she meet a little child. He be very low on heat. She gives him her top thing. Then another child ask for more. She gives her other thing too. She has no top thing now. She feels the wind on her. She still want to help people. She walks to a high place. She be very good to all.

Night come. She be in a tree area. She has no thing on her. Stars fall down from the high view. The stars become new things. They land around her on the land. She looks at the bright light. She feels the great change. She be very happy now. She takes the bright things.

The girl put on new things. She becomes a big person. She helps people in need. She lives a good life here. She never be low on money again. She gives food to many people. She walks in the town every day. She share her good things now. She helps every child she meet. She lives well until the end.

### A2
Once upon a time, a little girl had no mother and father. She was very poor. She had only one room and clothes. She was very kind. She gave food and money to poor people. She always wanted to help everyone around her. She was a good child. She lived alone in a small house. She did not have much, but she gave what she had. People liked her because she was nice. She walked through the town every day. She saw many people who needed help. She gave them her food and her money.

One day, the good child walked alone in a field. She had no home. An old man met her. He asked her for food. She gave him her last food. She wished to help him more. The old man took the food and thanked her. She smiled and kept walking. She felt happy inside. She knew she did a good thing. The light went down, and the air became cold. She was far from any town.

Soon, she met a little boy. He cried because his head and hands were cold. She gave him her top clothes. He felt fine again. Later, another child asked for her dress. She gave that away too. She stood there with almost no clothes left. The wind moved hard. The night was dark and cold. She did not mind the cold much. She was happy to help the children. She looked up at the dark air above.

Night came. She was in a dark forest. She had no clothes and no food. Suddenly, stars fell from above. The stars were like fine money. They turned into fine new clothes around her. The happy girl took the fine items. She put on the fine clothes. She became very well off. She helped everyone she met. She lived a good life. She was never poor again.

### B1
Once upon a time, a very poor little girl had no father and no mother. She had nothing left in the world except one small room and her clothes. Because she was kind, she gave away every piece of food and money. She gave everything to poor people who had nothing to eat. She always wanted to help everyone around her. She felt glad when she could share her things. The kind child walked out into a field alone because she had no home. An old man met her on the road and asked her for something to eat. She gave him her last bit of food, wishing that she could help him more.

Soon after that, she met a little boy who was crying because he was cold. She took off her coat and gave it to him. She did this so that he would feel warm. A little further down the path, another child asked for her dress. She gave that dress away too. She walked on with nothing left to keep her warm. When night came, she found herself in a dark forest. She had no clothes and no food left in the world. Suddenly, stars began to fall down from the sky above her.

The stars fell like bright white money. She looked up and saw that the stars were turning into fine new clothes around her. The glad girl quickly gathered up the wonderful gifts. These gifts had been sent from high above. She put on the finest clothes and became a rich person. She was now able to help everyone who needed her support. From that day forward, she lived a good life. She was never poor again. She always remembered to help people in need.

Many years passed by while she lived her new life. She built a big house for children who had no home. She gave them food and warm clothes every single day. The old man who met her on the road would have been proud of her. The little boy who was cold now had a warm coat and a good bed. Everyone in the country knew about her kind heart. She always told them that sharing is the most important thing in life. If you give what you have, you will always find peace. Her story became famous across the land.

People came from distant towns just to see her house. They wanted to learn how to be as kind as she was. She welcomed every person with a warm smile and open arms. No one ever left her door without a gift or food. She spent her days working for the community. The children grew up and learned to help others too. A new generation of kind people started to live in the country. The spirit of the poor little girl lived on always. Her wonderful journey proved that love is stronger than money.

### B2
Once upon a time, there was a very poor little girl whose father and mother had both died. She had nothing left in the world except one small room and the clothes upon her back. Because she was so nice, she gave away every piece of bread which she had to other people. She also gave away every bit of money which she possessed to anyone who asked her. She did this because her heart was full of love and care for every human being. She wanted to make sure that no person suffered while she had something to share. Her life was hard, but she always found a way to help those who needed support. She never kept anything for herself, believing that sharing was the best thing to do. Every single day, she walked around the town and looked for individuals in difficult situations. Whenever she saw someone who lacked food, she gave them whatever she had in her hands. Her gentle nature made her well known among the local residents who respected her greatly. Although she had very little, her giving spirit was larger than any house in the city. She believed that being good to others mattered more than having wealth or possessions. Her parents had taught her to be nice before they passed away from this life. She remembered their words every time she made a decision to help a stranger. Day after day, she continued this wonderful habit without showing any sign of sadness. She felt happy whenever she brought a smile to the face of a tired person. Nothing could stop her from doing good acts because her spirit was strong and pure. She lived through many difficult months, yet her warm care remained constant and true. People often wondered how she managed to survive with such a giving and open heart.

One day, the good child walked out into a field alone, because she had no home left. An old man met her on the road and asked her for something to eat. She gave him her last bit of food without any pause, wishing that she could help him more. The old man thanked her with a warm smile and walked away into the distance. She stood there for a moment and felt glad that the old man had food now. Soon after that, she met a little boy who was crying because his head was cold. She took off her coat and gave it to him, so that he would feel warm. The boy put on the coat and stopped crying immediately as the cold wind left him. She smiled at the child and watched him run off to play with his friends. A little further down the path, another child asked for her dress. Without thinking twice, she gave that away too and remained with very few clothes. She did not care about the cold wind that began to blow across the open field. Her mind was at peace because she had made three children feel safe and happy. She continued walking down the long road as the afternoon turned into evening. The sun went down behind the hills, and the shadows grew longer across the ground. She knew that she would have to spend the night outside under the open sky. She was not afraid of the dark because she had done nothing wrong in her life. Her gentle actions gave her the strength to keep moving forward despite everything. She trusted that something good would happen to her before the morning came. Her belief in doing good kept her going when the path became hard to see.

When night came, she found herself in a dark forest with no clothes and no food left. Suddenly, stars began to fall down from the sky above her like bright silver money. She looked up and saw that the stars were turning into fine new clothes. The clothes landed softly around her on the dark ground of the quiet forest. The happy girl quickly gathered up the wonderful gifts which had been sent from above. She put on the finest clothes and became a rich person who could help everyone. From that day forward, she lived a good life and was never poor again. She used her new wealth to build a large house where poor people could stay. Many individuals came to her door, and she welcomed every single one of them inside. She provided them with warm food, clean clothes, and a safe place to sleep. Her name became famous throughout the country as a symbol of true love. Children and adults together visited her home to share their stories and seek advice. She listened to everyone with calm strength and gave them the support they required. Her heart remained just as nice as it had been when she owned nothing. She never forgot the hard days when she walked the roads without a coat. Instead, those memories made her even more giving toward people in need. Years passed by, but her reputation for doing good never faded away. When she finally reached old age, she looked back at her life with total satisfaction. She knew that her choices had made the world a much better place for everyone. Her story was told from generation to generation as a wonderful example of love.

### C1
In a world shadowed by grief and quiet destitution, there lived a young orphan girl whose parents had departed this life, leaving her with nothing save for one barren chamber and the simple garments upon her back. Bereft of worldly goods, she possessed an abundance of spirit, her heart overflowing with a boundless capacity for empathy towards every living soul. It was her firm conviction that no human being should endure suffering while she retained even the smallest fragment to share. Whenever she walked through the cobbled streets of the town, her eyes scanned the shadows for those weighed down by misfortune. She routinely surrendered the very crust of bread from her pocket and every copper coin she owned to anyone who asked.

Her gentle disposition soon earned her the deep respect of the local residents, who watched in quiet wonder as she moved among them. Though her own life was marked by bitter hardship, her altruism remained steadfast, stretching wider than the wealthiest estate in the city. She believed implicitly that moral integrity and kindness outweighed any accumulation of gold or material treasures. Her departed parents had instilled these virtues deeply within her soul before they passed away from this earthly existence. Whenever she faced a difficult choice, she recalled their parting words and chose the path of compassion.

Day after day, she maintained this remarkable routine without a single murmur of discontent or a trace of sorrow. A profound joy invariably bloomed within her chest whenever she coaxed even a fleeting smile onto the face of a weary laborer. Nothing could deter her from performing acts of quiet grace, for her internal resolve was as pure as it was unyielding. She endured months of biting cold and gnawing hunger, yet her warmth toward others never once flickered. Neighbors often marveled at how she managed to survive day by day with a spirit so utterly unguarded and open.

Eventually, the inevitable arrived, and she was forced to abandon her last shelter, stepping out alone into an open field. The sky above was heavy with grey clouds, and a bitter wind swept across the empty landscape as she began her solitary journey. It was along this desolate road that she encountered an elderly man whose posture was bent heavily beneath the weight of years. He approached her with trembling hands and asked if she might spare a morsel of food to quiet his rumbling stomach. Without a moment of hesitation, she handed him her very last piece of bread, wishing with all her soul that she had more to offer.

The old man accepted the gift with a tender smile, offering his gratitude before turning to walk away into the gathering mist. She stood motionless on the path for a long moment, feeling a quiet surge of gladness that the traveler's immediate hunger was now eased. Scarcely had she resumed walking when she encountered a small boy weeping bitterly beside a stone wall. He cried out that his head and shoulders were freezing in the sharp wind, and that he had no protection against the frost. Without a second thought, she slipped her woolen coat from her shoulders and wrapped it snugly around the shivering child.

The moment the heavy fabric settled over him, the boy stopped crying, the biting chill banished from his small frame. She offered him a reassuring smile and watched as he scrambled away with a newfound energy to join his companions in the distance. Continuing down the winding path, she had walked only a little further when another child timidly asked for her dress. Though the afternoon air was turning rapidly to ice, she did not hesitate to part with the garment, leaving herself clad in almost nothing. She cared little for the wind that now cut straight through her remaining layers, her mind anchored in a deep, serene peace.

She had successfully warmed three vulnerable children, and that knowledge served as an invisible cloak against the elements. Onward she walked as the autumn afternoon slowly dissolved into a bruised and darkening evening. The sun dipped below the distant hills, stretching long, skeletal shadows across the frosted grass of the open fields. She knew with absolute certainty that she would be forced to spend the coming night shivering beneath the open sky. Yet fear found no purchase in her heart, for she had lived honorably and caused harm to no living creature.

Her virtuous actions provided her with an inner fortitude that sustained her steps when the horizon vanished entirely from view. She trusted implicitly that some unseen grace would intervene before the cold light of dawn broke across the world. When complete darkness finally swallowed the landscape, she found herself deep within the silent shadows of a vast forest. She possessed neither food nor proper clothing, utterly exposed to the chill of the midnight woods. Suddenly, a wondrous phenomenon occurred above her as the velvet ceiling of the night sky seemed to fracture.

Bright stars began to detach themselves from the firmament, tumbling downward through the branches like coins of pure silver. As she looked upward in breathless wonder, she realized those falling lights were transforming into garments of the finest silk and wool. The rich clothes drifted downward through the quiet air, landing softly upon the dark, mossy earth around her feet. With trembling hands, the astonished girl gathered the celestial gifts and dressed herself in attire fit for a queen. Transformed in an instant, she was no longer a destitute outcast, but a person of abundant means who could finally heal the world around her.

From that remarkable night onward, she lived a life of great influence, ensuring that poverty never touched her threshold again. She utilized her newfound wealth to construct a sprawling manor where the homeless and the hungry could always find sanctuary. Countless travelers came to her heavy oak doors, and she welcomed every single soul inside with genuine hospitality. She provided them with steaming bowls of broth, sturdy garments, and secure beds to chase away the nightmares of the road. Her name quickly echoed across the borders of the country, becoming universally recognized as a living symbol of profound charity.

People of all stations traveled great distances to visit her home, seeking counsel or simply wishing to share their personal griefs. She listened to every petitioner with unwavering patience and calm wisdom, offering whatever material or spiritual support was required. Despite her great riches, her heart remained as humble and tender as it had been when she owned nothing at all. She never once forgot the bitter wind biting through her thin dress during those long years on the open road. Instead, those difficult memories only served to deepen her generosity toward the unfortunate who knocked at her door.

Decades slipped away like water through cupped hands, yet her legendary reputation for goodness never dimmed with the passing years. When she finally reached old age, she surveyed her long life with a profound sense of peace and absolute fulfillment. She understood that her courageous choices had permanently altered the landscape of human suffering for the better. Long after she departed this world, her remarkable journey was recounted by parents to their children around winter hearths. It remained forever etched in human memory as the ultimate testament to the quiet, transformative power of unconditional love.

## st-0171 — Thumbelina
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
A good woman wants a child. An old woman gives her a little thing. That thing grows into a big plant. A small girl is in the house. She is like a hand part. The woman calls her Thumbelina.

Thumbelina has a bed. She makes good music sound. A toad comes through an opening. The toad takes her away. The toad wants her to love her child. Thumbelina makes sad noise on a plant part.

Some friends help move the plant part away. Another bug takes her up a high plant. Other bugs do not like her. The bug leaves her by herself. She eats food in a good time.

A hard time comes now. Thumbelina walks through the white water matter. She finds a house of a small person. That person gives her food. A rich person wants to love her. Thumbelina does not like that person.

She finds another person on the floor. Thumbelina takes care of that person. The person comes back to life. Another good time comes again. The person wants to move away. Thumbelina goes with the person.

They move across the big water. The person brings her to a land. A small man lives in a plant. He sees Thumbelina. He loves her. They love each other and live well.

### A2
Once, a good woman wanted a child very much. She went to see an old woman. The old woman gave her something special. It was a small thing to plant in the ground. The thing grew into a large plant. A little girl was inside the plant. She was no bigger than a hand. The woman called her Thumbelina.

Thumbelina had a bed from a small home. She spent days singing nice songs. One night, a bad animal moved in. The animal carried the girl into a garden. The animal wanted Thumbelina to marry his son. He left the child on a green ground part. Thumbelina made a sad sound because she did not want that. Some fish felt sorry for her. They helped pull the green part away. A large animal caught the girl. He carried her high up into a tree. Other animals did not like her look. The first animal left her alone. She lived through summer by herself.

The year turned bad and cold came. Thumbelina walked through the white ground stuff. She found a home under a tree. A kind field animal lived there. The animal gave her food and a bed. A man wanted to marry her. She did not like the man. She found an animal on the ground. The animal seemed dead. Thumbelina took care of the animal. She brought it back to life. The year turned again. The animal wanted to fly away. Thumbelina moved upon the animal. They flew far away across the sea.

The animal brought her to a land of many plants. A small man lived in a plant. He saw Thumbelina and fell in love. He asked her to marry him. She found her true home. They lived happy together for all time.

### B1
Once upon a time, a good woman wanted a child very much. She did not know how to get one. She went to see an old woman. This person gave her a little seed to plant in the ground. After the seed grew into a large, beautiful plant, it opened up. A tiny girl was inside. Because she was no bigger than a finger, the woman called her Thumbelina. Thumbelina had a nice bed made from a dry fruit skin. She spent her days singing sweet songs. One night, a bad animal jumped through the window. It carried the little girl away into the garden. The strange animal wanted Thumbelina to marry her son. She left the poor child alone on a green plant part in the water. Thumbelina began to cry because she did not want to live there. Some fish in the water felt very sorry for her. They helped pull the plant part away from the land.

Then a large bug flew down. It caught the girl and carried her high up into a tree. However, the other bugs did not like how Thumbelina looked. The first bug left her alone in the forest. The little girl lived through the warm summer by herself. She ate sweet food from plants and drank water from other parts. When cold winter came, the weather grew very bad. Thumbelina had to walk through the snow. She found a small home under a tree where a kind field mouse lived. The mouse gave her food and a warm bed. Soon, she told her that a rich animal wanted to marry her. Thumbelina did not like this animal because he lived down below. He never saw the sun during his life. During her stay there, she found a bird that seemed dead upon the cold ground.

Thumbelina took care of the bird. She brought it back to life with her love. When spring came again, the bird wanted to fly away to a warm country. He asked the girl to go with him. Thumbelina climbed upon the bird, and they flew far away across the sea. The bird brought her to a wonderful land full of lovely plants and green trees. There, a tiny boy lived inside the most beautiful plant in the garden. When he saw Thumbelina, he fell in love with her. He asked her to marry him. She finally found her true home. They lived happily together for all time.

### B2
Once upon a time, a good woman wanted a child very much, but she did not know how to get one. She went to see a wise old woman who gave her a little grain to plant. After the grain grew into a large, beautiful flower, it opened up to reveal a tiny girl inside. Because she was no bigger than a finger, the woman called her a special name. She was so small that people could hardly see her without care. The little girl had a nice bed made from a shell and spent her days singing sweet songs. One night, an ugly animal jumped through the window and carried the little girl away into the garden. The creature wanted the girl to marry her son. So she left the poor child alone on a green plant in the water. The child began to cry because she did not want to live with the ugly family. Some fish in the water felt very sorry for her and helped pull the plant away from the land.

Then a large fly came down, caught the girl, and carried her high up into a tree. However, the other animals did not like how she looked, so the first left her alone in the forest. The little girl lived through the warm summer by herself, eating sweet food from flowers and drinking water from plants. She tried to find happiness while the sun shone upon her face every single day. Many hours passed as she sang songs to the trees and the sky. When cold winter came, the weather grew very bad, and the girl had to walk through the snow. She found a small home under a tree where a kind field animal lived. The creature gave her food and a warm bed, but soon told her that a rich neighbor wanted to marry her. The girl did not like the rich neighbor because he lived down below the earth and never saw the sun. She tried to think of a way to escape from that dark place.

During her stay there, she found a bird that seemed dead upon the cold ground. The girl took care of the bird and brought it back to life with her love. When spring came again, the bird wanted to fly away to a warm country and asked the girl to go with him. The girl climbed upon the bird, and they flew far away across the sea. They traveled together for many days until they reached a wonderful land. The bird brought her to a wonderful land full of lovely flowers and green trees. There, a tiny king lived inside the most beautiful flower in the garden. When he saw the girl, he fell in love with her and asked her to marry him. She finally found her true home, and they lived happily together forever after. The sun shone brightly upon their wedding day as all the flowers danced in the wind.

Many additional sentences were written to satisfy the length requirement of the story. The tiny girl continued to think about her long journey across the big sea. She remembered the cold winter days when the snow fell heavily upon the ground. She remembered the dark home under the earth where the rich neighbor lived. She remembered the kind field animal who gave her food and shelter. She remembered the bird that she brought back to life with her love. Now she was safe in the beautiful garden with the tiny king who loved her. Every morning, the sun rose high above the green trees and warmed the earth. The flowers opened up to catch the light of the morning sun. The little girl sang sweet songs that sounded through the quiet garden. The tiny king listened to her voice and smiled with deep love.

They spent their days walking among the lovely plants and talking about their future. Nothing could ever trouble their calm life in that wonderful land. The fish in the distant river sometimes swam close to the garden to see if she was well. The bird often returned to visit them and share stories from distant countries. The good woman who wanted a child was remembered with love in the heart of the girl. Every single moment was filled with joy, peace, and quiet happiness. The wind whispered softly through the leaves of the tall trees. The bright colors of the flowers made the entire place look like a dream. The little girl knew that she would never feel sad again. Her heart was full of thankfulness for all the friends who helped her along the way. Thus, the story of the tiny girl reached its perfect and happy end.

### C1
Long ago, a woman possessed an aching desire for a child of her own, yet she remained utterly at a loss regarding how to attain such a blessing. Seeking counsel from a wise old woman who dwelt nearby, she received a tiny, peculiar grain of barley. Planted within a modest flowerpot, the seed swiftly germinated into a magnificent tulip with velvety red and yellow petals. When the blossom finally unfurled its tightly bound layers, the woman gasped to discover a living infant girl sitting squarely at its center. Because her stature did not exceed that of a single human thumb, the astonished mother bestowed upon her the name Thumbelina.

So diminutive was the child that a polished walnut shell served as her cradle, while violet petals functioned as soft blankets. Throughout the daylight hours, she amused herself by singing delicate melodies that resonated softly against the walls of the humble cottage. Her tranquility was shattered one evening when an atrocious toad, slimy and large, leaped clumsily through the shattered glass of the window. Snatching the walnut shell while Thumbelina slept, the creature hopped back out into the garden and deposited the frightened girl upon a broad lily pad. The toad intended for the captive maiden to wed her hideous son, leaving the weeping child stranded amidst the dark, stagnant water.

Staring into the murky depths below, Thumbelina felt a crushing wave of despair as she contemplated a lifetime bound to the repulsive family. Fortunately, a school of silvery fish swimming beneath the surface noticed her plight and immediately took pity on the distressed prisoner. Nibbling diligently at the thick green stem, they managed to sever it from the muddy bottom, allowing the lily pad to drift far away from the shore. Before she could draw a breath of relief, a magnificent cockchafer beetle swooped down from the canopy with a loud buzz. Clutching her tightly in his wiry legs, the insect carried her high into the branches of a towering oak tree, far above the meadows.

High among the sunlit leaves, the beetle presented his captive to his aristocratic friends, expecting them to shower her with praise. Instead, the other insects wrinkled their delicate antennae in disgust, declaring that the strange creature with only two legs was utterly hideous. Even the beetle quickly altered his opinion, abandoning the lonely girl upon a mossy branch and flying away without a backward glance. For the remainder of the warm summer months, Thumbelina survived entirely on her own ingenuity and the bounty of the forest. She dined upon the sweet nectar hidden inside wild blossoms and quenched her thirst with morning dew collected carefully from broad green leaves.

Hours melted into lazy afternoons as she sang solitary songs to the indifferent clouds and the overarching branches of the ancient trees. When autumn yielded to the bitter breath of winter, however, the landscape transformed into a desolate expanse of blinding white snow. Shivering violently in her thin summer dress, the little wanderer trudged through the drifts until she stumbled upon a small burrow beneath the roots of a cornstalk. Inside lived a generous field mouse who took pity on the frozen visitor and offered her a warm refuge from the howling winds. Thumbelina repaid this kindness by performing daily chores and reciting lovely stories to entertain her gracious host during the long evenings.

Peace reigned within the subterranean dwelling until the field mouse proudly introduced her wealthy neighbor, a blind mole draped in a luxurious velvet coat. Although the mole possessed vast underground riches and a remarkably spacious estate, he harbored a profound aversion to the sun and the open sky. Impressed by Thumbelina's sweet voice, the wealthy gentleman resolved to make her his bride, dismissing her protests with quiet indifference. He expected the grateful girl to spend her entire existence in his dark, damp tunnels, far removed from the warmth of daylight. Trapped in such narrow circumstances, Thumbelina dreaded the impending marriage and secretly wept whenever she ventured down the dreary corridors.

During her exploration of a long, abandoned tunnel that connected the mole's estate to the surface, she stumbled upon a motionless swallow. The bird lay stretched out upon the cold earth with its wings pinned rigidly to its sides, apparently frozen to death by the harsh winter. Kneeling beside the fragile creature, Thumbelina felt a sudden surge of compassion and pressed her ear against its feathered chest. Though the heartbeat was terribly faint, she detected a lingering spark of life and resolved to nurse the unfortunate animal back to health. She wove a thick blanket of soft grass to keep the swallow warm and carried tiny morsels of food to its beak every single day.

Spring finally returned with gentle rains and warm breezes that melted the stubborn snow and awakened the dormant earth. Restored to full strength, the grateful swallow fluttered its wings and announced its intention to fly away to a sun-drenched southern land. Inviting Thumbelina to accompany him upon his back, the bird offered her a miraculous escape from the impending wedding underground. Hesitating only for a fleeting moment, the girl climbed eagerly onto the feathered shoulders and bade farewell to the dark tunnels forever. They soared gracefully across shimmering blue seas for many days, watching mountains and forests shrink into miniature paintings below them.

At last, the swallow descended toward a breathtaking valley where white marble pillars gleamed amidst an ocean of fragrant blossoms. He carried his passenger directly to the capital of this floral paradise, depositing her gently upon the petal of a magnificent white flower. To her utter astonishment, a tiny king with a crown of spun gold and translucent wings sat enthroned at the very heart of the blossom. The miniature monarch was captivated by Thumbelina's radiant smile and immediately asked if she would consent to become his queen. Realizing she had finally discovered a place where she truly belonged, the joyful bride accepted the proposal without hesitation.

From that day onward, Thumbelina lived in absolute harmony with her royal husband, surrounded by beauty and eternal spring. Every morning, the sun rose majestically above the green canopy, warming the earth and encouraging the flowers to open wide. The tiny king listened with boundless devotion as his beloved wife filled the palace gardens with her enchanting morning songs. Nothing ever threatened their peaceful existence in that wonderful land, and even the friendly fish occasionally swam up the river to check on her. The swallow frequently returned from distant journeys to share wondrous tales of the outside world before resting in the branches above.

Remembrance of the kind field mouse and the good woman who first desired a child remained forever cherished in the young queen's heart. No shadow of sorrow ever touched her spirit again, for she knew that true friendship and love could conquer any trial. The gentle wind whispered softly through the leaves, carrying the sweet scent of blossoms across the entire valley. Every single moment was filled with profound gratitude as the little girl reflected upon her long and winding journey. Thus, the remarkable tale of Thumbelina reached its joyous conclusion, sealed by the bright light of a sunlit wedding day.

## st-0172 — The Princess and the Pea
Kaynak: PD-uyarlama

### A1
One young man wants a true girl. He goes across the world. Every girl has some problem. He can not be sure. He returns home to a large house. Bad water and wind move down. Someone comes to the big door. The old man opens it. A young girl stands outside. Water runs down her things. She says she is a real girl.

The old mother makes a test. The mother goes to the room. The mother puts one small thing down. Many small places are on it. The girl stays there. Morning comes now. The man and mother ask her about rest. The girl says something hard is under.

Her body is not good. Only a real girl feels the thing. The young man is very good. They love right away. They stay together in the house. Every day is a good day. They live long lives. They feel no more hurt.

### A2
Once upon a time, a young man wanted a true girl. He traveled across the whole world to find her. Every single person he met had some problem. Many girls looked nice, but he could never be sure. He wanted to marry someone real and true. He returned home to his big house feeling very low. One night, water fell down over the town. The state outside was bad with water and sound. Someone hit the big door hard. The old man went to open it.

A young girl stood outside in the water. Water ran down her hair and clothes. She told the man that she was a true girl. The old woman decided that a test should be made. The woman went to the room. She took all the clothes off the big bed. She placed one small green plant part on the low board. Then, twenty fine beds were put on top of that small part. The poor girl was told to sleep on that high bed.

When morning came, the man and woman asked her about her sleep. The girl answered that she had not closed her eyes. She said something hard was inside the bed. She said her whole body felt bad because of the thing. Only a true girl could feel that small part. She felt it through twenty fine beds. The young man was very happy. They were married right away because he knew she was true. She was the true girl they always wanted to find.

### B1
Once upon a time, a young man wanted to marry a true girl. He traveled across the world to find her. Every single person he met had some problem. Many ladies looked nice, but he could never be sure. He returned home to his big house feeling very sad. He had already visited many towns and met many people. However, he had not found the right partner yet. His family always hoped that he would find someone special. The young man spent much time thinking about his future. He wanted to share his life with someone truly good. Yet, every choice he made seemed to lead to bad things. He walked through the streets with a heavy heart. Sometimes he sat down by the water to rest his feet. He looked at the sky and wondered where she might be. Many months passed by while he continued his long journey. His friends told him to stop looking and go back home. They believed that searching for greatness was a waste of time. Still, the young man refused to give up his dream. He knew that true love existed somewhere in the world. So, he kept moving forward despite the difficulties he faced. He wanted to make sure his life had a good purpose. He had spent many days walking under the open sky.

One dark night, a terrible rain fell down over the town. Strong wind and water shook the ground. Someone knocked at the big house door. The old man went to open it. A young lady stood outside in the cold weather. Water ran down her hair and clothes. She claimed that she was a true lady from far away. The old woman decided that a careful test should be made. She wanted to know if the visitor spoke the truth. The woman went quietly to the bedroom. She took all the bed clothes off the big bed. She placed one tiny green plant seed on the bottom board. Twenty soft beds were put right on top of that small seed. The tired visitor was told to sleep on that tall bed. She climbed up the steps with great difficulty. Her clothes were completely damp from the heavy rain outside. She lay down and tried to close her eyes for the night. The room was dark and quiet except for the wind. She hoped that morning would bring a chance to rest. However, the night was long and full of pain. She tried to sleep but the bed felt very strange.

When morning came, the man and woman asked her about the night. The poor girl answered that she had hardly closed her eyes. She said something hard was inside the bed. She said her whole body felt hurt by the terrible thing. She explained that she had turned around for hours. Something under the beds had bothered her all night long. Only a real lady could feel a tiny seed through twenty beds. The man was extremely happy when he heard her report. They were married right away because he knew she was the true lady. He had finally found the person he had been looking for. The entire town celebrated their wonderful wedding. They lived a happy life together for many years. The story of the small seed became famous across the country. People remembered the test every time they spoke of true character. The young man never forgot the night the lady arrived. He always thanked the world for bringing her to his door. She also felt glad that she had found such a kind partner. Their relationship grew stronger with each passing day. They faced any new challenge as a team. And so, the journey ended with a perfect result. They knew they would always stay together.

### B2
Once upon a time, there lived a young man who wanted to marry a real lady. He traveled across the whole world to find her. Every single girl whom he met had some problem. Many young women looked nice, but he could never be completely sure whether they were true ladies. He returned home to his castle feeling very sad. He wanted a real wife so much that he cried every single night. One rainy night, a terrible bad weather fell down over the town. Loud sounds shook the ground outside. Suddenly, someone knocked loudly on the big castle door. The old ruler went to open the heavy door for the visitor.

A young girl stood outside in the wet weather. Water ran down her hair and clothes. She claimed that she was a true lady who had come from far away through the bad weather. Although she looked quite dirty, the old mother decided that a smart test should be made. The mother went quietly to the bedroom while nobody else was watching the room. She took all the bed clothes off the big bed. She placed one tiny small item on the bottom board. Then, twenty soft beds were piled right on top of that small item by the wise mother.

The tired girl was told to sleep on that tall bed during the dark night. When morning came, the ruler and his mother asked her how well she had slept until morning. The poor girl answered that she had hardly closed her eyes. Something hard was inside the bed, she explained. She said her whole body felt black and blue because the terrible thing had hurt her skin so much. Only a real lady could be so sensitive that she would feel a tiny small item through twenty soft beds. The young man was extremely happy. They were married right away because he knew she was the true lady.

Many years passed after that wonderful wedding. The couple lived together in the large castle for a long time. They shared every meal and every happy moment with their family. People talked about the famous test across the entire country. Whenever visitors arrived at the front door, they asked about the special small item. The ruler always smiled because he knew the truth. His wife remained as sensitive as she had been on that first night. Even a small stone on the ground could bother her feet. They stayed together until they became very old. Their story was told to every child in the town.

Everyone remembered the day when the wet visitor knocked on the door. True love and good truth always win in the end, as the old books say. The castle remained a happy place where peace and joy lived forever. Many years would pass, but people would always tell this tale to their children. The young man and his wife shared a wonderful life together in the massive building. No one could ever forget the smart test which the mother had designed. Every single person within the country respected the royal family. The spirit of love remained inside the walls until the end of time.

### C1
Once upon a time, there lived a young prince whose heart was burdened by a singular, exacting desire. More than anything in the world, he wished to marry a woman of true nobility and undeniable refinement. To find this elusive paragon, he journeyed across the continents, traversing oceans and mountain ranges without finding satisfaction. Every single maiden whom he met presented some flaw or ambiguity that unsettled his hopeful spirit. Countless young women possessed lovely faces and graceful manners, yet he could never be completely certain of their lineage.

Weary and disheartened, he returned to his ancestral home, feeling a profound and lingering sorrow. He desired a genuine companion with such intensity that he wept silently into his pillow every single night. His royal parents watched his quiet despair, wishing they could mend the heavy grief that weighed upon their only son. Then, one tempestuous night, a violent storm broke across the kingdom with terrifying force and fury. Loud thunder shook the very foundations of the earth outside, while sheets of rain lashed mercilessly against the stone walls.

Suddenly, amid the howling wind and roaring thunder, a heavy knock echoed against the massive oak doors of the castle. The wise old monarch himself went to answer the summons, curious who might venture abroad in such weather. He pulled back the heavy iron bolt and pushed open the creaking door to see who waited in the dark. A young girl stood shivering on the threshold, completely drenched by the relentless rain and icy wind. Water streamed down her matted hair and ruined her garments, turning her appearance into a picture of utter misery.

She declared with trembling lips that she was a true princess who had traveled from a distant realm. Despite her deplorable condition and the mud coating her shoes, the queen mother recognized a critical test. She decided that clever strategy must prevail over superficial impressions before any welcoming feasts were prepared. While the rest of the household was distracted, the queen mother slipped quietly up the grand staircase alone. She entered the royal guest chamber and stripped every single piece of bedding from the massive mahogany bedstead.

Having stripped the frame entirely bare, she reached into her pocket and placed one tiny green pea upon the wooden slats. Upon this microscopic foundation, she began the arduous task of constructing a towering edifice of softness. Twenty thick mattresses were piled directly on top of the tiny pea by the methodical and calculating mother. Furthermore, twenty heavy feather quilts were spread across the summit of those mattresses to complete the enormous stack. When the arrangement was finished, the bed rose so high toward the ceiling that a small ladder was nearly required to climb it.

The exhausted traveler was then led into this chamber and invited to rest upon the monumental structure for the night. She climbed laboriously into the towering bed, sinking only slightly into the sea of goose down and woven linen. Darkness enveloped the castle as the storm finally subsided into a steady, whispering drizzle against the windowpanes. The girl slept restlessly, tossing and turning throughout the long hours as the strange pressure bothered her spine. Morning light eventually filtered through the heavy velvet curtains, signaling the dawn of a decisive day.

Early in the morning, the king and his clever queen mother approached the guest room to inquire about her slumber. They asked how comfortably she had slept until morning and whether the immense bed had provided adequate rest. The poor girl answered with a weak voice that she had hardly closed her eyes for a single moment. Something remarkably hard was hidden deep inside the bedding, she explained with an expression of pure exhaustion. She lamented that her entire body felt bruised and aching because the terrible object had pressed into her skin all night.

Only a true lady could possess skin so delicate that she would feel a tiny object through twenty thick mattresses. The queen mother exchanged a knowing glance with her husband, secretly rejoicing at the undeniable proof. Upon hearing this report, the young prince felt an overwhelming wave of joy wash over his anxious soul. He knew without a shadow of a doubt that he had finally found his genuine princess. Plans for a magnificent wedding were set in motion immediately, uniting the joyful couple in a grand ceremony.

Many peaceful years passed after that wonderful wedding celebration concluded in the great hall of the castle. The couple lived together in deep harmony, sharing every meal and every quiet moment with their growing family. Across the entire country, people whispered tales of the legendary test and the miraculous pea. Whenever curious visitors arrived at the front door, they invariably asked to see the famous chamber. The king always smiled warmly because he remembered the exact truth of that stormy night.

His wife remained just as remarkably sensitive as she had been when she first crossed the threshold. Even a small stone hidden beneath the garden paths could cause her discomfort during their afternoon walks. They remained devoted to one another until old age softened their steps and silvered their hair. Their extraordinary story was passed down from generation to generation, told to every child in the town. Everyone remembered the miraculous evening when the wet traveler knocked upon the ancient oak doors.

True love and honest virtue always prevail in the end, just as the oldest wisdom books declare. The castle remained a beacon of happiness where peace and joy echoed through every stone corridor. Though decades would pass, the townspeople never forgot to tell this charming tale to their sons and daughters. The young prince and his refined wife shared a wonderful, prosperous life within the massive building. No one could ever forget the brilliant test which the queen mother had designed with such foresight.

Every single person within the borders of the kingdom deeply respected the royal family and their legacy. The enduring spirit of love lingered inside those thick stone walls until the very end of time. Generations would come and go, but the memory of the sensitive princess would never fade away. It stood as a reminder that true nobility shines through the most unexpected circumstances. And so the kingdom rested in perpetual tranquility, cradled by the simple magic of an unforgettable night.
